# Issue triage ledger

Durable record of GitHub issues already checked against this repository. Daily sweeps must skip every issue number listed here.

Analysis baseline: 2026-08-24, working tree at `ab05c55580ec45e5a17451ec7d9b579310c3ab0d` plus pre-existing uncommitted changes. No issue or source code was modified during triage.

## 2026-08-24 — #913 Migration banner renders the detected legacy-install path unescaped via dangerouslySetInnerHTML

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/913
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: `src/renderer/src/components/settings/DataPane.tsx:82-86` sends the translated banner, including `openclawPath`, to `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. Global i18next configuration disables escaping at `src/shared/i18n/index.ts:602`. The path comes from `checkOpenClawExists` (`src/main/installer.ts:668-680`) through the preload API, so it is locally derived rather than ordinary remote input, but markup is demonstrably preserved. A local i18next reproduction produced the injected `<img>` verbatim; per-call `interpolation.escapeValue: true` escaped it.
- Inference: Practical exploitability is low because the returned path is a fixed OpenClaw directory under the OS home path; a symlink does not change the returned string. The HTML injection sink is nevertheless real and needlessly violates the renderer trust boundary.
- Recommended action: Keep the static `<code>` markup and enable i18next escaping for this call only: pass `interpolation: { escapeValue: true }` with the `path` option. Add one focused renderer/i18n test using a path containing quotes, angle brackets, and an `onerror` payload. No sanitizer dependency is needed.

## 2026-08-24 — #899 Desktop app auto-approves dangerous commands in local chat

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/899
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The legacy local TUI path handles `approval.request` by immediately calling `approval.respond` with `choice: "once"` at `src/main/hermes.ts:2170-2182`. The main-process runs path instead stops and falls back when approval is requested (`src/main/hermes.ts:1892-1898`) even though run capability detection requires `/v1/runs/{run_id}/approval` (`src/main/run-stream.ts:28-38`). The default renderer dashboard handler has explicit cases for session/background/completion/clarify but none for approval (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:989-1128`); `applyDashboardStreamEvent` also has no approval case (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:641-722`). Its existing Approve/Deny bar is only a post-response text heuristic (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageRow.tsx:13-14,231-235,331-344`).
- Inference: The exact symptom varies by transport: legacy TUI silently approves once, dashboard chat hides the request and can remain blocked, and runs abandon the structured flow. All violate manual-approval semantics; the legacy behavior is the security-impacting path.
- Recommended action: Reuse the existing inline `ClarifyCard` interaction pattern for a dedicated structured approval card. On `approval.request`, render the server-provided choices and keep the turn paused; send the selected choice through `approval.respond` (dashboard/TUI) or `/v1/runs/{run_id}/approval` (runs). Delete the automatic `once` response and stop using response-text matching as the approval authority. Regression check: inject an approval event, assert no response occurs before user input, then verify once/session/always/deny map exactly once and denial never executes the tool.

## 2026-08-24 — #715 Named custom-provider sessions fail on Desktop resume

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/715
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report targets Desktop 0.6.1; this tree is 0.7.4 (`package.json:3`). The issue's diagnosed identity-loss path is fixed upstream by NousResearch/hermes-agent commits `643dc8279306751048e573c53f3f5441d9f773f8` and `0fa7d6f6609c515b6eaafda0594a1472d11d93b5`, which persist/heal `custom:<name>` instead of bare `custom`. Desktop installs the upstream `main` installer (`src/main/installer.ts:942`) and exposes `hermes update` (`src/main/installer.ts:746-768`). Current renderer code also resolves a bare custom selection back to a live named provider by endpoint/model (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:505-559`). The focused suite `npm test -- tests/dashboard-chat-transport.test.ts` passed 47/47, including custom-slug and base-URL selection cases at `tests/dashboard-chat-transport.test.ts:234,686-731`.
- Inference: Source and regression evidence strongly indicate the original failure is fixed, but this sweep did not have the reporter's exact config/runtime for an end-to-end resume test. Existing installations must update Hermes Agent to receive the upstream fix.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to run `hermes update`, restart Desktop, and retry one new plus one resumed session on 0.7.4 or newer. If it no longer reproduces, close as fixed upstream.
- Suggested close note: "The named-custom-provider identity-loss path is fixed upstream in Hermes Agent (`643dc8279`, hardened by `0fa7d6f66`), and current Hermes Desktop resolves named custom providers by live endpoint/model. Please run `hermes update` and retest on Desktop 0.7.4+. Closing as fixed; we can reopen if a fresh and resumed session still degrade the provider to bare `custom`."

## 2026-08-29 — #920 Remote SSH backend can truncate the remote `~/.hermes/.env`, destroying all API credentials

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/920
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: At current checkout `65fe87f7475958d5219f8606ac058e66e10636b0`, `sshEnsureDashboardToken` builds a temporary copy, then replaces the credential file with `cat $tmp > ${envPath}` (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2540-2569`). `sshPersistDashboardPort` independently uses the same truncating sequence (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2617-2635`). The in-flight map at `src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2538-2578` serializes token calls only; it does not serialize token writes against port writes. Both are reached by `ensureDashboardInner` (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2946-2957`). The existing `.env` tests cover only the pure single-key rewrite (`src/main/ssh-remote.test.ts:97-143`), not concurrent persistence. The issue has no comments, and the reported source exactly matches the checkout.
- Inference: During `cat > .env`, the target exists at length zero before the copy completes. A concurrent reader can therefore build its temporary file from an empty snapshot and later replace the credential file with only its own dashboard variable. Atomic rename alone removes torn reads but still permits lost updates between two read-modify-write operations, so serialization is also required.
- Recommended action: In `src/main/ssh-remote.ts`, replace the token, port, and existing API-server `.env` read/write sequences with one shared remote-side locked, atomic multi-key upsert: lock a sibling lock file, read once, write a same-directory temporary file, preserve mode, `fsync`, then `os.replace`. Reuse the existing `upsertEnvLine` semantics and remove the read-error-as-empty behavior. Focused regression check: force token and port updates to overlap against a temp `.env` containing sentinel provider keys, then assert every sentinel remains and each dashboard key appears exactly once.

## 2026-08-29 — #925 False "dashboard did not switch" error after profile switch: named provider (omlx) vs normalized live provider (custom)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/925
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: `dashboardModelMatches` first requires equal model names, then accepts only an exact provider match or the one-way alias `requested=custom` / `live=custom:<slug>` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:563-583`). It therefore returns false for the issue's `requested=omlx`, `live=custom`, same-model state even when `live.providers` contains an `omlx` row. `switchAndValidate` calls that function after the successful `/model` command and throws the reported blocking error on false (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1417-1449`). `ModelOptionsResponse` already carries provider inventory (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:27-41`), and provider resolution already searches every named row by endpoint (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:506-552`), so no new API is needed. The current tests cover exact and `custom -> custom:<slug>` matches but not the reverse normalized case (`tests/dashboard-chat-transport.test.ts:224-253`). The focused dashboard/MCP suites passed 55/55.
- Inference: Hermes Agent's live provider normalization is the only runtime premise not reproduced locally, but the issue's captured `/model` success plus the deterministic current predicate is sufficient to establish the desktop-side false negative.
- Recommended action: Extend `dashboardModelMatches` only for `liveProvider === "custom"`: accept when `live.providers` has a row whose normalized slug is the requested provider or `custom:<requested-provider>` and whose models include the already-equal requested model. Keep all existing exact-model and inventory checks so a genuinely wrong endpoint/provider is not masked. Add one focused table test accepting `omlx -> custom` with a matching row/model and rejecting absent row, absent model, and different model.

## 2026-08-29 — #914 Failed MCP stdio spawn surfaces as repeated Windows "Open with" dialogs for the server's `.js` entry file

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/914
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The desktop MCP path only validates and persists configuration or proxies REST calls (`src/main/mcp-servers.ts:667-879`), wired by IPC at `src/main/ipc/register.ts:3294-3327`; its local Test action invokes `hermes mcp test` through the Hermes Python CLI (`src/main/mcp-servers.ts:83-114,851-879`). There is no Electron `ShellExecute`, `openPath`, or MCP-server spawn in that path. The installed Hermes Agent source is the actual stdio owner: `_resolve_stdio_command` resolves bare `npx` with `shutil.which` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tools/mcp_tool.py:647-691`), `_run_stdio` passes the resolved command to the Python MCP SDK (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tools/mcp_tool.py:2351-2449`), and connection failures retry with backoff before parking (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tools/mcp_tool.py:3209-3305`; limits at lines 330-339). The issue's close/reopen correlation, `OpenWith.exe` observation, working explicit `node.exe <server.js>` bypass, and absence of the MCP child are consistent with that Windows launcher boundary. The issue has no comments.
- Inference: The symptom is likely real, but the exact failing argv and exception are not proven because this host cannot reproduce Windows and the issue provides no `~/.hermes/logs/mcp-stderr.log` excerpt. The report's attribution to a desktop ShellExecute fallback is contradicted by source; the likely fault is the Hermes Agent/Python MCP SDK's Windows handling of an npm `.cmd` shim or the package's generated `.js` bin shim, followed by the agent retry policy.
- Recommended action: Route the fix upstream to Hermes Agent, not the Electron MCP editor. First capture the resolved command/argv and first exception on Windows. Then make `_resolve_stdio_command` produce an explicit executable launch for npm shims (prefer `node.exe` plus the resolved JS CLI over document association; do not add a generic ShellExecute fallback), and classify this deterministic launch failure as permanent so it parks immediately. Keep the explicit `node.exe <absolute server.js>` config as the safe workaround. Focused regression check: on Windows with `.js` unassociated, connect through a fixture `npx.cmd` package whose bin targets JS and assert one MCP handshake succeeds without spawning `OpenWith.exe`; a broken fixture must surface one actionable error and park.

## 2026-08-30 — #919 SSH Tunnel + Legacy transport: delete-session/delete-sessions throw "requires dashboard transport" — no SSH-native fallback

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/919
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: At current checkout `65fe87f7475958d5219f8606ac058e66e10636b0`, `withSshDashboardSessions` throws exactly `This SSH session operation requires dashboard transport.` when legacy mode has no fallback, and auto mode only falls back when one is supplied (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:586-604`). SSH list and message reads supply `sshListSessions` / `sshGetSessionMessages` fallbacks (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2244-2294`), but both delete handlers explicitly pass `undefined` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2362-2394`). The delete-session handler was introduced this way in commit `05d1f26ed`; the issue has no comments. The installed agent already exposes the safe primitive `hermes sessions delete <id> --yes` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:15266-15272,15922-15937`), which delegates to `SessionDB.delete_session` and cleans delegate children, messages, and transcript files (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_state.py:8603-8642`). By contrast, a raw SQLite `DELETE` would omit some of those semantics.
- Inference: The reported legacy failure is deterministic, and auto mode fails identically whenever dashboard setup/probing fails because there is no fallback to invoke. Implementing the issue's suggested raw-SQL deletion literally would risk behavioral drift from the agent's canonical deletion contract.
- Recommended action: In `src/main/ssh-remote.ts`, add `sshDeleteSession` using the existing `buildRemoteHermesCmd` and shell quoting to run `hermes [--profile <name>] sessions delete <id> --yes`; implement bulk deletion as the same per-ID loop already used by `remoteDeleteSessions`, returning the real deleted count. Pass those functions as the two missing fallbacks in `src/main/ipc/register.ts`. Focused regression check: stub SSH execution, invoke both handlers in `legacy` and failing-dashboard `auto` modes, and assert safely quoted IDs/profile reach the CLI exactly once per unique ID and the returned count excludes not-found IDs.

## 2026-08-30 — #918 Context gauge: /deepseek/i fallback still 131072, and SSH mode ignores server's model.context_length (1M only shows as 131.1k)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/918
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The static lookup still maps every DeepSeek identifier to 131,072 (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/contextWindows.ts:28-32`), and its current test explicitly expects `deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4-pro` to resolve to 131,072 (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/contextWindows.test.ts:15-20`). Current official DeepSeek documentation reports a 1M context for the V4 Flash, V4 Pro, and V4 Flash Vision models. Separately, `sshGetModelConfig` reads only `model.provider`, `model.default`, and `model.base_url` (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:1325-1340`), while the context-window IPC handler never branches on connection mode and calls the local config/provider resolver (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1952-1971`; `src/main/model-discovery.ts:624-685`). The renderer then falls back from gateway usage to that result and finally the static heuristic (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:435-463,945-955`). Local override precedence is tested, but there is no SSH override test (`tests/model-discovery.test.ts:546-604`). The issue has no comments.
- Inference: A gateway event containing `context_max` still wins, but when it is absent the SSH server's explicit `model.context_length` is invisible and the V4 heuristic deterministically produces 131.1k. Broadly changing all `/deepseek/i` matches to 1M would mislabel older/self-hosted V3-family models; the model-specific rule must precede the generic fallback.
- Recommended action: In `src/main/ssh-remote.ts`, expose a positive-integer SSH context override only when the requested model exactly matches the remote `model.default`; branch `get-model-context-window` in `src/main/ipc/register.ts` to use it before local discovery. In `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/contextWindows.ts`, add a `deepseek-v4` 1M rule before the existing generic DeepSeek 131,072 rule. Update `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/contextWindows.test.ts` and add one SSH IPC/path regression: V4 resolves to 1M, V3 remains 131,072, and an exact remote `context_length: 1000000` wins without leaking to a different model.

## 2026-08-30 — #907 Security hardening: pin and verify installer and registry payloads

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/907
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The Unix installer executes a script directly from mutable Hermes Agent `main` through `curl | bash` (`src/main/installer.ts:936-946`); Windows downloads the same mutable branch's PowerShell script and runs it under `ExecutionPolicy Bypass` without a digest/signature check (`src/main/installer.ts:1038-1060,1091-1101`). The user-facing fallback commands repeat these paths (`src/main/installer.ts:1136-1146`; `src/renderer/src/constants.ts:1337-1348`). The fetched agent installer itself supports commit pinning (`--commit` / `-Commit`) but Desktop supplies neither. The registry likewise defines its index, tree, manifests, and payload URLs from mutable `main` (`src/main/registry.ts:24-41,332-367`), writes fetched files without verification (`src/main/registry.ts:375-391`), and can persist fetched MCP commands/arguments into executable configuration (`src/main/registry.ts:401-479`). Repository search found no installer/registry SHA-256 or signature verification. The issue has no comments, and history shows these mutable URLs remain from commits `083e6e42d`, `e2f544a6f`, and `4224efef4`.
- Inference: This is a supply-chain integrity gap, not evidence that either upstream is currently compromised. HTTPS protects transit but does not stop a compromised maintainer account or branch rewrite from changing code executed by an already-released Desktop binary. Pinning only the bootstrap script is incomplete unless that script is also told to install the same immutable agent commit.
- Recommended action: Add one reviewed Hermes Agent commit constant per Desktop release; download the Unix/Windows script from that commit to a temporary file, verify a build-time SHA-256, execute the file, and pass the same `--commit` / `-Commit` value. Replace the registry branch constant with a reviewed registry commit and use that SHA consistently for index, tree, manifests, and payloads; fail closed if a fetched object cannot be tied to that snapshot. Update the displayed manual commands too. A signed digest manifest can come later if trust must extend beyond release-reviewed pins. Focused regression check: mock fetches and assert every executable/content URL contains the approved commit, a hash mismatch executes/writes nothing, and no install/registry path references `refs/heads/main`.

## 2026-08-31 — #894 Not retrieving all available models from OpenRouter

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/894
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report and its follow-up specifically concern `$HERMES_HOME/provider_models_cache.json`. That file belongs to Hermes Agent, not Desktop: repository search finds no Desktop read/write path for it, while the installed agent defines it at `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/models.py:2810-2835` and rewrites a non-empty provider result at `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/models.py:2933-2972`. OpenRouter fetching first builds `preferred_ids` from a curated manifest/snapshot, then iterates only those IDs; any live `/api/v1/models` item absent from the curated list is unconditionally omitted even if it is free and tool-capable (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/models.py:1458-1530`). Its tests cover pricing and tool filtering only for IDs already present in the curated list (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/hermes_cli/test_models.py:60-175`). Current Desktop separately calls the provider's standard `/models` endpoint and parses every returned ID (`src/main/model-discovery.ts:406-491,514-585`), then unions live-discovered IDs into its picker (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:566-583`); the focused Desktop discovery suite passed 23/23.
- Inference: The incomplete cache is deterministic and explains why manual cache additions work until the agent refresh overwrites them. The correct inventory boundary is still “models Hermes can drive,” not every OpenRouter card: explicit non-tool models should remain excluded. The undocumented frontend `models/find` endpoint is unnecessary because the official `/api/v1/models` payload already supplies IDs, pricing, and capabilities.
- Recommended action: Fix Hermes Agent's `fetch_openrouter_models`, not Desktop: preserve curated models first, then append every remaining live model that advertises tool support (permissive when capability metadata is absent), carrying the existing free-price tag. Keep the disk-cache layer unchanged. Update `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/hermes_cli/test_models.py` with a non-curated free tool-capable fixture that must be returned and a non-tool fixture that must remain hidden; also verify the expanded result survives `cached_provider_model_ids`.

## 2026-08-31 — #863 Groq provider sends unsupported "think" parameter even when Reasoning is Off

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/863
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: Desktop lists Groq at `https://api.groq.com/openai/v1` (`src/renderer/src/constants.ts:378-395`) but deliberately persists every provider in that compatibility map as `provider=custom` plus its base URL (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:300-329`), matching the issue log. The installed agent's generic `CustomProfile` maps disabled reasoning to both top-level `reasoning_effort="none"` and `extra_body.think=false` for every custom endpoint (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/plugins/model-providers/custom/__init__.py:24-69`); its current unit tests explicitly require that wire shape (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/plugins/model_providers/test_custom_profile.py:40-71`). The chat transport passes the custom profile the endpoint's `base_url`, so the profile already has enough context to distinguish Groq from Ollama (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/agent/transports/chat_completions.py:582-595`). The issue has no comments.
- Inference: Groq's HTTP 400 is the direct consequence of the generic custom profile applying Ollama-specific disable controls to a strict hosted OpenAI-compatible API. The profile comment claiming unknown endpoints ignore these fields is contradicted by the captured response. Changing the Desktop toggle or retry policy would only mask the provider-contract error.
- Recommended action: In Hermes Agent's custom provider profile, gate `think=false` and the Ollama `reasoning_effort=none` workaround to Ollama-compatible/local endpoints (or an explicit capability), and emit neither field for Groq when reasoning is off. Preserve provider-specific reasoning controls only where declared supported. Focused regression check: build kwargs for `provider=custom`, Groq base URL, and `{enabled: false}` and assert neither `think` nor `reasoning_effort` is present; the equivalent Ollama case must retain its existing disable fields. No Desktop source change is required.

## 2026-08-31 — #822 First reply duplicates after window open — dual WS connect race

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/822
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report targets Desktop 0.7.2/0.7.3 with Hermes Agent 0.18.0; this checkout is Desktop 0.7.4 and the installed agent checkout is `55ef425d0c3967022cb54093112e638c5c3f9e01`. The zero-message socket followed by a second socket is not evidence of two live subscriptions: `probeDashboardWebSocket` intentionally upgrades and immediately destroys a readiness-probe socket (`src/main/dashboard.ts:301-331`), after which the renderer opens the persistent client. Current renderer connection setup is single-flight through `connectingRef`, retains only one connected client, and rejects a superseded generation (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1146-1255`); `DashboardGatewayClient.connect` closes any prior owned socket before constructing another (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardGatewayClient.ts:107-170`). Current Hermes Agent no longer repoints a dropped WS session to stdio and immediately tears it down as hypothesized: it detaches reconnectable sessions to a drop sentinel and gives `session.resume` a grace window to rebind before reaping (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:826-906`), invoked once by the disconnect path (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tui_gateway/ws.py:434-475`). The focused dashboard-client, dashboard-transport, and reconciliation suites passed 58/58, including duplicate streamed-bubble coverage at `tests/reconcile-streamed-with-db.test.ts:545-561`.
- Inference: The report's proposed dual-live-WS/hydration mechanism does not match the current Desktop or Agent lifecycle, and both sides now have guards against stale connections and duplicate reconciliation. This sweep did not reproduce on the reporter's Intel/Tahoe setup, so “appears fixed” is stronger than “not reproducible,” but an exact launch/first-turn retest is still appropriate before closing.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to update both Desktop and Hermes Agent, fully quit/reopen, and retry the first turn once. If it no longer duplicates, close; if it does, request one renderer event trace showing `message.delta`/`message.complete` counts and the current `ws closed` line (including reaped/detached counts), rather than treating the expected probe socket as the cause.
- Suggested close note: "Current Desktop uses a short-lived WebSocket readiness probe followed by one single-flight persistent client, so the zero-message connection in the log is expected rather than a second chat subscription. Current Hermes Agent also preserves reconnectable sessions through a disconnect grace window, and the duplicate-message regression suites pass. Please update Desktop and run `hermes update`, then retry the first turn after a full quit/reopen. Closing as apparently fixed; we can reopen with a current renderer event trace if two bubbles still appear."

## 2026-09-02 — #877 app launch fails in Ubuntu when installed under `/opt/Hermes One`

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/877
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: At checkout `65fe87f7475958d5219f8606ac058e66e10636b0`, the package product name remains `Hermes One` (`electron-builder.yml:1-2`) and there is no supported Linux install-directory override in the installed electron-builder 26.8.1 options. Its FPM target always maps the app to `/opt/${appInfo.sanitizedProductName}` (`node_modules/app-builder-lib/out/targets/FpmTarget.js:184-201`), while `sanitizedProductName` preserves the product-name space (`node_modules/app-builder-lib/out/appInfo.js:54-57`). The project's post-install hook also hard-codes `/opt/Hermes One/chrome-sandbox` (`build/linux-after-install.sh:18-26`). This exactly matches the failing `/opt/Hermes One/hermes-desktop` path and Chromium's reported attempt to execute the truncated `/opt/Hermes`; `--no-sandbox` avoids the failing zygote/sandbox child launch. No later packaging commit removes the spaced prefix.
- Inference: The official DEB deterministically installs to the risky spaced prefix; the reporter's and follow-up reproduction/relocation evidence establishes the Electron/Chromium launch failure on affected Ubuntu systems. A proposed `linux.installDir` setting would not work with the repository's builder version because that option does not exist.
- Recommended action: Build Linux artifacts with a Linux-only top-level `productName` override that has no spaces (for example `Hermes-One`/`hermes-one`), set a stable `linux.executableName`, and preserve the visible brand through `linux.desktop.entry.Name: Hermes One`. Wire the same Linux config/override into `package.json:29-30` and both release workflows (`.github/workflows/release.yml:142-186`, `.github/workflows/beta-release.yml:199-214`) so local and CI packages agree. Focused regression check: inspect the DEB data archive and desktop `Exec` target to assert no installed executable path contains whitespace, then launch the installed binary normally (without `--no-sandbox`) on Ubuntu 24.04 and the affected newer release.

## 2026-09-02 — #873 DEB omits installation of its bundled AppArmor profile

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/873
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: electron-builder 26.8.1 generates and bundles `resources/apparmor-profile` for FPM packages (`node_modules/app-builder-lib/out/targets/FpmTarget.js:27-47,118-129`) and its default `after-install.tpl` conditionally validates, copies, and loads that profile on AppArmor-enabled hosts (`node_modules/app-builder-lib/templates/linux/after-install.tpl:29-57`). However, both DEB and RPM configure `build/linux-after-install.sh` as a custom `afterInstall` (`electron-builder.yml:60-68`); the builder treats that as a replacement for the default template (`node_modules/app-builder-lib/out/targets/FpmTarget.js:37-46`). The custom script only chmods `chrome-sandbox` (`build/linux-after-install.sh:18-29`) and never installs AppArmor. This matches the 0.7.6 package containing the profile but becoming launchable only after the reporter manually copied/loaded it. No comments or subsequent packaging fix were present.
- Inference: The missing post-install step is deterministic on current source. The custom hook's comment that electron-builder does not provide this behavior is stale for the installed builder version; the default now handles both conditional sandbox permissions and AppArmor.
- Recommended action: Remove the DEB/RPM `afterInstall` overrides and retire `build/linux-after-install.sh`, allowing electron-builder's current default hook to handle the symlink, conditional SUID mode, desktop/MIME refresh, and AppArmor profile. Keep a custom hook only if it faithfully carries forward the complete upstream template. Focused regression check: build the DEB, inspect `postinst` for the AppArmor validate/copy/load commands and the corrected space-free prefix from #877, then install on Ubuntu 24.04 with AppArmor enabled and verify a normal menu launch without manual profile commands.

## 2026-09-02 — #857 Model picker can persist the previous model under the newly selected provider/base URL

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/857
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: Changing the provider group updates only `pickGroupKey` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:627-629`); `pickModel` is repaired later in an effect (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:605-613`). During that intervening render, `confirmModelPick` trusts the new `activeProvider` together with the stale previous `pickModel` and persists that cross-product (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:635-647`). The local store intentionally deduplicates by the full model/provider/base-URL triple (`src/main/models.ts:450-493`), and the injected remote compatibility endpoint does the same (`src/main/hermes-agent-compat.ts:162-185`), so a previous DeepSeek model submitted with GLM's URL becomes a distinct durable `provider: custom` row exactly as reported. Current tests verify store idempotence for identical triples but do not cover an immediate picker group switch (`tests/models-agent-config-sync.test.ts:44-75`; `tests/model-definitions.test.ts:72-96`). The non-network model/config/compat suites passed 25/25; the remote REST suite could not bind its localhost fixture in the sandbox and was not used as evidence.
- Inference: Timing determines whether a user hits the stale-selection window, but the invalid provider/model combination is reachable in current source and the storage layers correctly preserve what the UI mistakenly submits. Deduplicating only by model id would be unsafe because the same model may legitimately exist at multiple endpoints.
- Recommended action: In `Providers.tsx`, make a provider-group change synchronously select a valid model for that group, disable confirmation while the selection is not in `pickModelOptions`, and revalidate inside `confirmModelPick` before calling `addModel`. Do not weaken the provider+model+base-URL storage key. Focused regression check: render the picker with DeepSeek selected, switch to GLM and immediately confirm before effects flush; assert no `addModel(deepseek-v4-pro, custom, GLM_URL)` call occurs and only a model belonging to the GLM options can be persisted.

## 2026-09-03 — #930 Opening Hermes Desktop on Windows corrupts CRLF config.yaml

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/930
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: At checkout `aa17806efaa7f86aee2dfb36dae8f87afbd555c0`, `readPlatformOverride` walks every indented line in the platform block and accepts `enabled:` at any depth (`src/main/config.ts:1877-1884`), so `discord.voice_fx.enabled` is mistaken for the direct Discord override. `setPlatformEnabled` repeats the indentation-blind match, splits CRLF on `/\r?\n/`, but advances offsets by only `line.length + 1` (`src/main/config.ts:1942-1945,1969-1992`); its enable branch then removes bytes at that drifted offset (`src/main/config.ts:1994-2002`). A local execution of the current algorithm on a CRLF Discord fixture matched the nested flag and removed bytes from the preceding `voice_fx` region, leaving a dangling `abled: false`. The main IPC restarts a running gateway after this write (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2109-2121`). Repository search found no direct platform-config regression test. The current renderer invokes the write on an explicit toggle (`src/renderer/src/screens/Gateway/Gateway.tsx:211-223`); its current load/poll path is read-only (`src/renderer/src/screens/Gateway/Gateway.tsx:53-84`), so the packaged-version claim that launch alone initiates the write was not located in this checkout.
- Inference: The destructive CRLF mutation is deterministic whenever `setPlatformEnabled(discord, true)` reaches this fixture. The report's exact automatic-launch trigger may be specific to its 0.7.6 packaged renderer or another caller no longer present, but that does not reduce the config-corruption defect in the current shared writer.
- Recommended action: In `src/main/config.ts`, scan the platform block with exact source spans that retain each `\r\n`/`\n` terminator, derive the immediate-child indentation, and only read/edit an `enabled:` line at that indentation. Preserve the file's detected EOL for insertions and do not write when enabling finds no direct override. Add a focused temp-config test using the reported CRLF Discord/`voice_fx.enabled` fixture: `getPlatformEnabled` must ignore the nested flag, enabling must leave bytes and mtime unchanged, disabling must insert one direct child while preserving nested content and valid YAML.

## 2026-09-03 — #932 SSH backend fails when remote login shell is fish

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/932
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: `sshExec` appends the supplied command string directly to the OpenSSH argv (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:58-73`) and forwards stdin unchanged (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:100-106`). Many callers supply POSIX grammar, including `tmp=...`, `$tmp`, semicolon chains, and `|| true` in dashboard-token and port persistence (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2540-2569,2617-2635`) plus assignments, `[ ... ]`, and `for ...; do` in dashboard-root discovery (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2777-2791`). Some file helpers explicitly prefix their own commands with `sh -c` (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:215-240`), confirming the central helper does not establish a shell contract. Existing SSH tests exercise generated POSIX command strings, but repository search found no spawn-level assertion that all remote commands select `/bin/sh` or preserve stdin through that wrapper. The issue has no comments and reports the expected fish parse failure before the command runs.
- Inference: On an account whose configured OpenSSH command shell is fish, these unwrapped POSIX assignments cannot execute; fixing individual assignments would leave numerous other incompatible call sites. A POSIX shell is already an implicit remote prerequisite throughout this module.
- Recommended action: Make `sshExec` wrap every remote command as `/bin/sh -c <shellQuote(command)>` at the single spawn boundary, leaving its stdin pipe untouched; audit/remove only redundant inner wrappers when safe. Add a spawn/integration regression with a fish-login fixture covering token provisioning, port persistence, root probing, a health check, and `sshPython` stdin. Assert the complete command reaches `/bin/sh`, stdin arrives byte-for-byte, and stderr remains actionable.

That commit also removed the redundant second `model.options` request on turns where no slash command changed model state (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1431-1438`) and added a request-count regression (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.test.tsx:201-224`). Current rows use `content-visibility: auto` and intrinsic-size containment to bound off-screen layout (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:4940-4958`), while tables still render through memoized GFM Markdown (`src/renderer/src/components/AgentMarkdown.tsx:206-214`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageRow.tsx:285-311`). The focused Markdown/transport suites passed 18/18 on this checkout.
- Inference: The report's memory-leak diagnosis is contradicted by the issue-specific heap snapshots; the growth was live transcript DOM. The most direct `model.options` excess request was fixed after the report, and current layout containment addresses long-transcript stalls. This sweep did not reproduce the reporter's exact Windows/llama.cpp environment, so a current packaged-build retest remains appropriate before closure.

If it still freezes, collect a Performance trace and heap snapshots before/after unmount plus WebSocket request/response timing; do not infer a leak from working-set growth alone.
- Suggested close note: "We profiled the production message renderer with 5 and 50 table-heavy turns and found no detached-DOM retention: transcript nodes dropped from 17,169 live to 19 after unmount. Commit `5e7ce0c6` also removed the redundant per-turn `model.options` request and added regression coverage, while current chat rows contain off-screen layout. Please retest on the latest build; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a current Performance trace/heap snapshot if the freeze remains."

## 2026-09-04 — #929 Markdown table cells clip leading/trailing characters when containing long Windows paths

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/929
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: At checkout `823f1aae3bd5bb51e42f88f63dea719b6a8aa958`, the agent bubble applies `overflow-wrap: break-word` and horizontal overflow (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:5027-5040`), while inline code restores `white-space: pre-wrap` (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:5155-5167`). The table remains `width: 100%` with automatic layout, and its cells have no stronger wrapping/min-width rule (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:5548-5560`). A local headless-Chromium check with those rules and a long Windows path produced a 684 px scroll width inside a 460 px bubble; adding fixed table layout plus `overflow-wrap: anywhere` reduced it to 460 px. The existing Markdown regression only verifies that a table element renders (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageRow.test.tsx:44-51`), not constrained-width layout. The issue has no comments.
- Inference: `break-word` does not reduce the cell's min-content contribution, so the unbroken path can force the automatic table wider than the message bubble; the bubble's horizontal clipping/scrolling then hides path edges at common widths. The same constraint explains awkward adjacent CJK/label wrapping. This is a CSS layout defect, not Markdown parsing or lost text.
- Recommended action: In `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css`, add `table-layout: fixed` to agent tables and `overflow-wrap: anywhere; min-width: 0` to agent `th`/`td` (and inline `code` if required by Chromium's `pre-wrap` behavior). Avoid global `word-break: break-all`, which would degrade ordinary prose. Focused regression check: render the reported table at a narrow width in Chromium, assert the full code `textContent` remains present, every cell stays within the table, and the bubble has no horizontal overflow.

A cold send can start/probe the dashboard, obtain a fresh WebSocket URL, and connect with up to three attempts (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1146-1247`), then establish/resume a runtime session (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1266-1335`). Every ordinary model-selected send still performs one `model.options` request before `prompt.submit` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1339-1456,1618-1659`); commit `5e7ce0c6f117385c5c12ba9eb55d21aef4966a20` had already removed a redundant second request six days before this issue was filed, with request-count coverage at `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.test.tsx:201-224`. Focused transport/auth/metadata suites passed 24/24.

It therefore cannot support a single root cause or safe code change yet.

Use those measurements to determine whether to cache/skip the unchanged-model validation, optimize dashboard startup/session recovery, or investigate backend/model latency; do not change the transport speculatively.

## 2026-09-05 — #866 Support self-signed CAs for API URLs

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/866
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The reported `get-hermes-version` IPC resolves direct Remote mode to `remoteGetHermesVersion` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:508-526,783-790`), which fetches `/api/status` through Node's `https.request` without a custom CA or agent (`src/main/remote-metadata.ts:25-79,106-110`). Token-auth dashboard requests do the same (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:71-150`); health/capabilities/chat paths also select Node `https` without CA configuration (`src/main/hermes.ts:244-289,1069-1098,1618-1627`). OAuth status probing uses the main process's Node `fetch` (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:107-175`), while only authenticated OAuth requests move to Electron's Chromium-backed `net` session. Main startup has no system-CA initialization (`src/main/index.ts:1-21`), and repository search found no `--use-system-ca`, `tls.setDefaultCACertificates`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, certificate verification hook, or pinned-certificate support. This matches the issue's Node TLS `SELF_SIGNED_CERT_IN_CHAIN` stack; the issue has no comments.
- Inference: A CA trusted in macOS Keychain is still rejected because these main-process Node TLS clients use Node's CA defaults rather than Electron/Chromium's operating-system trust path. The impact extends beyond onboarding/version detection to token remote metadata, sessions, health, capabilities, and legacy HTTPS chat.
- Recommended action: At the earliest main-process bootstrap point, initialize Node's default CA list to the union of its bundled default CAs and the operating-system CA set using the runtime's `tls.getCACertificates(...)` / `tls.setDefaultCACertificates(...)`; retain both sets and fail closed. Do not use `rejectUnauthorized: false`. If an explicit private-CA option is later added, accept a PEM CA and scope it to the configured connection rather than allowing arbitrary certificates. Affected files: `src/main/index.ts` plus a small testable CA bootstrap helper, and remote TLS tests. Focused regression check: serve the metadata endpoint with a leaf signed by a temporary trusted root, assert the merged trust path succeeds while an unrelated self-signed leaf still fails, and cover `get-hermes-version` plus one shared remote-request caller.

## 2026-09-05 — #874 Uninstall opens `findstr /r /c:" 23836 "` command window and stalls

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/874
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The screenshots show a Windows Terminal window titled with `findstr /r /c:" 23836 "`, followed by a Python `Uninstalling...` console. The standalone repository no longer contains this uninstall implementation, but the currently installed Hermes Agent Desktop source at commit `55ef425d0c3967022cb54093112e638c5c3f9e01` generates that exact command in its Windows cleanup batch (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/electron/desktop-uninstall.ts:194-235`). It polls the still-running Desktop PID once per second and only advances when the PID disappears or 60 iterations elapse (`desktop-uninstall.ts:220-235`); its test asserts both the `findstr` pipeline and cap (`desktop-uninstall.test.ts:211-235`). The caller writes the batch, launches `cmd.exe /c` detached with `windowsHide: true`, then requests app quit after 800 ms (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/electron/main.ts:10427-10469`). Quit can additionally be deferred up to four seconds for SSH cleanup (`main.ts:10679-10694`). The issue has no comments, and this Windows-only behavior was not reproduced on the macOS triage host.
- Inference: The screenshot proves the generated PID-wait pipeline is surfacing despite the intended hidden top-level `cmd.exe`; closing that console terminates/bypasses the waiter and lets Python cleanup proceed. Current source bounds the wait to about 60 seconds, so a truly indefinite hang is not demonstrated, but a visible, apparently stalled helper for that interval is consistent with the report. Because the checked Hermes Agent checkout predates the report by two days and the behavior is platform-specific, this remains likely rather than fully reproduced.
- Recommended action: Fix this in the Hermes Agent Desktop, not the standalone app's NSIS config. Replace the repeated `tasklist | findstr` subprocess pipeline with one no-console helper that waits on the Desktop process handle/PID under a strict deadline and then launches cleanup; ensure child processes inherit no-window creation flags and log failures to a file instead of opening a terminal. Keep the forced deadline and post-exit directory-removal retries. Affected files: `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/electron/desktop-uninstall.ts`, its test, and possibly the spawn boundary in `main.ts`. Focused regression check: on packaged Windows, start uninstall against a live Desktop PID and assert no Windows Terminal/conhost window becomes visible, cleanup begins after exit, and a deliberately non-exiting PID reaches the deadline and continues rather than hanging.

## 2026-09-06 — #940 Installer fails: venv creation returns exit code 259 on Windows

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/940
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The attached bootstrap log shows fallback Python 3.12.14 installing successfully, but the venv stage then reselects managed CPython 3.11.16, declares the venv ready, and proceeds even though that venv interpreter exits 259; all dependency-install tiers subsequently retry against the same unusable interpreter and fail. At upstream Hermes Agent commit `245e48008fa814b3251f50755eb656bd9fb86cb1`, `scripts/install.ps1:1190-1247` accepts `uv python find` success/provenance without executing the interpreter, `scripts/install.ps1:1250-1328` does not require a zero exit status from its version probe, and `scripts/install.ps1:2695-2736` treats `uv venv` success plus existence of `venv\\Scripts\\python.exe` as a working interpreter without running it. The standalone Desktop delegates Windows installation to that upstream script (`src/main/installer.ts:1038-1060`). The issue has no comments.
- Inference: The installer validation defect is confirmed even though the host-specific reason CPython returns 259 is not yet established. The stage boundary loses the already-working 3.12 fallback, and the lack of a post-create execution probe lets a broken 3.11 venv masquerade as successful; repeated dependency strategies cannot repair that prerequisite failure. The evidence does not establish that all uv-managed CPython 3.11 installations are affected.
- Recommended action: In upstream `scripts/install.ps1`, execute both each candidate base interpreter and every newly created venv interpreter using a no-window process with a small isolated Python probe, wait for completion, and require exit code 0 plus expected structured output. Mark a failed candidate ineligible for the current run, recreate the venv explicitly with the already-verified fallback (3.12 here), and stop before dependency installation if no interpreter passes. Affected files: upstream `scripts/install.ps1` and its managed-Python installer tests; the standalone Desktop only consumes that script. Focused regression check: use a Windows shim whose venv executable exists but exits 259, assert the venv stage never reports success and dependency installation never starts on it, then verify a healthy fallback creates and probes successfully.

## 2026-09-06 — #939 Local session list/cache ignores Hermes Agent native archived flag

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/939
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: Direct local listing selects sessions without any archive predicate (`src/main/sessions.ts:263-285`). Both cold/incremental cache queries omit archive state (`src/main/session-cache.ts:125-149`), and cache reconciliation only checks row existence and message count, so an archived row remains cached (`src/main/session-cache.ts:201-255`); cached reads merely slice stored JSON (`src/main/session-cache.ts:261-271`). In contrast, both remote routes explicitly send `archived=exclude` (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:283-300`). The local IPC paths call these direct/cache implementations (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2244-2270,2700-2746`), and the renderer preserves old rows when a successful sync returns an empty array (`src/renderer/src/screens/Sessions/Sessions.tsx:420-446`). Existing cache tests use a schema without `archived` and do not cover archive transitions (`tests/session-cache-sync.test.ts:65-105`). The issue has no comments.
- Inference: Native Agent archiving neither removes a session from a warm Desktop cache nor prevents it from appearing after a cold rebuild. Because archive changes do not change `started_at`, the incremental query cannot discover the state transition; conversely, unarchiving an older row would not reintroduce it. Even after backend filtering is corrected, the renderer's empty-result fallback would keep stale rows when all sessions are archived.
- Recommended action: Detect the optional `sessions.archived` column for backward compatibility. Filter direct listing with `COALESCE(archived, 0) = 0`; during every cache sync reconcile all session IDs plus archive/message-count state, evict archived IDs, and load visible IDs missing from cache so old unarchived sessions return. Treat a valid empty sync as authoritative in the renderer, while representing sync failures separately. Affected files: `src/main/sessions.ts`, `src/main/session-cache.ts`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Sessions/Sessions.tsx`, and cache/session tests. Focused regression check: cover cold exclusion, warm archive eviction, restoration after unarchiving an old row, an all-archived empty list, pagination/profile isolation, and a legacy database with no `archived` column.

## 2026-09-06 — #681 Custom providers share a single CUSTOM_API_KEY — per-provider API keys are ignored at runtime

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/681
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: Named custom providers now derive a dedicated `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_<NAME>_KEY` (`src/shared/url-key-map.ts:45-61`), and the provider UI persists that identity instead of only a generic custom provider (`src/renderer/src/components/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:956-980`). Provider-dictionary import aliases configured keys to the derived per-name key and upsert mirrors them back to Agent config (`src/main/providers-store.ts:80-127,163-203`). Legacy `custom_providers` synchronization writes per-name and host-derived keys and retains `providerLabel` (`src/main/models.ts:304-416`); runtime resolution matches the model's base URL, derives the key from that provider label/name, and only then falls back to the shared key (`src/main/hermes.ts:2623-2653`). Focused provider/config/model suites passed 69/69. The relevant changes landed after the report in commits `d3340c82ba4abcac7d0213dfa31b1ec4bc5d9001`, `0f6175f7c6e7e0690a44b7dcd92c5bd425a85dc6`, and `9ddca9587d5a943cd4853bc8060af31f74ae60ef`. The issue has no comments.
- Inference: For the report's two distinct custom endpoints, current configuration synchronization and runtime endpoint matching preserve provider identity and select distinct credentials. The old parser can still label models generically as `custom`, but the retained `providerLabel` and endpoint-derived key path prevent the reported collapse. This conclusion is source/test based; two live third-party endpoints were not exercised.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retest the current release with two named custom providers using different base URLs and keys, through both legacy `custom_providers` and current `providers:` configuration, and close if alternating requests authenticate correctly. Suggested close note: "Named custom providers now receive dedicated `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_<NAME>_KEY` variables, retain their provider identity on imported models, and resolve credentials by the matching endpoint at runtime. The fixes landed in `d3340c82`, `0f6175f7`, and `9ddca958`. Please retest the latest release with two distinct custom endpoints; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with current config/log details if credentials still cross over."

The focused stream/renderer suite passed in today's checkout.
- Inference: The screenshots are consistent with the stale-snapshot chunk race followed by incorrect live/final reconciliation, and current code has direct guards for both truncation and duplication. The affected Windows profile/model was not available for a packaged live retest, so this is source-and-regression evidence rather than a fresh reproduction.
