Focused regression check: emit high-rate deltas with alternating dropped chunks and a repeated completion seam, then assert exactly one assistant bubble whose text equals the final payload. Suggested close note: "Current chat streaming serializes deltas through a synchronous transcript ref and replaces lossy or duplicated live text with the canonical completion. The relevant fixes landed after 0.7.3 in `e233752b`, `8b55ef98`, and `852e0d48`, with regressions for dropped chunks and duplicate seams. Please retest the affected profile/model on the latest build; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a current event trace if it persists."

## 2026-09-07 — #821 no pending answer request

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/821
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report shows the raw `no pending answer request` protocol error when a follow-up, possibly with an image, is sent while another turn is active. Current Desktop accepts ordinary text/attachments during a turn into an explicit FIFO queue and drains one item only after loading ends, restoring the item on send failure (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:273-274,749-757,801-847`); input readiness deliberately permits queuing while loading (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx:497-504`). Clarify replies are now sent only when an exact live `request_id` has been recorded (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1119-1131,1478-1504`), and the legacy bridge likewise keys one-shot resolvers by that ID and clears them at turn end (`src/main/hermes.ts:1177-1209,2065-2075,2213-2260`; `src/main/ipc/register.ts:1851-1860`). The currently installed Hermes Agent explicitly makes a late `clarify.respond` return `{status: "expired"}` instead of raw error 4009 (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:12163-12183`), with a direct regression at `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/tui_gateway/test_protocol.py:285-306`.
- Inference: The exact screenshot is the old raw gateway response produced when a reply reached an already-expired clarify request. Current client queuing, exact request correlation, and Agent-side idempotent expiry handling jointly cover both the ordinary mid-turn follow-up and the late-clarify race. The original timing/image combination was not reproduced on its Windows setup.
- Recommended action: Retest on the latest Desktop and Hermes Agent by queuing both text-only and image-bearing follow-ups during an active turn; close if each sends once after the turn without a red error. Focused regression check: expire a clarify request, submit its late answer, assert an `expired` result rather than error 4009, then verify an ordinary in-flight follow-up remains queued and sends once. Suggested close note: "Current Desktop queues ordinary follow-ups while a turn is active and correlates clarify answers to their exact request ID. Current Hermes Agent also treats a late clarify answer as an idempotent expired response instead of surfacing `no pending answer request`. Please retest the latest Desktop and Agent with both text and image follow-ups; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a current trace if it recurs."

## 2026-09-07 — #852 High, sustained GPU usage while idle — Render/3D engine pinned at ~65–70% on Linux/Wayland

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/852
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The report measured roughly 71% Render/3D and 25% CPU after long idle on KDE Wayland/Intel, and a comment says restarting dropped Hermes GPU usage to roughly 1.2%. Current navigation lazy-mounts a view on first visit but keeps it mounted under `display: none` afterward (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:266-290`); Office is passed `visible=false` rather than unmounted (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:959-962`). That flag gates data and GPU-status polling (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx:202-221`), but `Office3D` is rendered unconditionally (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx:601-619`). Its React Three Fiber `Canvas` uses shadows, up to 2× DPR, and the default continuous frame loop because no `frameloop` is specified (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx:414-439`); the scene contains active `useFrame` callbacks, including the camera rig (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx:141-155`). Existing Office visibility coverage mocks the 3D component, so it does not test whether rendering pauses. The focused Office/GPU suites passed, but they do not exercise a real Linux WebGL renderer.
- Inference: Once Office has been visited, hiding its pane does not stop the WebGL render loop or scene simulations, so sustained background GPU use is a deterministic source defect and fits the restart recovery. The exact 65–70% level and gradual buildup were not reproduced on the macOS triage host, and the report does not state whether Office had been visited, so the full Linux symptom remains likely rather than directly reproduced.
- Recommended action: Preserve Office state but pass an active-rendering flag derived from `visible && document.visibilityState === "visible"` into `Office3D`, using `frameloop="always"` only while active and `frameloop="never"` while hidden/backgrounded; invalidate/resume on reactivation. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx`, and a non-mocked visibility/render-loop regression. Focused regression check: visit Office, switch to Chat or background the document, and assert frame callbacks stop and packaged Linux GPU returns near idle; returning to Office must resume animations.

## 2026-09-08 — #944 Legacy SSH model library deduplicates custom models without base URL

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/944
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue targets current checkout `3f744975f818bbb40ed029e6b3022cd0c5ad7a24`, has no comments, and describes an offline mocked-SSH reproduction. The current legacy SSH add path reads `models.json` and returns an existing row when only `model` and `provider` match, without comparing the supplied `baseUrl` (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:3479-3505,3517-3539`). The IPC handler reaches that path whenever SSH is explicitly legacy or automatic dashboard access falls back (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:607-637,2875-2907`). In contrast, local storage normalizes trailing slashes/case and keys an attachment by model + provider + base URL (`src/main/models.ts:450-493`), while the Dashboard compatibility endpoint uses the same three-part key (`src/main/hermes-agent-compat.ts:162-185`). Git blame shows the SSH predicate has remained two-field since `af10dd898`, even after local dedup gained base-URL identity in `37449bcf3`. Repository tests cover Dashboard model writes but have no legacy `sshAddModel` identity regression.
- Inference: The second attachment is deterministically discarded whenever two legacy-SSH custom endpoints expose the same model ID, and the returned first row can route selection to the wrong URL and credentials. This is transport drift, not expected model-definition sharing: metadata may be shared by model ID, but endpoint attachments are distinct.
- Recommended action: Reuse one shared attachment-key normalizer across local, Dashboard compatibility, and legacy SSH; minimally, include normalized `m.baseUrl === baseUrl` in `sshAddModel` while retaining trailing-slash/host-case idempotence. Affected files: `src/main/ssh-remote.ts`, preferably a shared model-key helper used by `src/main/models.ts` and `src/main/hermes-agent-compat.ts`, plus `src/main/ssh-remote.test.ts`. Focused regression check: through a mocked SSH file transport, add `custom/shared-model` at endpoint A, endpoint B, and an equivalent spelling of B; assert two stored rows, B's returned row has B's URL, and the equivalent B add performs no write.

## 2026-09-08 — #819 Desktop voice conversation records silent audio — STT returns empty transcript

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/819
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The report includes backend logs proving a duration-bearing WebM reached healthy local Whisper, direct transcription of known-good WebM succeeded, and ffmpeg measured the Desktop captures at `RMS -inf`; a second user reproduced on two Apple Silicon Macs with 0.7.6. The reported `voiceConversation` implementation lives in the Hermes Agent Desktop rather than this standalone tree, and the currently installed Agent checkout still requests `echoCancellation: true` and `noiseSuppression: true` without an explicit `autoGainControl` value (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/app/chat/composer/hooks/use-mic-recorder.ts:169-205`). Its meter derives RMS and `heardSpeech` from that processed stream (`use-mic-recorder.ts:99-163`), while the conversation loop silently resets to idle both when speech is not detected and when STT returns an empty transcript (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/app/chat/composer/hooks/use-voice-conversation.ts:95-148`). The standalone 0.7.7 fallback still uses browser-default `{audio: true}` capture and has no energy/track-settings diagnostics (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useVoiceInput.ts:92-163`), although it does display `No speech detected` via `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx:658-662`. Git history shows neither standalone capture behavior nor the affected installed Agent recorder has a later targeted fix; no automated microphone-capture regression exists.
- Inference: The recorded-silence symptom is well evidenced and still actionable, but the proposed Chromium/macOS processing interaction is not independently proven on this triage host. Explicit processing settings are the strongest current lead; signing/TCC is less consistent with a live duration-bearing track and should not be treated as the root cause without a controlled comparison.
- Recommended action: In the Agent Desktop recorder, request a mono raw-capture profile with `echoCancellation`, `noiseSuppression`, and `autoGainControl` explicitly false, using ideal rather than exact sample-rate/channel constraints and retrying `{audio: true}` only if constraints are rejected. Log the selected track settings and treat sustained near-zero RMS as a user-visible capture failure instead of silently returning to idle; mirror the safe constraint/detection behavior in standalone `useVoiceInput`. Affected files: the Agent Desktop `use-mic-recorder.ts` and `use-voice-conversation.ts`, standalone `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useVoiceInput.ts`, and recorder tests. Focused regression check: inject a synthetic non-zero audio track and a zero-energy track on packaged Apple Silicon; assert the first produces a non-empty blob/transcription and the second shows an actionable error, then compare raw-processing-off versus fallback settings on the reported machines.

## 2026-09-08 — #803 SSH Tunnel dashboard falls back to Legacy with invalid API key and blank Show details

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/803
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports two failures in Desktop 0.7.2. That tag did conflate the gateway API key with dashboard authentication: it tunneled to the gateway api_server port and chose `conn.apiKey || sshReadRemoteApiKey` for `/api/*` (`v0.7.2:src/main/ipc/register.ts:367-385`; `v0.7.2:src/main/dashboard.ts:131-150`). Current code instead provisions/reads `HERMES_DASHBOARD_SESSION_TOKEN`, starts the remote dashboard with that token, and verifies an authenticated `/api/sessions` probe before handing the target to callers (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2529-2579,2664-2703,2705-2736`); `getSshDashboardSessionConfig` tunnels to that dashboard and returns the same token (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:449-475`). These changes are present in v0.7.5+ but absent from v0.7.2/v0.7.3; focused SSH/dashboard/model suites passed 44/44. However, the reported details-navigation defect remains: `ConfigHealthBanner` promises Settings → Diagnose but calls an unparameterized callback (`src/renderer/src/components/ConfigHealthBanner.tsx:5-20,208-215`); Layout therefore calls `openSettings(undefined)` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:884-899`), which resolves to Appearance, and the current Settings modal has no Diagnose section or `ConfigHealth` render at all (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/SettingsModal.tsx:28-112,202-213`). `src/renderer/src/screens/Settings/ConfigHealth.tsx:130-205` is consequently orphaned. Banner tests do not click “Show details” (`src/renderer/src/components/ConfigHealthBanner.test.tsx:36-103`).
- Inference: The original 401/fallback cause appears fixed in current releases, but the same issue cannot be closed because its “Show details” path still cannot display the promised diagnostic report; it now opens the wrong pane rather than necessarily a black view. The surviving bug came from the Settings-modal navigation refactor leaving the banner contract and Diagnose component disconnected.
- Recommended action: Add `diagnose` to `SettingsSection`/navigation, render `ConfigHealth` for that section, and make the banner invoke `onOpenDiagnose("diagnose")` (or give the callback a dedicated no-argument implementation that always targets it). Affected files: `src/renderer/src/components/settings/SettingsModal.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/components/ConfigHealthBanner.tsx`, the Layout callback, and navigation/banner tests. Focused regression check: render a warning report, click “Show details,” and assert the global Settings modal opens with the Diagnose item active and that exact issue text visible; separately retain the SSH authenticated-token tests to prevent the resolved 401 from returning.

## 2026-09-09 — #949 Non-CJK text (Russian) also corrupted during streaming

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/949
- Classification: **NEEDS INFO**
- Observed evidence: The report has no Desktop/Agent version, raw `message.delta` / `message.complete` payload, or comments. Current local streaming applies accepted deltas through a synchronous transcript ref, specifically guarding against an older React snapshot dropping chunks (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:930-957,991-1062`). Completion reads `text`/`rendered` from the event and reconciles it with the streamed bubble (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:641-727`); the merge replaces a run-based chunk-dropped copy with the final text and replaces a corrupted prefix when the texts converge on a substantial suffix (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:450-495`). The report's four rendered/DB text pairs would all resolve to the complete DB spelling if that complete spelling were the `message.complete` text: three are ordered chunk-dropped copies under `isLossyChunkCopy` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/lossyText.ts:24-61`) and the short `Чтолось` pair meets the common-suffix replacement guard. Existing tests cover dropped chunks, repeated seams, and false-positive protection, but only with Latin text (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.test.ts:51-105`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/lossyText.test.ts:12-61`). The focused stream/GPU suite passed 96/96.
- Inference: The visible-vs-DB mismatch is credible, but the issue's asserted greedy-matcher root cause is not established by the supplied evidence and does not explain the examples if `message.complete` contains the DB text. A current failure would instead require the complete payload itself to differ from the persisted row, an unrepresented delta ordering, or a build predating the July stream fixes (`852e0d482`, `3b381913b`). Restart recovery is consistent with the DB being authoritative, but does not identify which live event was wrong.
- Recommended action: Ask for the exact Desktop and Hermes Agent versions plus one redacted accepted-event trace containing every `message.delta` and the `message.complete` payload for a corrupt turn, alongside that turn's DB text. Do not change the matcher until that trace is reduced to a failing Cyrillic regression. If the completion payload is non-canonical while the DB is correct, the smallest safe fix is to treat live completion as provisional and perform the same-session DB reconciliation after local completion; otherwise fix the first event-ordering divergence shown by the trace.

## 2026-09-09 — #750 Hermes One desktop client crashes in the GPU subprocess during normal use

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/750
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report targets Desktop 0.6.358 on AMD/Wayland and describes a GPU crash followed by an immediate relaunch crash unless all user data is deleted. Current 0.7.7 installs GPU handling before app readiness (`src/main/index.ts:1-12`). Any non-clean GPU child exit is handled once: Desktop persists `disable-gpu.flag`, relaunches with the one-shot `--hermes-gpu-disabled` sentinel, and exits the failed process (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:304-349`). On the next boot the flag/sentinel disables hardware acceleration and GPU compositing before Chromium starts while retaining SwiftShader (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:128-209`). The flag is scoped to `userData`, expires after 24 hours, and can be controlled from Settings without deleting Preferences or Local Storage (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:42-76,95-105,120-153,225-301`). Tests verify flag persistence, sentinel recovery even when persistence fails, clean-exit filtering, and one-relaunch behavior (`tests/gpu-fallback.test.ts:193-239`); the focused suite passed 96/96. The guard originated in `8bf2fe0f6` and was hardened after the report in `3ad08e6e2`.
- Inference: Current code does not implement the report's proposed GPU-cache deletion or Mesa extension override, so it does not cure the underlying driver defect. It does, however, address the user-visible crash loop with the safer cross-platform response: restart immediately in software rendering while preserving all user data. A packaged AMD/Mesa 26.1.0 live run was not available in this triage.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retest current 0.7.7+ on the same AMD/Wayland machine under sustained tool-stream updates. Close if a GPU crash causes one automatic software-rendered recovery with preferences/history intact. Suggested close note: "Current Hermes Desktop catches abnormal GPU-process exits, relaunches once with hardware acceleration disabled, and persists a short-lived fallback flag without deleting Preferences, Local Storage, or session data. The recovery guard is covered by crash-loop regressions and was hardened in `3ad08e6e`. Please retest 0.7.7+ on the affected AMD/Wayland setup; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a current crash log if recovery still loops."

## 2026-09-09 — #785 Hermes One Session Proliferation and Idle API Consumption Bug Report

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/785
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report and attachment target Hermes Agent 0.16.0 (2026-06-05) and show failed delegated children plus four sessions detached on WebSocket disconnect. The currently installed Agent checkout `55ef425d0c3967022cb54093112e638c5c3f9e01` has explicit post-report lifecycle guards in the relevant, unmodified files: oneshot wraps construction and execution in `finally`, calls `agent.close()` (which ends its DB session), and closes the store (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/oneshot.py:398-469`); every delegated child shares the parent DB (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tools/delegate_tool.py:1364-1406`) and its success/error/timeout path ends in a `finally` that calls `child.close()` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tools/delegate_tool.py:2325-2404`). WebSocket disconnect now routes all owned sessions through one teardown path, immediately reaping `close_on_disconnect` sessions and scheduling other non-running detached sessions for a 20-second orphan reap (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tui_gateway/ws.py:434-475`; `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:826-906`). Unified finalization writes `ended_at`, interrupts session-owned async delegations, and closes the agent/worker (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:665-768`). Desktop itself highlights sessions by the selected `currentSessionId`, not by `ended_at IS NULL` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Sessions/Sessions.tsx:232-243,954-973,1111`), and keeps at most one managed local dashboard per profile within the process (`src/main/dashboard.ts:67-75,184-190,632-708`).
- Inference: Current Agent code directly covers the report's two harmful lifecycle mechanisms: failed children now close, and disconnected idle sessions are finalized instead of remaining detached indefinitely. Concurrent child creation remains intentional fan-out controlled by `delegation.max_concurrent_children`, and one gateway per profile is also intentional; seeing several session rows or profile gateways is therefore not independently proof of idle consumption. External legacy LaunchAgents can still create competing processes outside Desktop's in-memory ownership, so the original machine should be retested after removing obsolete services and updating Agent.
- Recommended action: Update Hermes Agent and repeat the report's provider-403 batch and WebSocket-disconnect scenarios, then close if every child receives a terminal `ended_at` and API traffic stops after the orphan grace window. Focused regression check: spawn three delegated children against a deterministic 403 provider, assert all three DB rows end on every error path, disconnect the owning WebSocket, wait beyond the configured grace, and assert no live runtime/delegation remains. Suggested close note: "Current Hermes Agent closes oneshot and delegated-child sessions in `finally`, and its dashboard now reaps disconnected non-running sessions through a grace-windowed, idempotent teardown that writes the terminal DB state and interrupts owned background delegations. Please update Agent and rerun the original 403 fan-out plus WebSocket-disconnect test; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with current versions/logs if API calls continue after teardown."

## 2026-09-10 — #952 Deleted profiles reappear after restarting Hermes One

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/952
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and omits OS/version/mode, but its signed-in Hermes One path is deterministic in current source. The delete control invokes only the local/SSH profile deletion bridge (`src/renderer/src/components/profile/ProfileModal.tsx:252-262`; `src/main/ipc/register.ts:2447-2451`), and local deletion shells out to `hermes profile delete` without inspecting or deleting the profile's linked cloud agent (`src/main/profiles.ts:328-358`). Cloud sync explicitly promises that deletions never propagate (`src/main/agent-sync.ts:23-29,348-351`). After the local directory and its `cloud-sync.json` disappear, the surviving backend agent is unclaimed and the sync pass calls `createProfile`, pulls its data, and records `action: "created-local"` (`src/main/agent-sync.ts:410-419,636-667`). The Agents page automatically runs that pass once per signed-in visit and reloads the list when a local profile is created (`src/renderer/src/screens/Agents/Agents.tsx:125-156`). Existing tests intentionally prove both cloud-only pull-creation and the absence of any DELETE request (`src/main/agent-sync.test.ts:397-428,430-465`); the focused profiles/sync suite passed.
- Inference: Restart is not the deletion failure itself; reopening/visiting the Agents surface triggers cloud reconciliation, which materializes the cloud counterpart again. The report's unverified theory that the local directory survives is unnecessary to explain the symptom. Unsigned local-only profiles should remain deleted, so mode/account state is useful for confirming the reporter's exact instance but not for establishing this cloud-linked defect.
- Recommended action: Make profile deletion cloud-aware. Before removing a profile that is linked to the current signed-in account, delete its `/api/agents/{id}` counterpart and only report success/remove the local directory after that request succeeds; offer an explicit "local only" unlink choice if preserving the cloud agent is desired. Do not silently turn a failed cloud delete into local success. Affected files: `src/main/agent-sync.ts` (authenticated delete helper), `src/main/profiles.ts` or a new deletion orchestrator, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, the profile modal copy/error path, and sync/profile tests. Focused regression check: delete a linked named profile, run the next auto-sync/restart flow, and assert one backend DELETE, no `created-local` outcome, no recreated directory, and an actionable error with the local profile retained when DELETE fails.

## 2026-09-10 — #951 Bug: Auxiliary vision model loses API key when provider changes in Desktop UI

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/951
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports Agent 0.21.1 on Windows. The standalone Desktop editor sends only provider/model/base URL (`src/renderer/src/components/AuxiliaryTasksSection.tsx:160-168`) and its writer updates exactly those three YAML fields while preserving every other task field, so an existing `key_env` survives a provider change (`src/main/auxiliary-config.ts:60-63,139-155`). Reset likewise changes only provider/model/base URL (`src/main/auxiliary-config.ts:158-167`). The current upstream Agent at commit `7cdc6a73388356e9eaa5122b010dc20b9179170c` has the same defect: `_apply_aux_assignment_sync` calls `clear_model_endpoint_credentials` on a built-in provider change (`hermes_cli/web_server_config.py:676-729`), but that helper removes only `api_key`, legacy `api`, `api_mode`, and optional `base_url`—not `key_env` (`hermes_cli/config.py:806-822`). Its Desktop caller sends provider/model and only an optional base URL, never the provider credential pointer (`apps/desktop/src/app/settings/model-settings.tsx:702-774`). Current tests cover the three routing fields but contain no stale-`key_env` switch case (`tests/auxiliary-config.test.ts:215-321`); the focused auxiliary suite passed. Conversely, the reported "Set to main" non-persistence is not supported by current upstream source: the UI calls `setModelAssignment`, the backend calls `save_config`, and refreshes (`model-settings.tsx:716-744`; `web_server_config.py:727-744`).
- Inference: A stale `key_env` can therefore select the previous provider's secret after either built-in or custom endpoint changes, matching the reported authentication failure. Whether the endpoint returns 401 or 404 depends on its auth behavior. The separate restart/reversion claim needs a before/after config and current logs; it should not be folded into the credential fix without a reproduction.
- Recommended action: At the Agent assignment chokepoint, treat `key_env` as part of the provider-owned credential lifecycle: on provider change clear the old pointer, resolve the new provider's raw `providers.<name>.key_env` (never its expanded secret), and persist that pointer for both built-in and custom/base-URL branches. Mirror the same reconciliation in this repository's direct YAML writer, or route it through the Agent API to avoid two drifting implementations. Affected files: upstream `hermes_cli/web_server_config.py` and its tests; locally `src/main/auxiliary-config.ts`, `tests/auxiliary-config.test.ts`, and possibly the editor contract. Current pure-HTTP Remote mode unconditionally replaces the Providers and standalone Skills pages with `RemoteNotice` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:965-985`), and the Capabilities screen independently replaces its Skills tab with the same notice (`src/renderer/src/screens/Tools/Tools.tsx:640-646`). MCP itself is not globally blocked: Remote mode opens Capabilities on MCP and proxies CRUD/test/catalog to `/api/mcp/*` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Tools/Tools.tsx:311-322`; `src/main/mcp-servers.ts:607-644,667-709,799-880,889-930`). Focused skills/MCP transport tests passed, showing the underlying clients are usable even though Skills/Providers are hidden.
- Inference: The broad report is correct that remote parity is incomplete, but it combines two causes: stale renderer-only gates definitely hide Skills and model/provider surfaces, while an MCP failure is endpoint/version/auth-specific and cannot be diagnosed from the one-sentence report. The fact that the Hermes Agent repository's own packaged Desktop works is consistent with this standalone UI having drifted from the available remote API contract.
- Recommended action: Remove the RemoteNotice gates only for surfaces whose operations are already remote-aware: enable installed Skills and remote model selection now, while keeping provider-secret editing server-owned and clearly labeled. Retain MCP's current remote path and request the exact Agent version, URL, auth mode, failing endpoint/status, and screenshot/log from the reporter before changing it. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Tools/Tools.tsx`, the Providers/model selection composition, and remote-mode UI tests. Focused regression check: with Remote mode and mocked authenticated Dashboard endpoints, navigate to Skills, MCP, and model selection; assert no unsupported notice on supported operations, requests target the remote profile/API (never local files/CLI), remote 404s get capability-specific guidance, and server-owned credential controls remain disabled.

## 2026-09-11 — #799 Remote mode incompatible with hermes-agent 0.17.0 — dashboard/sessions transport spams 401s and breaks auto chat

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/799
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: Two comments reproduce the same mismatch on Desktop 0.7.3/Agent 0.18.2 and Desktop 0.7.6/Agent 0.20.0; the latter reports an Electron-level fix by sending both authentication headers and allowing model-library reads in legacy mode. Current `remoteRequestJson` still sends only `X-Hermes-Session-Token`, not `Authorization: Bearer` (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:90-107`). Session listing still falls back from `/api/profiles/sessions` to `/api/sessions` without changing that header (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:283-300`), and direct-Remote list/cache IPC calls it regardless of `remoteChatTransport` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2244-2261,2697-2713`). The current model-list handler also still rejects Remote legacy mode before making its read (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2843-2852`). Existing tests assert the session-token header and endpoint fallback but do not cover a bearer-only gateway (`tests/remote-sessions.test.ts:285-304,315-359`).
- Inference: A raw Agent api_server on port 8642 that accepts only `Authorization: Bearer API_SERVER_KEY` will continue returning the reported 401 for background session reads, even when legacy `/v1` chat works. The unconditional Remote session IPC path explains why selecting Legacy does not stop the failures. The model-read guard is an additional parity defect confirmed by the latest reporter's successful patched run.
- Recommended action: For token-auth Remote requests, send `Authorization: Bearer <token>` alongside `X-Hermes-Session-Token` (or negotiate the endpoint/auth scheme once and cache the capability); retain cookie-only handling for OAuth. Remove only the Remote-legacy guard for read-only `list-models`; keep model mutations, OAuth, and media guards unchanged. Affected files: `src/main/remote-sessions.ts`, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, and their focused tests. Regression check: use a fixture that rejects `X-Hermes-Session-Token` alone but accepts Bearer, then assert session list/cache sync and legacy-mode model reads succeed with no 401 while model writes remain rejected.

Focused dashboard, SSH, session, and OAuth suites passed 59/59.

A real remote matching the reporter's installation layout was not available, so closure should follow a current-version retest rather than source evidence alone.

Direct Remote browser OAuth is also implemented. Please retest with current Desktop and Agent; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with the remote dashboard log if either path still fails."

## 2026-09-12 — #795 Remote/SSH mode: chat POSTs /v1/chat/completions -> HTTP 405 on Hermes Agent v0.17 (serve/dashboard has no /v1)

- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report is from Desktop 0.7.1/Agent 0.17.0 and correctly demonstrates that the dashboard on port 9119 has `/api/*` plus `/api/ws`, not `/v1`. Current SSH setup treats those servers as disjoint: an available dashboard is started and tunneled with its session token (`src/main/dashboard.ts:159-181`; `src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2887-2971`), while only a genuinely dashboard-less target provisions `API_SERVER_KEY`/`API_SERVER_ENABLED`, waits for gateway `/health`, and tunnels the gateway `/v1` port (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:529-584`). The renderer connects to `/api/ws`; if that reachable dashboard's WebSocket fails it retries three times and raises a tagged error instead of falling through to `/v1` on the dashboard tunnel (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1146-1246,1579-1598`). This exact no-405 behavior was introduced in commit `452bead0d5a8d56f5e6c8dcda821d0de36a9cda1` on 2026-06-28 and is contained in release tags from 0.7.4 onward. Focused dashboard transport/auth suites passed 70/70 on the current checkout.
- Inference: The reported request was real in the packaged 0.7.1 path, but current source no longer sends legacy `/v1` traffic through a reachable dashboard tunnel. A live SSH target matching the reporter's VM was not available, so a current packaged-build retest is still the appropriate closure gate.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to update Desktop and Agent and retest SSH Auto mode. If it still fails, collect the selected transport, remote dashboard stderr, and accepted WebSocket event log; do not reintroduce an HTTP `/v1` fallback against port 9119. Focused regression check: make `startDashboard` report a reachable dashboard while WebSocket connection fails, then assert three `/api/ws` attempts, a visible turn error, and zero legacy `/v1/chat/completions` calls; separately assert a truly absent dashboard provisions and uses the gateway port.
- Suggested close note: "Current Desktop treats the dashboard and gateway as separate transports: SSH dashboard chat uses `/api/ws`, and a reachable dashboard WebSocket failure no longer falls back to `/v1` on port 9119. Only a genuinely unavailable dashboard uses the separately provisioned, health-checked gateway `/v1` endpoint. Please retest with the latest Desktop and Agent; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with current SSH/dashboard logs if the 405 remains."

Current local chat accumulates accepted `message.delta` events synchronously through `messagesRef`, and `message.complete` reconciles the streamed bubble with its final text (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:931-957,991-1060`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:431-495`). Release 0.7.6 already contains these reconciliation algorithms, and current changes since that tag do not alter `dashboardEventAdapter.ts` or `lossyText.ts`. The current source cannot identify the root cause or a safe CJK-specific change without the actual delta/complete payloads; bypassing lossy reconciliation for all CJK could preserve a corrupted stream or duplicate pre-tool and final segments.
- Recommended action: Request current Desktop/Agent versions and one redacted accepted-event trace containing the ordered `message.delta` and `message.complete` payloads plus the matching final DB message text, ideally for the short example and a table/code case. ## 2026-09-12 — #791 Public remote mode appears incompatible with self hosted OIDC

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/791
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report predates the direct-Remote OAuth implementation and contains no versions, endpoint, identity provider, errors, or comments. Current Remote setup probes public `/api/status`; `auth_required: true` selects OAuth, while a missing dashboard endpoint falls back to bearer-authenticated raw-gateway `/health` detection (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:107-175`). OAuth setup opens the Hermes dashboard `/login` page in a sandboxed persistent Electron partition and recognizes the resulting Hermes access/refresh cookies (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:80-104,354-428`); authenticated management requests reuse that cookie session and chat mints a fresh single-use WebSocket ticket (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:206-351`; `src/main/dashboard.ts:396-470`). The connection IPC performs detection and browser login before committing Remote mode (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1473-1508`). This support landed after the report in the July 14 commits `307b809322ce2f51e04dfdc7e5c719923fe625b5`, `6cc44858e34c109bc327de9f9e25f92365254874`, and `eba5a9f8d422add5f49edb770925edc6ac78235d`. SSH intentionally remains token-based, but current SSH startup provisions the dashboard token or a gateway API key rather than consuming Remote-mode OIDC cookies (`lat.md/main-process.md:69-78,92-104`). OAuth/auth-mode/dashboard focused tests passed as part of the 70/70 run (`tests/remote-auth-mode.test.ts:12-90`; `tests/remote-oauth.test.ts:122-327`; `tests/dashboard-remote.test.ts:149-271`).
- Inference: A self-hosted Hermes dashboard whose `/login` flow delegates to OIDC and returns Hermes session cookies is now supported in direct Remote mode, so the core report appears fixed. This is not generic OIDC-client support for an arbitrary non-Hermes endpoint. The one-line SSH claim is too underspecified to reproduce independently, but its old credential/setup path has also been replaced.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retest direct Remote against the latest Hermes dashboard and clarify the IdP only if `/login` does not end in a Hermes session. Retest SSH separately in Auto mode and provide the remote dashboard log if it fails. Focused regression check: run a self-hosted dashboard fixture whose `/login` redirects through an OIDC provider, assert cookies stay in `persist:hermes-remote-oauth`, management REST succeeds, every WebSocket attempt uses a fresh ticket, and no OAuth cookie/token reaches renderer state.
- Suggested close note: "Direct Remote now detects browser-authenticated Hermes dashboards, opens the dashboard `/login` flow in a persistent sandboxed session, reuses the resulting Hermes session cookies for API calls, and mints one-time WebSocket tickets for chat. SSH remains a separate token-based transport and now provisions its required credentials. Please retest with current Desktop and Agent; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with the self-hosted dashboard URL shape, IdP, and current error if sign-in still fails."

## 2026-09-13 — #953 [Bug]: Links display incorrectly in session titles

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/953
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue identifies Hermes Agent build `45a6101f36576367359c171cd5820ee76a3d047b` (v0.21.1), has no comments, and shows the title exposing the serialized `@url:` reference. In that exact commit, Desktop converts an added URL to `@url:${formatRefValue(url)}` (`apps/desktop/src/app/chat/session-tile.tsx:259-261`; `apps/desktop/src/app/contrib/wiring.tsx:994-997`), and values containing syntax-sensitive characters are quote-wrapped (`apps/desktop/src/components/assistant-ui/directive-text.tsx:157-178,209-214`). The title path does not decode references: `deriveDraftTitle` returns the first raw draft line (`apps/desktop/src/lib/draft-title.ts:18-43`), the backend's `_summarize_user_message` only strips skill/control wrappers (`agent/title_generator.py:142-168`), and the session list falls back to raw `preview` when no title is present (`apps/desktop/src/lib/chat-runtime.ts:51-53`). The local standalone shell also has inconsistent URL handling: its active-tab title slices the raw first message (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:184-195`), while its cache title generator removes URLs entirely (`src/main/session-cache.ts:44-72`). Focused title/Markdown/session-cache suites passed 30/30 but contain no URL-reference title regression.
- Inference: The displayed `@url:` prefix and quote wrapper are a deterministic presentation leak: transport syntax needed for context injection is being reused as human-facing title input. The issue is real in the reported build; whether the screenshot captured the pre-send draft title, instant derived title, or preview fallback does not alter the shared root cause.
- Recommended action: Add one non-mutating display-title sanitizer that parses serialized context references and replaces `@url:<wrapped-value>` with the decoded URL before draft-title derivation, backend derived/LLM title input, and preview display. Do not alter stored message text or the agent prompt. Affected upstream files: `apps/desktop/src/lib/draft-title.ts`, `agent/title_generator.py`, the preview/session-title boundary, and focused tests; keep standalone parity in `src/main/session-cache.ts` and `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx`. Focused regression check: submit `` @url:`https://example.com/a path?x=1` `` as the first turn and assert every draft/sidebar/tab title shows the decoded URL (or a clean semantic title), never `@url:` or wrapper quotes, while persisted content still contains the directive.

## 2026-09-14 — #954 ClarifyCard is unreachable for dashboard/WebSocket sessions — clarify renders as a plain text bubble

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/954
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue targets current main and has no comments. The dashboard adapter handles `clarify.request` by flattening its choices into numbered Markdown and constructing an ordinary local-only agent bubble (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:131-169,702-706`); its regression explicitly expects `role: "agent"` rather than `kind: "clarify"` (`tests/dashboard-event-adapter.test.ts:157-194`). By contrast, the IPC path preserves `requestId`, question, and choices in a `ClarifyMessage` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatIPC.ts:245-270`), which is the only shape `MessageList` sends to `ClarifyCard` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageList.tsx:145-153`). The dashboard transport records the pending request ID and only answers it when the next composer submission calls WebSocket RPC `clarify.respond` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1119-1131,1478-1508`), while `ClarifyCard` is hard-wired to the unrelated main-process IPC resolver (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ClarifyCard.tsx:35-53`; `src/main/ipc/register.ts:1854-1863`; `src/main/hermes.ts:1177-1205`). The focused adapter/card/constants suites passed 54/54, confirming the currently encoded plain-bubble behavior rather than covering a dashboard card response.
- Inference: Dashboard users can type a clarify answer, but they deterministically cannot receive the interactive choice card. Converting only the adapter message would expose a card whose buttons call an IPC resolver that the renderer-owned dashboard request never registered, so rendering and response routing must change together.
- Recommended action: Preserve `clarify.request` as a structured `ClarifyMessage`, make `ClarifyCard` accept a transport-neutral `onRespond(requestId, answer)` callback, and expose a dashboard responder that verifies the pending ID before issuing exactly one `clarify.respond`; retain the composer fallback. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts`, `hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts`, `Chat.tsx`, `MessageList.tsx`, `ClarifyCard.tsx`, and their focused tests. Focused regression check: replay a dashboard clarify event, click a choice, and assert one WebSocket `clarify.respond`, no IPC call, a resolved card, and cleared pending state; retain the IPC-card test.

Live model discovery also has no canonical endpoint for either provider, so an empty UI base URL reaches `unknown-host` (`src/main/provider-registry.ts:15-44`; `src/main/model-discovery.ts:515-579`); URL-to-key mapping lacks `opencode.ai` as well (`src/shared/url-key-map.ts:25-43`). The current Agent registry supports both and defines canonical endpoints as `https://opencode.ai/zen/v1` and `https://opencode.ai/zen/go/v1` (`../hermes-agent/plugins/model-providers/opencode-zen/__init__.py:202-218`; `../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/auth.py:466-485`). The focused suites passed 54/54, but existing coverage only proves the partial env-key routing.
- Inference: A user can now save these keys in Settings and reach their provider route in some keyed-model flows, but first-run setup, credential-pool selection, model discovery, and exact install readiness remain incomplete. The Go URL proposed in the issue is stale; current Agent authority includes `/zen/go/v1`.
- Recommended action: Add both providers to the relevant picker/setup metadata, `PROVIDER_BASE_URLS`, `PROVIDER_ENV_KEYS`, and URL-key mappings using the Agent's current canonical URLs; longer term, replace the duplicated registries with one shared provider descriptor. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/constants.ts`, `src/main/provider-registry.ts`, `src/main/installer.ts`, `src/shared/url-key-map.ts`, and provider/setup/discovery tests. Focused regression check: configure each provider from first-run and Settings, assert its key persists, its model request reaches the correct `/models` endpoint (especially Go's `/zen/go/v1`), and it appears in the credential picker without triggering the install gate.

Many Windows updater fixes landed after the report, but none can be tied to this instance without its captured output or `update.log`.

It is therefore unsafe to claim a root cause or that the old report is fixed.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retry on the latest Desktop/Agent after closing all Hermes CLI/Desktop instances, then attach the complete updater progress output, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\hermes\\logs\\update.log` (or the actual `HERMES_HOME\\logs\\update.log`), Debug Dump, and current versions. If it succeeds, close as stale; if it fails, preserve the failing stage/output in the issue and add a focused regression for that exact Windows path. Separately, improve Desktop diagnostics so a nonzero result includes the captured tail and log path rather than only the exit code.

## 2026-09-15 — #958 [Bug] Clicking config-health "Show details" crashes the renderer to a white screen

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/958
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Baseline: Desktop main at `ba86e0f210525302359efef84987ecb7bcb29b1b`; file references below are relative to `/Users/fathah/Desktop/opensource/hermes/hermes-desktop`.
- Observed evidence: The full issue reports Local/Windows 11/Desktop 0.7.7 and has no comments. `ConfigHealthBanner` types its callback as no-argument but passes it directly to React `onClick`, which supplies the click event (`src/renderer/src/components/ConfigHealthBanner.tsx:15-19,208-215`). Chat forwards the callback unchanged (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:978`), and Layout forwards its runtime argument to `openSettings` as a section (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:892-894`). The provider stores that argument and passes it as `initialSection` (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/SettingsModalProvider.tsx:27-31,47-54`); `resolveSection` immediately calls `.trim()` (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/SettingsModal.tsx:104-111,136-144`). A source-extracted execution of the current resolver with a click-event object reproduced `TypeError`. The Settings modal is a sibling of the supplied children inside its provider, outside the app's existing ErrorBoundary (`SettingsModalProvider.tsx:44-55`; `src/renderer/src/App.tsx:259-283`), explaining root unmount instead of an error fallback. The navigation union/list and render switch also omit Diagnose (`SettingsModal.tsx:28-97,202-213`). Existing banner/composer suites passed 9/9, but banner tests never click Show details.
- Inference: This is a deterministic renderer exception, not a Windows or Local transport fault. The no-argument TypeScript callback type hides the runtime event leak. Preventing the leak alone avoids the crash but still cannot display configuration details because Diagnose is not a modal destination.
- Recommended action: Invoke the banner's no-argument callback through `onClick={() => onOpenDiagnose?.()}` and adapt it in Chat as `onOpenDiagnose={() => onOpenDiagnose?.("diagnose")}`; keep the parameterized callback for slash-command Settings navigation. Add Diagnose to the modal section/navigation/render switch with the existing ConfigHealth screen; defensively reject non-string resolver input and place the Settings modal under an error boundary. Affected files: `ConfigHealthBanner.tsx`, `Chat.tsx`, `SettingsModal.tsx`, `SettingsModalProvider.tsx` or `App.tsx`, and renderer tests. Focused regression check: mount the real provider and banner with a warning, click Show details, assert Diagnose and the warning text appear, the callback receives no event as a section, and an intentionally invalid section cannot unmount the app.

## 2026-09-15 — #654 Desktop chat composer needs recoverable submitted-draft state for prompt integrity

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/654
- Classification: **CONFIRMED** (recoverability/state-audit gap; the reported middle-clause mutation remains unproven)
- Observed evidence: The full issue is from Desktop 0.5.9, explicitly acknowledges uncertain input/history/edit causes, and has no comments. Current 0.7.7 still keeps composer text and attachments only in component state (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx:101-105`), pushes only normalized text into volatile history, clears text/attachments, and invokes a void parent submission callback (`ChatInput.tsx:74,363-378`). Single-line ArrowUp can replace a nonempty draft (`ChatInput.tsx:451-464`); the history hook saves the draft temporarily but `push` erases that saved draft (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useInputHistory.ts:32-60`). A live React renderHook reproduction using the current hook verified ArrowDown restores the unsent draft before sending recalled history, but cannot restore it after that recalled prompt is sent. Current ordinary sends do preserve their full normalized content in a user bubble and pass the same text to the transport (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatActions.ts:143-181,375-383`), so no middle-clause deletion was established. Busy prompts are stored in a separate volatile queue (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:273-274,801-845`) and shown as previews with cancellation but no restore/edit action (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/QueuedMessages.tsx:33-89`). The queue-drain catch intends to restore failed sends, but the lower `sendToAgent` catches and resolves transport rejection (`useChatActions.ts:161-184`), preventing that catch from receiving the failure. Existing banner/composer suites passed 9/9 without draft-recovery coverage.
- Inference: The concrete outstanding defect is inability to recover/audit pre-replacement drafts, submitted attachments, and failed queue transitions, not proven transport corruption. Some text is recoverable through volatile input history or copying the user bubble, so the issue's broad claim of no recovery at all is overstated. Sending recalled text discards the original draft snapshot, and composer unmount/restart loses the remaining volatile history.
- Recommended action: Keep a bounded, run/profile-scoped snapshot of raw draft, normalized submission, and attachments before history/quick-action replacement or clearing, and offer an explicit Restore action without overwriting a newer edited draft. Make parent submission return an accepted/queued/failed outcome; propagate transport failures (after rendering an error) so queued items can be retained/retried rather than silently treated as delivered. Do not change agent prompt content or introduce unbounded/telemetry draft logging. Affected files: `ChatInput.tsx`, `hooks/useInputHistory.ts`, `hooks/useChatActions.ts`, `Chat.tsx`, `QueuedMessages.tsx`, and tests. Focused regression check: type draft A with an attachment, recall/send history B, reject the send, then restore A exactly; repeat for a queued submission and composer remount, verifying the submitted snapshot is immutable and restoration never overwrites later draft C. Request an event/payload trace separately if actual middle-clause mutation still occurs.

## 2026-09-15 — #753 Bug: Chat Message Virtual Scrolling Implementation — Scroll Jumping & Positioning Issues

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/753
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The full issue has no comments and says its experimental absolute-positioned TanStack transcript virtualizer was reverted. Current main's `MessageList` imports no virtualizer, builds ordinary React rows, and returns them directly without an absolute-positioned total-size canvas (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageList.tsx:1-11,94-187`). Chat places those rows and a real bottom sentinel in the scroll container (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:980-995`); `useChatScroll` uses that sentinel and a manual-scroll threshold, not scrollToIndex or measurement observers (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts:14-54`). CSS keeps the container in block flow and rows in normal flow with content-visibility containment (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:4822-4834,4940-4958`). The cited input-lag fix is also present: textarea measurement runs through one input-keyed layout effect instead of a per-keystroke requestAnimationFrame (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx:134-152`), introduced by ancestor commit `3ab39c1f6857d7f33d73e8bc4173e05aaca6317e`. `lat.md/chat-performance.md:1-41` documents this intentional design. The current command palette is separately virtualized; it is not the transcript. Composer/banner tests passed 9/9, but no packaged 200-message wheel/resume test was performed.
- Inference: The specific estimated-height/ResizeObserver transcript implementation described in this report is absent, consistent with the reported revert. This does not prove every possible scroll issue is fixed, nor support the report's blanket claim that all virtualized chat implementations are fundamentally incompatible with auto-scroll.
- Recommended action: Retest a current packaged build with 200+ variable-height messages: resume at the latest message, scroll upward during streaming without being snapped back, and return to bottom to re-enable following. If that passes, close this implementation-specific report; keep any surviving scroll defect as a separate reproducible issue with versions and a recording.
- Suggested close note: "Current main renders transcript rows in normal document flow with content-visibility, a bottom sentinel, and a manual-scroll guard; the absolute-positioned TanStack transcript virtualizer and its measurement observer are absent. The composer resize fix is also present in `3ab39c1f`. Please retest resume and wheel scrolling on the latest build; closing this reverted-implementation report, and we can reopen with a current reproduction if the same behavior persists."

## 2026-09-16 — #752 Bug: "session busy" Red Toast on Resuming Sessions After Restart, Previously Caused Session Pollution

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/752
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The issue and follow-up discussion distinguish opening a stored session from the first send after reopening. Current Agent `session.resume` does not reject a live running session: it reuses the live runtime and returns its `running`/`status` payload (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/methods_session.py:356-495`), so the issue's original gateway-side claim is no longer true for resume itself. Desktop also only creates on a literal `session not found`; other resume errors propagate (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:228-278`; `tests/dashboard-chat-transport.test.ts:455-610`). A remaining deterministic pollution path exists during the first send: after restart the applied-model cache is empty, Desktop may execute `/model`; Agent returns `session busy` for a mutating slash command on a running session (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:14188-14210`), and if the live model differs from the selected Desktop model the subsequent verification throws (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1339-1462`). The generic send catch marks every such error as requiring runtime recreation (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1661-1665`); the next send closes that live runtime, force-creates a seeded session, and replaces the durable session id (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1600-1635,1266-1315`). The focused suites passed 91/91, but they cover non-missing resume errors and explicit force-create separately, not this busy model-sync sequence.
- Inference: The exact click-to-resume toast is not reproducible against the current Agent, but the reporter's clarified first-send/session-scattering symptom remains likely when a resumed turn is still running and Desktop wants to change its model. Blindly interrupting a genuinely live turn, as the issue proposes, risks losing valid work.
- Recommended action: Preserve the runtime and durable ids for transient `session busy`/4009 failures; do not set `recreateRuntimeSessionRef` for that class. Wait for the running turn to complete (or present an explicit user-controlled interrupt), then retry model synchronization and the prompt. Reserve replacement-session recovery for missing or demonstrably unusable sessions. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts` and its focused hook/transport tests. Regression check: resume a live `running: true` session whose model differs, make `slash.exec` report busy, and assert no `session.close` or `session.create`, the stored id is unchanged, and a retry after completion synchronizes the model and submits once.

## 2026-09-16 — #716 Desktop chat never sends to gateway: 0 TCP connections, 0 aiohttp.access rows, perpetual loading state

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/716
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report is from Desktop 0.6.1 on Windows 10 and has no comments. Current local Auto chat uses the dashboard WebSocket transport by default (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:200-205,276-281`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:142-160`), a path introduced after the report by commit `c5067689d6d4314473a01aae67140e2f445f5574` and included from 0.7.4 beta onward. Its WebSocket handshake is bounded at 10 seconds and all RPCs at 30 seconds (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardGatewayClient.ts:41-42,93-168`); connection setup retries three times and then rejects visibly (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1146-1246`), while the send path converts that rejection into a failed turn and clears loading (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1541-1570,1579-1598`). If Auto falls back to legacy, the main-process health probe is bounded at 1.5 seconds and send either recovers the gateway/API or starts the CLI fallback (`src/main/hermes.ts:1069-1099,3052-3135`). Focused dashboard, legacy recovery, readiness, and banner suites passed 91/91, including stopped/unhealthy gateway, stale cache, socket reset, and accepted-timeout cases (`tests/hermes-cli-session-id.test.ts:432-678`).
- Inference: The v0.6.1 path that could leave this report with neither a request nor a terminal UI event has been replaced for default local chat, and both current transports now have bounded failure/recovery paths. A packaged Windows 10 reproduction was not available, so current-build confirmation is still warranted before treating this as proven fixed in every environment.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retest current Desktop/Agent in local Auto mode. If it still hangs, collect renderer console output, the selected transport, dashboard WebSocket frames, and the `send-message` IPC start/result rather than only gateway access logs. Regression check: simulate a WebSocket that never opens and a dead legacy API; assert the turn leaves loading within the configured deadline with a visible error or successful CLI fallback.
- Suggested close note: "Current local Auto chat now uses a bounded dashboard WebSocket transport, and its legacy fallback has bounded health checks plus gateway recovery/CLI fallback. Connection and RPC failures clear the loading state instead of spinning indefinitely, with focused recovery tests passing on current main. Please retest on the latest Desktop/Agent; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with renderer/IPC and WebSocket diagnostics if it persists on Windows."
