- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/965
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports four Windows reproductions where read-only `computer_use` actions work but click/type/key/drag/scroll wait exactly 300 seconds and fail closed. Hermes Agent registers a per-session approval notifier and emits `approval.request` (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:1942-2006,2434-2444,7325-7329`), retains the request for reconnect replay (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:9029-9036`), accepts a correlated `approval.respond` (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/methods_prompt.py:1521-1539`), and waits on the request for the configured 300-second default (`../hermes-agent/tools/approval.py:2589-2606,3221-3232,4235-4328`). Current Dashboard chat's accepted-event handler has special handling for `session.info`, `background.complete`, completion, and `clarify.request`, but none for `approval.request` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:991-1133`); its event adapter likewise has no approval case and drops unknown events unchanged (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:641-731`). The separate main-process gateway path is not a safe substitute: it silently answers every approval with `choice: "once"` (`src/main/hermes.ts:2187-2210`), while the Runs path abandons the run because the renderer cannot show a mid-stream prompt (`src/main/hermes.ts:1906-1912`). The 104 focused transport, adapter, provider, and dashboard tests pass, but none exercises a structured approval request on this current checkout.
- Inference: The exact five-minute failure is the Agent's intended fail-closed timeout after Desktop receives but ignores the structured event; it is not a `computer_use` backend failure. The lack of a rendered prompt and correlated response is also a security boundary: fixing availability by auto-approving would silently authorize user-visible input.
- Recommended action: Add pending-approval state to the Dashboard transport, normalize and render a mid-stream approval card, acknowledge/replay by `request_id`, and send exactly one correlated `approval.respond` for once/session/always/deny. Clear or deny pending requests on turn cancellation, disconnect, and session replacement; remove the main-process unconditional `once` response. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts`, the Chat approval UI/types, and the legacy main-process transport in `src/main/hermes.ts`. Focused regression check: inject a `computer_use` click `approval.request`, assert the prompt appears and no response is sent before interaction, then choose once and assert exactly one matching `approval.respond` resumes the tool; repeat with deny, reconnect replay, and timeout/cancel cleanup.

## 2026-09-18 — #966 Custom LLM provider disappears from Configure screen (replaced by a phantom "Hermes One — key set" card) after a session hits a mismatched provider/model 404

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/966
- Classification: **NEEDS INFO**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and says the custom provider files and key remain after a mismatched per-session provider/model request returns 404, yet the card vanishes and a Hermes One card appears. Current provider cards are derived from three independent durable sources: valid `providers.json` records, custom rows in `models.json`, and nonempty `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_*_KEY` environment entries (`src/renderer/src/components/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:519-629`); custom cards render independently of the selected session model (`src/renderer/src/components/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:680-730`). A `providers.json` record is silently excluded unless it has string `id`, `name`, `baseUrl`, and numeric `createdAt`, and malformed/version-mismatched files silently degrade to an empty list (`src/main/providers-store.ts:45-70`). Conversely, a Hermes One card cannot be synthesized by a session override: configured branded cards require a nonempty environment key (`src/renderer/src/components/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:544-565,683-710`). A persisted signed-in account intentionally provisions `HERMESONE_API_KEY` when Providers opens and reloads the environment (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:192-236`; `src/main/hermesone-provision.ts:53-121`), and login also triggers provisioning (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:971-1017`). The focused provider-store and provisioning suites pass, including profile isolation and Hermes One mirroring (`src/main/providers-store.test.ts:29-225`; `src/main/hermesone-provision.test.ts:60-158`).
- Inference: The reported 404/session override has no data path that replaces provider cards. The two UI changes more likely have separate causes: Hermes One was auto-provisioned for a signed-in account, while the custom provider was read from a different active profile/home or filtered because its full record is invalid/legacy. Re-adding it would create fresh `id`/`createdAt` metadata, which is consistent with the latter, but the issue only summarizes the JSON rather than supplying its complete redacted shape and resolved paths, so the root cause is not established.
- Recommended action: Request the active profile and resolved absolute Hermes home, `.env`, `providers.json`, and `models.json` paths; the full redacted provider record including `version`, `id`, and `createdAt`; signed-in account state; the `list-custom-providers` IPC result/renderer error; and environment key names returned after the screen opens (never values). Do not change session-override routing based on this report. If invalid legacy records are confirmed, migrate missing metadata rather than silently dropping records and surface a config-health warning; also label automatically provisioned Hermes One credentials with their account provenance. Focused regression check: open Providers while signed in with a legacy custom record and mismatched session override, then assert the record is migrated/visible, Hermes One is separately identified, and the session failure mutates neither card.

## 2026-09-18 — #967 Backend dashboard process (hermes_cli.main dashboard) survives app quit/relaunch, causing permanently stale in-process caches (e.g. Chromium detection)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/967
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and includes a Windows process-list reproduction: Electron exits, but the `pythonw.exe -m hermes_cli.main dashboard` process and its slash worker remain across relaunch; killing the old tree makes Chromium detection refresh. Current Desktop spawns the local dashboard as a nondetached child (`src/main/dashboard.ts:617-684`) but shutdown removes it from the managed map and calls only `managed.proc.kill()` without awaiting exit, checking the result, escalating, or terminating descendants (`src/main/dashboard.ts:742-759`). `before-quit` invokes that synchronous fire-and-forget cleanup and immediately continues app teardown (`src/main/app/start.ts:115-130`). Failed startup probing uses the same immediate single-child kill (`src/main/dashboard.ts:713-725`). The repository already demonstrates the necessary Windows primitive elsewhere: Claw3D uses `taskkill /F /T /PID` to terminate a process tree, falling back to `SIGKILL` (`src/main/claw3d.ts:816-835`). The stop implementation has been unchanged since `5225dd861`; the only dashboard launch test asserts argv and there is no stop/tree/quit regression (`tests/dashboard-launch.test.ts:1-34`).
- Inference: The reported orphan is a direct consequence of the lifecycle gap. On Windows, a single non-awaited `ChildProcess.kill()` does not establish that the Python process tree has exited before Electron quits, and descendants may outlive the parent; stale module caches then persist because the old interpreter remains resident.
- Recommended action: Centralize process-tree termination and use Windows `taskkill /T` (graceful first where possible, bounded forced escalation) plus an equivalent process-group/descendant strategy on POSIX. Make dashboard stopping asynchronous and idempotent, keep entries until exit is confirmed, and gate the final app exit with a one-shot `before-quit` latch that awaits cleanup without recursing. Apply the same helper to failed readiness probes. Affected files: `src/main/dashboard.ts`, `src/main/app/start.ts`, a shared process-lifecycle helper, and focused lifecycle tests. Regression check: spawn a fixture dashboard parent with a long-lived child, invoke app quit, await the bounded cleanup, and assert both PIDs are dead and the next launch can bind the same port/use freshly evaluated detection state; also cover already-exited and forced-escalation paths.

## 2026-09-19 — #779 PowerShell terminal window briefly opens and closes while Hermes Desktop is running on Windows 11

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/779
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The issue and three independent confirmations describe an idle/every-few-minutes Windows 11 console flash that steals focus, but do not include Desktop/Agent versions or a Process Create record identifying the executable, command line, or parent PID. Current Desktop launches its Python worker through the GUI-subsystem `pythonw.exe` and documents the console-allocation race (`src/main/installer.ts:181-194`); its verification/version subprocesses also use the shared `windowsHide: true` option (`src/main/installer.ts:573-604,607-660`; `src/main/process-options.ts:1-6`). More importantly, post-report Hermes Agent commit `cb982ad997c5e04c6b647c4cbb3d1b020ec383fb` (first tagged `v2026.7.1`) added `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` to backend git/gh/wmic/bash/PowerShell-related helper spawns, and commits `5c5960d9f93ad4ddb3abb224ef1a2cb2faed0649` and `0fb0ba475dee759999fe665a7c59eedba7932be3` (first tagged `v2026.7.30`) covered environment probes, lazy installs, and bootstrap/platform probes. Current Agent centralizes the flag and explains why a hidden console inherited by descendants prevents git/gh/cmd/node/wmic/PowerShell flashes (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/_subprocess_compat.py:139-160,250-267`); it also neutralizes CPython's hidden `cmd /c ver` probe before heavyweight imports (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/_subprocess_compat.py:270-304`; `../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:64-72`). Focused Desktop platform/process tests passed in the 13/13 run.
- Inference: The original runtime symptom was credible, but the exact old spawn cannot be proven without process metadata. Current Desktop and Agent now address both the top-level Python console and the descendant/helper console-allocation paths that match the periodic focus-stealing report, so it appears fixed after the report.
- Recommended action: Ask reporters to update both Desktop and Agent and leave the app idle for the same reproduction interval. If a flash remains, capture a Windows Process Monitor Process Create event containing image path, command line, and parent PID before changing any more flags. Suggested close note: "Current Desktop starts the Agent with `pythonw.exe` and hidden subprocess options, and post-report Agent releases added `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` across background probes/watchdogs plus removed the hidden `cmd /c ver` platform probe. Please update both components and retest; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a Procmon Process Create record if any window still flashes."

## 2026-09-19 — #764 [Bug] Severe UI lag after opening 3D rendered "Office" tab

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/764
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue reports Desktop v0.6.35 on Windows 11 with severe lag that begins in Office and persists after navigating away; it has no comments. Current Layout lazy-mounts each visited view once and then hides it with `display: none` instead of unmounting it (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:265-289`), including Office (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:959-963`). `Office` passes `visible` to pause its status polling, but always renders `Office3D` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx:202-221,261-267,601-619`). `Office3D` accepts no visibility/active prop and its Canvas specifies no `frameloop` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx:166-211,424-439`); the installed React Three Fiber build defaults that setting to `always` (`node_modules/@react-three/fiber/dist/events-b389eeca.esm.js:1019,15603-15714`). The mounted scene contains continuous `useFrame` simulations for traffic, pedestrians, and agents (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Traffic.tsx:543-566`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Pedestrians.tsx:176-205`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/AgentsLayer.tsx:284-310`). The focused Office status-polling suite passes, but it does not assert that WebGL rendering stops while hidden.
- Inference: Hiding the container does not stop the still-mounted R3F request-animation-frame loop or its scene simulations, so renderer/GPU work continues after leaving Office and directly explains the persistent application-wide lag. This is independently confirmed by the current source even though the persistent visited-view implementation was later revised in commit `3eaea197f`.
- Recommended action: Give `Office3D` an explicit active state and stop its frame loop and input handlers whenever `visible` is false; conditionally unmounting `Office3D` from `Office` is the smallest robust option if releasing the WebGL context/resources is preferred over instant return. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx`, and a focused Office lifecycle test. Regression check: visit Office, switch to Chat, and assert Canvas frame callbacks stop (and the WebGL context is disposed if unmounted), then switch back and assert exactly one render loop resumes without duplicate listeners.

## 2026-09-19 — #836 Mac端不能连接Hermes v0.18.0版本

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/836
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments and says a Mac Desktop cannot connect over LAN to Hermes Agent v0.18.0 in a NAS container; it correctly suspects that the legacy connection validator should probe `/health` rather than `/`. Current Remote setup first probes dashboard `/api/status`, then falls back on 404/405 to authenticated raw-gateway `/health`, treating 200/401/403 as evidence of token mode (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:107-175`). The connection flow then validates token mode with `testRemoteConnection`, which targets normalized `/health` and sends the bearer key (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1476-1511`; `src/main/hermes.ts:3567-3599`); URL normalization strips a trailing slash or `/v1` (`src/main/hermes.ts:111-128`). Remote chat waits on the same health path and sends messages to `/v1/chat/completions` (`src/main/hermes.ts:1069-1099,1472-1510,3052-3081`). Commit `5e7ce0c6f117385c5c12ba9eb55d21aef4966a20` added the raw `/health` fallback after the report and is contained in v0.7.7; its regression covers a supplied URL ending in `/v1` plus bearer authentication (`tests/remote-auth-mode.test.ts:45-76`). The focused remote-auth test passed as part of the 13/13 run.
- Inference: The exact API-server validation mismatch described by the reporter is removed in current Desktop. A live Mac-to-NAS deployment matching the reporter's container/network was not available, so closure should still include a current-version retest.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to update Desktop and Agent, enter the NAS base URL with or without `/v1`, and retest. Suggested close note: "Current Desktop detects raw Hermes Agent API servers by falling back from unsupported `/api/status` to authenticated `/health`, normalizes URLs ending in `/v1`, and then uses `/v1/chat/completions`. This landed in `5e7ce0c6` and is included in v0.7.7. Please update and retest the NAS URL; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with the current URL shape and returned status codes if it persists."

## 2026-09-20 — #777 GitHub Copilot (ACP) Provider Cannot Authenticate— relies on internal GitHub Agent APIs not available publicly

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/777
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The detailed report has no comments. Its broad architectural claim is stale: current Agent distinguishes direct `copilot` (public GitHub token/API) from `copilot-acp` (external process), resolves the direct provider from supported env tokens or `gh auth token`, and invokes the public `@github/copilot` CLI as `copilot --acp --stdio` (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/auth.py:293-307,7202-7229,7425-7462`; `../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/copilot_auth.py:1-17,75-121,165-221`; `../hermes-agent/agent/copilot_acp_client.py:62-110,568-601`). The specific Desktop setup defect nevertheless remains in the current checkout: the Accounts catalog tells users to run invalid `copilot /login` and its dedicated status function always returns `logged_in: false` (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/web_server.py:10606-10620,10685-10692`). The current onboarding treats ACP as an external flow, displays that command, and offers an enabled recheck button (`../hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/store/onboarding.ts:604-611,741-769`; `../hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/components/onboarding/flow.tsx:99-113`), so `gh auth login` alone does not configure the ACP subprocess and the UI's own suggested command cannot complete CLI login. Fix commits `323168a2896e5bdab1bc258f8ea06d7fa0ca8ed2` and `fd439ac1b86018508fbf41e01d819be683750746` correct the command/status on another release line, but neither is an ancestor of the current Agent HEAD `503d863f`; the present source has regressed to the old catalog. The 128 focused Agent tests pass, but no test asserts the catalog command or `_copilot_acp_status` mapping.
- Inference: Public Copilot/ACP support itself is viable; the reproducible failure is a stale Desktop-facing adapter that conflates GitHub CLI login with Copilot CLI login and hardcodes a disconnected card. Users following the current instructions remain stuck even though the runtime can launch a correctly installed and authenticated public Copilot CLI.
- Recommended action: Port the focused behavior from `323168a289` and `fd439ac1b8`: use the resolved external-process command with `login` (not `/login`), derive the card from positive auth evidence without claiming that an unverifiable OS-keychain session is signed out, and preserve the existing model-reachability recheck. Smallest affected files are `../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/web_server.py`, `../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/auth.py`, and focused backend/catalog tests; the current Desktop external-flow UI can remain unchanged. Regression check: mock a public `copilot` executable and positive credential evidence, request `/api/providers/oauth`, assert a copyable `copilot login` command and connected status, then assert the recheck selects `copilot-acp`; also cover installed-but-unverified and missing-CLI states.

## 2026-09-20 — #770 Hermes One crashes on Ubuntu 24.04 unless launched with --no-sandbox.

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/770
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports Ubuntu 24.04/Wayland/NVIDIA with AppArmor restricting unprivileged user namespaces; it does not identify the Desktop version or package format. Current `.deb`/`.rpm` packaging runs an after-install hook that makes Electron's `chrome-sandbox` root-owned SUID (`electron-builder.yml:58-88`; `build/linux-after-install.sh:1-29`), preserving Chromium sandboxing instead of adding the insecure `--no-sandbox` workaround. That hook landed before the report in `48f810f5c6c6d95ef092a58bf7b6c61efe7d35b2` and is contained in releases from v0.5.2 onward. The report's exact Chromium zygote `Invalid argument (22)` failure also matches the later diagnosed whitespace install-path defect: current packaging uses `/opt/HermesOne` rather than `/opt/Hermes One` (`electron-builder.yml:1-11`), fixed by post-report commit `8f1cd21fac50a3aa293b2acc890e5cc42e471d90`. The packaging regression calculates `/opt/HermesOne` and verifies the sandbox hook targets the same directory (`tests/packaging-identity.test.ts:21-46`); all 4 focused tests pass. The path fix is in current main but no current repository tag contains it yet.
- Inference: For the installed `.deb`/`.rpm` path, current source addresses both relevant launch blockers—the hardened-host sandbox helper and the exact EINVAL-producing space in `argv[0]`—so the report appears fixed. Because the reporter omitted package type, static evidence cannot establish AppImage or Snap behavior, and a packaged Ubuntu 24.04 retest is still required before unconditional closure.
- Recommended action: Publish/test a build containing `8f1cd21f`, install the `.deb` on Ubuntu 24.04 with `apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`, verify `/opt/HermesOne/chrome-sandbox` is root-owned mode 4755, and launch without flags. If successful, close; if not, request the exact package format, installed path, `stat` output for `chrome-sandbox`, and full zygote stderr. Suggested close note: "Current Linux packaging removes the whitespace from the installed executable path and configures Electron's SUID sandbox helper for hardened Ubuntu hosts, so `--no-sandbox` should no longer be required. Please retest a build containing `8f1cd21f` using the Ubuntu package you originally installed; closing as apparently fixed for `.deb`/`.rpm`, and we can reopen with the package format and `chrome-sandbox` permissions if it persists."

## 2026-09-21 — #979 can't list the agents on cloud

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/979
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports two related behaviors: cloud agents cannot be listed/managed, and deleting their local profiles lets them return. The checked-out `main` is 15 commits behind `origin/main`; at checkout commit `ba86e0f2`, sync fetches the complete cloud list but exposes it only as reconciliation input (`src/main/agent-sync.ts:389-415`), automatically materializes every unclaimed cloud agent as a local profile (`src/main/agent-sync.ts:636-667`), and the local delete IPC invokes only the Agent CLI (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2480-2485`; `src/main/profiles.ts:328-358`). The renderer likewise lists only `listProfiles()` results (`src/renderer/src/screens/Agents/Agents.tsx:55-59`) and its cloud pane can show only one local profile's linked id and trigger app-wide sync (`src/renderer/src/components/profile/ProfileSyncPane.tsx:38-72,104-156`); the cloud-sync IPC surface contains run, status, and linked-id, but no cloud inventory or delete operation (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1021-1036`). The resurrection half was fixed after this checkout by `86c0b1d30144eb5f7ad7b755a8b2d8b7e9218b54` (now an ancestor of `origin/main`): it persists an account/backend-scoped cloud-id exclusion before local deletion and skips excluded ids during name matching/import (`src/main/agent-sync.ts` at that commit, lines 355-456, 662-684, 830-880), with restart and failure regressions in `src/main/agent-sync.test.ts`. That fix intentionally retains the cloud copy and still makes no backend DELETE request (`lat.md/agent-sync.md` at `origin/main`:31-41). The focused sync/UI-adjacent suites passed in today's 114-test run.
- Inference: Local-profile reappearance is deterministic in the checked-out source and should stop once the `86c0b1d3` change reaches the user's build, but the broader inability to inspect or explicitly delete cloud agents remains real even on `origin/main`. The design currently collapses remote inventory into local profiles and deliberately treats local deletion as device-only; there is no Desktop operation representing an intentional cloud deletion.
- Recommended action: Keep device-local deletion and its exclusion semantics, but add a separately named, explicitly confirmed cloud-management path: authenticated/account-scoped cloud-agent listing plus `DELETE /api/agents/:id`, exposed through typed IPC/preload APIs and a cloud inventory in the Agents UI. After a cloud delete, keep an existing local profile as local-only and clear only the matching link; never infer remote deletion from local deletion. Smallest affected surface: `src/main/agent-sync.ts`, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, `src/preload/index.ts`/`index.d.ts`, shared agent-sync types, `src/renderer/src/screens/Agents/Agents.tsx`, and focused tests. Regression check: show a cloud-only agent without importing it as the sole way to inspect it, verify local-only delete does not re-import after restart, then explicitly delete the remote and assert one account-scoped DELETE, no later re-import, and no deletion of an unrelated local profile.

## 2026-09-21 — #667 SSH Tunnel mode: chat messages not sent in v0.6.1 Windows

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/667
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report is specifically Desktop v0.6.1 on Windows with remote Agent v0.16.0 and correctly identifies the old mismatch: the desktop attempted dashboard `/api/ws` through a tunnel targeting the gateway api_server on port 8642, where only `/v1` exists. Merged fix `bbc411d82bcc97ccc998c24205e26f92d5c6d7d7` / PR merge `1352d8cc2260c311d9c7ba464119c8fd4aff329b` landed on 2026-06-17 and is contained in release tags from v0.6.2 onward. Current code explicitly separates the transports: dashboard port 9119 serves `/api/*` and `/api/ws`, while gateway port 8642 serves `/v1` (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2525-2538`); `prepareSshTunnel` starts/tunnels the authenticated dashboard when available or provisions `API_SERVER_KEY` plus `API_SERVER_ENABLED` and tunnels the gateway only for the legacy fallback (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:531-586`). Renderer chat uses `/api/ws` while dashboard is available, retries transient tunnel/WS failures, and refuses to send `/v1` through that dashboard tunnel; only a genuinely unavailable dashboard falls back (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1146-1246,1579-1598`). The issue-specific tests assert sticky fast fallback and connection-change retry (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.test.tsx:469-570`), while the SSH URL regression asserts the tunnel builds `/api/ws` with the dashboard token (`tests/dashboard-remote.test.ts:310-340`). All 114 selected transport/sync/run tests passed; the localhost dashboard cases initially hit sandbox `listen EPERM` and passed 16/16 when rerun with localhost binding permitted.
- Inference: The precise v0.6.1 transport mismatch is removed. The latest commenter’s manual 9119 forwarding workaround matches the current automatic dashboard path, while a blanket same-port fallback to `/v1` would be wrong because the dashboard is not a `/v1` server. A live Windows-to-remote-host retest was not available, so this is source, history, and regression evidence rather than an end-to-end reproduction.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retest with Desktop v0.6.2 or newer (preferably current) and a current Agent. Close if chat either connects through the dashboard or clearly falls back to the provisioned gateway path. Suggested close note: "This v0.6.1 SSH transport mismatch was fixed in `bbc411d8` / merged PR #714 and shipped from v0.6.2 onward. Current Desktop tunnels the dashboard for `/api/ws` and uses a separately provisioned gateway tunnel for `/v1` only when the dashboard is unavailable. Focused regressions pass; please retest on the latest Desktop/Agent, and reopen with current versions and SSH/dashboard logs if sends still stall."

## 2026-09-21 — #719 new sessions are automatically created bug

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/719
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The v0.6.2 AOSC OS/Wayland report says each question creates another same-content session, some cannot be deleted, and explicitly links the related #752 session-pollution report. Current UI protects the ordinary “open saved session twice” path with an exact connection/profile/session lookup plus an in-flight resume guard (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:627-665`), and current dashboard resume creates a replacement only after a literal `session not found`; other resume errors propagate (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:228-278`; `tests/dashboard-chat-transport.test.ts:455-610`). A narrower current pollution path remains: a reopened live session can be reused by Agent (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/methods_session.py:356-495`), but Desktop's empty model-application cache may run a model-mutating slash command; Agent rejects that command while the session is running with `session busy` (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:14188-14210`). Desktop's generic send catch marks every error as requiring recreation (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1661-1665`), so the next question closes the runtime, force-creates a transcript-seeded session, and replaces the durable id (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1600-1635,1266-1315`). A failed completion does the same (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1062-1066`). The focused 114-test run passed, but existing tests cover non-missing resume errors and explicit force-create independently, not the busy-model-sync-to-next-send sequence.
- Inference: The exact historical “every question” frequency is not proven on current builds, but the report's same-content session proliferation is consistent with the still-broad recovery policy already implicated by #752. Any transient first-send or completion error can arm a replacement on the following question even when the durable session is healthy; repeated transient errors can therefore keep scattering the conversation.
- Recommended action: Classify transport/model/session errors before recovery. Preserve runtime and durable ids for `session busy`/4009 and other transient failures, wait for completion or offer an explicit interrupt, then retry model synchronization and submit once. Reserve close-and-seeded-recreate for missing or demonstrably unusable sessions, and do not replace ids after an ordinary failed completion. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts` and its focused hook/transport tests. Regression check: resume a live running session with a model mismatch, return 4009 from `slash.exec`, send again after the turn settles, and assert no `session.close`/`session.create`, one durable session id, and exactly one submitted follow-up; separately prove a true 4007 still creates one seeded replacement.

## 2026-09-22 — #980 Bug New session never shows up in sessions until first prompt is finished by agent.

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/980
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The new-chat runtime id is published as soon as `session.create` returns (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1266-1315`), and `Chat` immediately reports it to `Layout` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:167-183`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:246-254`). The sidebar reacts to that id with a throttled cache sync (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/SidebarRecentSessions.tsx:314-330,443-448`), but its opening sync has just started the five-second throttle window (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/SidebarRecentSessions.tsx:360-395`) and the cache contains only rows already present in `state.db` (`src/main/session-cache.ts:125-215`). Current Agent deliberately creates the durable session row only inside `prompt.submit`, after `session.create`, so untouched drafts stay absent (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/methods_prompt.py:742-769`; `../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:2926-3059`). Desktop receives the successful `prompt.submit` acknowledgement at `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1647-1660` but does not invalidate the sidebar then. The only later refreshes are focus, profile change, context-folder change, or the 60-second timer (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/SidebarRecentSessions.tsx:397-421,450-457`). The issue has no comments. The focused renderer suites passed 16/16, but no test covers the session-create / first-submit / sidebar-cache race.
- Inference: The report's workaround is explained by the forced profile-change refresh. The first id-driven refresh can either be suppressed by the throttle or race ahead of the first durable row; because the successful first submit has no matching invalidation, the new session remains absent until an unrelated refresh. Agent completion may coincide with that later refresh, but completion itself is not the persistence boundary.
- Recommended action: After a successful first `prompt.submit` acknowledgement, emit a dedicated durable-session-created event containing connection/profile/session id and have `SidebarRecentSessions` force one cache sync for the matching scope. Do not create the row at draft/session-create time, which would regress the intentional no-empty-session behavior. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx` or `src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/SidebarRecentSessions.tsx`, and a focused renderer regression. Regression check: start with a recent sidebar sync, return a session id before the DB row exists, make the row visible when first `prompt.submit` resolves, and assert it appears immediately while the agent is still streaming; also assert an untouched draft remains absent.

If it remains immediate, close; otherwise attach a current Chromium Performance trace distinguishing layout/paint from React/Markdown work. ## 2026-09-23 — #666 Auto-scroll in agent chat stopped working after latest update

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/666
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The Windows report has no comments. Current chat still uses `useChatScroll(messages)` and places its bottom sentinel after the rendered transcript (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:347,980-996`). The hook calls `scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" })` whenever the message array changes, but its ordinary `scroll` listener treats every intermediate offset more than 60px from the bottom as a manual user scroll and permanently sets `userScrolledUpRef` until another user message is appended (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts:15-51`). Local dashboard streaming creates a new message array for each assistant/reasoning/tool event (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1038-1060`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:641-690`), so a programmatic smooth-scroll event can disable follow mode during the same response. The hook also observes message state only, not actual rendered-height changes; there is no `ResizeObserver`, so late image/layout growth or expanding live tool content can leave the bottom behind without another scroll trigger. Closely related open report #639 independently identifies that same missing height-based bottom pin. The hook is unchanged from commit `0b99243cf1fc7f344c889ea8339c25955d99df3c`; the 15 commits on `origin/main` add an instant jump for initial history loading but retain smooth live scrolling and no height observer. The 21 focused renderer tests pass, but there is no `useChatScroll` regression.
- Inference: The report's lost follow behavior is explained by two current deterministic gaps: programmatic smooth scrolling is indistinguishable from manual upward scrolling, and following is keyed to React message snapshots rather than rendered content height. The exact trigger depends on response height/timing, which explains why short replies may still appear correct.
- Recommended action: Make bottom pinning explicit in `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts`: while pinned, use an immediate container-bottom assignment for live growth and keep a `ResizeObserver` on transcript content/bottom-sentinel geometry; relinquish the pin only on genuine user scroll intent, not on the hook's own programmatic scroll events. Preserve the existing rule that a new user message re-engages follow mode. Pass streaming state/content ownership from `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx` only if needed, and add a dedicated hook test. Focused regression check: begin at bottom, stream text into the same assistant row and separately simulate late height growth with unchanged message count, asserting the viewport remains pinned; then manually scroll up and assert more growth does not jump, and finally send a user message and assert pinning resumes.

## 2026-09-24 — #638 Queued mid-turn prompts lose context instead of staying anchored to active tool output

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/638
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The detailed v0.5.9 report has no comments, and the same behavior remains at current Desktop HEAD `ba86e0f210525302359efef84987ecb7bcb29b1b`. A plain prompt submitted while `isLoading` is true is stored only as `{ text, attachments }` in `queueRef`; no transcript id, turn id, timestamp, or visible-boundary anchor is captured (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:749-757,820-847`). The queue indicator is rendered separately above the composer, outside the transcript (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:1011-1015`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/QueuedMessages.tsx:16-94`). Only after `isLoading` becomes false does the drain remove the item and call the ordinary send path (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:801-813`), which then appends the user bubble at the current transcript tail (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatActions.ts:143-157,375-383`). Commit `14cafa9569a4d7273fa5d723a274aaefc5f3e662` contains an anchoring implementation on `remotes/github-desktop-LacBang/fix/queue-turn-rendering`, but it is an ancestor of neither this checkout nor `origin/main`, and there is no queue-anchor regression in the current tests. The nearby slash/reconciliation suites passed in the focused 47/47 run.
- Inference: The queued prompt's original visual relationship is irrecoverably lost because the only durable renderer state is FIFO order; tool/reasoning/assistant rows can continue to accumulate before the prompt becomes a normal tail user message. The composer indicator tells the user that something is pending but cannot make the final transcript accurately show when or why it was submitted.
- Recommended action: Capture a stable queued-message id and a renderer-only anchor (`afterMessageId`/index plus active turn id and sequence) at enqueue time. Render a cancellable pending marker at that boundary without inserting it into canonical backend history; when FIFO drain succeeds, carry the anchor onto the optimistic user bubble and preserve its visual position through DB reconciliation, while keeping backend order unchanged. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/types.ts`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/QueuedMessages.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageList.tsx`, a small queue-anchoring helper, and focused renderer tests. Regression check: during a tool-heavy turn, queue two prompts at different visible boundaries, continue streaming rows, and assert both markers remain at their captured positions; then finish the turn and assert FIFO delivery, stable anchored placement, cancellation, retry-on-send-failure, and no duplicate/canonical DB messages.

## 2026-09-24 — #712 Windows curl always pop windows

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/712
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments, version, command line, or parent-process evidence; it only says curl always opens windows. Current Desktop launches the Agent through GUI-subsystem `pythonw.exe`, specifically to prevent console allocation before hidden-process flags take effect (`src/main/installer.ts:181-194`). Current Agent's local terminal path starts Git Bash with `creationflags=windows_hide_flags()` (`../hermes-agent/tools/environments/local.py:1790-1838`), and the shared spawn helper applies the same flag to bash-backed commands (`../hermes-agent/tools/environments/base.py:364-384`). On Windows that helper is exactly `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`; its documented purpose is to give the backend a hidden console inherited by console-subsystem descendants such as curl, git, cmd, and PowerShell (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/_subprocess_compat.py:139-160,250-267`). These protections landed after the report in the Windows console-hardening series, including `e7d2f0b93ca29b2cd95d6623fe8af7646173a75b` and auxiliary-spawn coverage in `cb982ad997c5e04c6b647c4cbb3d1b020ec383fb`, first contained in Agent tag `v2026.7.1`. Seven platform-independent hidden-spawn regressions passed and three Windows-only assertions were skipped on this macOS host; the Desktop focused run also passed 47/47.
- Inference: A curl command launched through the current local terminal inherits a hidden console and should no longer create the visible window reported in June. Because the original report lacks enough process metadata to distinguish Agent terminal curl from an installer, user shell, or unrelated curl executable, this is strong source/history evidence rather than a live Windows reproduction.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to update both Desktop and Agent and retry the same curl command. If any window remains, capture a Windows Process Monitor Process Create event with image path, command line, and parent PID so the remaining spawn site can be identified. Suggested close note: "Current Desktop starts its backend with `pythonw.exe`, and current Agent launches local terminal/bash commands with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, so curl and other console descendants inherit a hidden console. This hardening ships from Agent v2026.7.1. Please update both components and retest; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a Procmon Process Create record if a window still appears."

Regressions cover external clears/same-length replacements (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.test.tsx:595-680`) and lossy-stream/final-text replacement (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.test.ts:51-72`). Today's focused chat suites passed 54/54 tests.
- Inference: The linked #757 race and the visible symptom described here are removed in current source; restart no longer needs to recover text lost by a stale ref, and final completion text repairs a lossy stream as a second safety net. A packaged AOSC/Wayland retest was not available, so closure should still request confirmation on a current release.
- Recommended action: Retest a current build with a long, rapidly streamed answer on the reporter's KDE/Wayland setup and verify the complete text is visible before restart. Suggested close note: "The v0.6.34 streaming-state race linked here was fixed in `3f65ba105` and hardened in `c5067689d`; current completion reconciliation also replaces lossy partial streams with the full final response. Focused regressions pass. Please retest on the latest release; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with a current accepted-event trace if text is still truncated."

## 2026-09-25 — #784 工作区点开之后就卡

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/784
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report says opening the localized “工作区” makes the entire app unusably laggy, restart clears it, and reopening the view restores the lag; it has no comments. Chinese localization maps both navigation `office` and `office.title` to “工作区” (`src/shared/i18n/locales/zh-CN/navigation.ts:1-12`; `src/shared/i18n/locales/zh-CN/office.ts:1-3`). Layout lazy-mounts visited views once and subsequently hides them with `display: none` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:266-290`), including Office, which remains mounted after navigation (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:959-963`). The `visible` prop gates data/GPU-status polling but `Office` always renders `Office3D` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx:202-221,601-619`). Its Canvas specifies no `frameloop` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx:424-439`), while the installed React Three Fiber defaults to `always` and continues active roots on requestAnimationFrame (`node_modules/@react-three/fiber/dist/events-583399dd.cjs.prod.js:1038-1045,7894-7916`); traffic, pedestrians, and agents all perform continuous `useFrame` work (`src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/objects/Traffic.tsx:562-566`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/objects/Pedestrians.tsx:176-200`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/objects/AgentsLayer.tsx:284-310`). The focused Office polling suite passed 4/4, but it mocks `Office3D` and contains no hidden-render-loop assertion.
- Inference: The still-mounted continuous WebGL loop and simulations deterministically explain why lag starts after entering Workspace/Office and persists until process restart. Exact severity remains GPU/driver dependent, but the lifecycle defect is present in current source and matches the independently reported #764/#852 family.
- Recommended action: Pass an explicit active state derived from `visible && document.visibilityState === "visible"` into `Office3D` and pause rendering/input while inactive; conditionally unmounting `Office3D` is the smallest robust alternative if releasing its WebGL context is acceptable. Affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/Office.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Office/office3d/Office3D.tsx`, and a focused lifecycle test. Regression check: visit Workspace, navigate to Chat, and assert Canvas frame callbacks stop and GPU returns near idle; revisit it and assert exactly one render loop resumes without duplicate listeners.

## 2026-09-26 — #414 [Bug]: Agent context/task metadata leaked into user conversation

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/414
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and describes `Active Task` plus internal context appearing after responses through the Desktop/API-server path. Current Agent deliberately persists context-compaction handoffs as ordinary `role="user"` or `role="assistant"` messages whose content includes the reference-only summary and task headings (`../hermes-agent/agent/context_compressor.py:114-154,7064-7072,7986-8006`). Agent commit `a2a23a8f7e30e644702459cfe0c4c74897524cfd` (2026-08-19) added a canonical client projection that strips pure handoffs and internal reasoning/tool state (`../hermes-agent/agent/compaction_display.py:13-47`; `../hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/api_server.py:506-541`), so current dashboard/API responses are protected. Desktop's local history path bypasses that projection: it selects raw user/assistant/tool rows and unconditionally turns every nonempty user row into a visible bubble (`src/main/sessions.ts:564-580,679-703`). The mid-stream and completion refreshes then merge those raw rows into the visible conversation (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatIPC.ts:82-110,208-218`). Legacy SSH duplicates the same raw-row behavior inline (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:1439-1457,1532-1557`). The focused history/remote pure suites pass 47/47, but the history suite contains no compaction-carrier case.
- Inference: The upstream fix removes the leak only on surfaces that use the Agent's display projection. Local Desktop and legacy SSH remain deterministically vulnerable once a compaction carrier exists: a pure internal handoff is nonempty and therefore satisfies the visible-user branch. That also explains why the block can recur on every post-turn DB refresh after the first compaction.
- Recommended action: Apply one canonical compaction display projection before `HistoryItem` construction, covering current and historical standalone prefixes plus merged/force-user-leading carriers; drop pure handoffs, preserve only authentic prior-tail/live-user content, and remove inherited tool/reasoning fields. Reuse it for local and legacy SSH, while keeping already-projected Remote dashboard rows harmless. Affected files: `src/main/sessions.ts`, `src/main/ssh-remote.ts`, and `tests/sessions-history-items.test.ts` (prefer a shared helper to avoid a second classifier). Regression check: feed standalone user- and assistant-role handoffs, a merged carrier with authentic prior text, and a force-user-leading carrier through local and SSH history conversion; assert pure metadata is absent, authentic content appears once, and subsequent DB refreshes never append the summary.

## 2026-09-26 — #536 Installation fails on Windows 11 with Smart App Control enabled

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/536
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and includes a Windows 11 25H2 reproduction where Smart App Control blocks `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\hermes\\hermes-agent\\venv\\Scripts\\python.exe` with OS error 4551 during dependency installation. Current Desktop still chooses `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\hermes` for a fresh Windows install, requires the venv interpreter/launcher layout, and later directly executes `pythonw.exe` from that tree (`src/main/installer.ts:41-81,174-213,573-604`). Its Windows bootstrap downloads the current upstream `install.ps1` and invokes it with that AppData install directory (`src/main/installer.ts:1080-1116,1147-1171`). The checked Agent installer defaults to the same paths, creates `venv` with `uv`, and repeatedly executes the generated `venv\\Scripts\\python.exe` during dependency validation (`../hermes-agent/scripts/install.ps1:15-35,2408-2426,2545-2565,2700-2721,2851-2878`). Windows release packaging produces unsigned NSIS/portable artifacts with no certificate/signing step or MSIX target (`electron-builder.yml:24-40`; `.github/workflows/release.yml:210-246`).
- Inference: The present install design still creates and executes an untrusted runtime in a user-writable AppData tree, exactly the executable and stage Smart App Control blocked. A Defender exclusion cannot override Smart App Control trust, and signing only the outer Electron installer would not establish trust for dynamically provisioned Python/entry-point binaries.
- Recommended action: Treat this as a distribution/signing defect, not an antivirus exception. The smallest safe product fix is to publish a publisher-signed Windows package that contains a prebuilt, signed Agent/runtime payload (MSIX or an equivalently signed installed runtime) and execute only those trusted binaries; do not recommend disabling Smart App Control. Affected surfaces: `electron-builder.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml` and beta release, `src/main/installer.ts`, plus the Agent's Windows packaging/bootstrap contract. Regression check: on a clean Windows 11 VM with Smart App Control Evaluation/On and no exclusions, verify install, dependency setup, first chat, update, and uninstall without 4551 or trust prompts, and verify Authenticode/package identity for every executed Hermes-owned `.exe`/`.dll`.

- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and describes Desktop v0.15.2: configuring only the Agent terminal SSH backend left files/browser/memory/skills local, raw non-loopback `/api/ws` returned 401, and there was no usable token/auth setup. Current Desktop has explicit Local, Remote, and SSH connection modes (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/ConnectionPane.tsx:193-235`). Direct Remote auto-detects token versus OAuth, opens a dedicated browser login when required, validates before committing the connection, and obtains single-use WebSocket tickets (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1481-1511,1514-1573`; `src/main/remote-oauth.ts:125-175,198-203,337-351`). Token requests send both the dashboard session-token header and Bearer compatibility auth, and the shared authenticated boundary supports management APIs rather than chat alone (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:71-124`; `src/main/remote-api.ts:46-89`). SSH mode now starts the dashboard on the remote host, tunnels `/api/*` and `/api/ws`, and explicitly provides full local-mode parity with legacy `/v1` fallback (`src/main/dashboard.ts:159-181`; `src/renderer/src/components/settings/ConnectionPane.tsx:488-508`). Remote gateway management landed after the report in commit `6621f0051dbba24a101e92fed4336b91ea39bf7f` (2026-07-14). The user-facing settings now explain `API_SERVER_KEY`, OAuth browser sign-in, and the SSH no-exposed-port path (`src/shared/i18n/locales/en/settings.ts:180-205,225-238`). The pure auth/API/skills/history suites pass 47/47; socket-backed tests could not bind loopback in this sandbox (`EPERM`) and therefore were not counted as product failures.
- Inference: The report conflated the Agent's terminal-only SSH tool backend with Desktop's machine-level SSH/Remote connection. Current Desktop has the missing machine-level transports and authenticated dashboard path, so chat and management/tool configuration are routed to the remote Agent rather than merely forwarding shell commands. Static evidence is strong, but a two-Windows-machine LAN retest is still needed before unconditional closure.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to update both Desktop and Agent, choose Desktop **SSH Tunnel** for the simplest LAN setup (or Remote with `API_SERVER_KEY`/browser OAuth), and verify a file/browser operation plus sessions/skills against the laptop's Hermes home. Suggested close note: "Remote connectivity has been rebuilt since v0.15.2: Desktop now has explicit machine-level SSH and Remote modes, tunnels the authenticated dashboard WebSocket/API over SSH, auto-detects token versus browser OAuth, and routes remote management surfaces through the selected gateway. Please update both components and retest with SSH Tunnel or the documented Remote auth flow; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with current connection diagnostics if any operation still executes on the client PC."

## 2026-09-27 — #442 API keys were all saved in plain text and then leaked

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/442
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments and its screenshot shows a Hermes `.env`-style configuration containing a full GLM credential; the screenshot itself is public, but it does not identify an application exfiltration path. Current source confirms the underlying exposure surface remains: an unset or unknown `secrets.provider` selects the plaintext `.env` backend (`src/main/secrets/index.ts:28-45`), provider-key edits write the literal value as `KEY=value` (`src/main/config.ts:548-581`), and the generic atomic writer creates replacement files without an explicit restrictive mode (`src/main/utils.ts:212-231`). More importantly, the `get-env` IPC returns the complete parsed secret map to the renderer (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1044-1048`; `src/preload/index.ts:306-311`), where Providers stores the raw map in React state and supports revealing/editing each value (`src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx:259-271,963-999`). A later opt-in command/vault provider (`838b786946eff46de64f2320611a18c388059417`) can resolve secrets without writing them to `.env`, but the default path is intentionally unchanged and the focused secrets-provider suite confirms that fallback behavior. Today's three focused suites passed 40/40 tests.
- Inference: Plaintext-at-rest and unnecessary main-to-renderer secret exposure are confirmed; an external breach or automatic upload is not established by the issue. Any local process/user able to read the Hermes home, a permissively created `.env`, or a compromised renderer can recover every configured key, so the report is materially valid even though the specific word “leaked” needs incident details.
- Recommended action: First make `.env`/`auth.json` creation and replacement preserve owner-only permissions (`0600` on POSIX, an equivalent user-only ACL on Windows), and replace broad `get-env`/credential-pool reads with main-process status/masked metadata plus per-key set/clear operations so raw secrets do not cross into the renderer after entry. Then make the existing command/vault provider the guided default where supported and offer an explicit migration that deletes plaintext only after verification. Smallest affected surface: `src/main/utils.ts`, `src/main/config.ts`, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, `src/preload/index.ts`/`index.d.ts`, and the Providers UI/tests. Regression check: save two provider keys and assert secret files are user-only, IPC/rendered state contains only set/masked indicators (never either literal), chat still resolves both in main, and vault migration removes the plaintext only after a successful provider lookup.
