## 2026-09-29 — #988 Gemma4-E4B vision support is not recognized

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/988
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report targets the newer Hermes Desktop v0.21.5 local-runtime UI, not this standalone Electron checkout (which is v0.7.7 at `package.json:1-4` and has no `/api/local-models` browser). The relevant current implementation is upstream Hermes Agent commit `39faafb61688282a372ff2d59191581588e9a003`. Its Hugging Face browser deliberately removes every `mmproj`/draft GGUF from the selectable file groups (`hermes_cli/local_runtime/hf_browse.py:90-104`), and the arbitrary `/download-browsed` route downloads only the selected model paths into the models directory without recording a projector association (`hermes_cli/web_routers/local_models.py:942-962`). Preset generation looks up `entry_for_model(model_id)` and adds `mmproj` only when that curated catalog entry declares one (`hermes_cli/local_runtime/presets.py:79-102,130-134`); it atomically rewrites the complete `presets.ini` from generated entries (`hermes_cli/local_runtime/presets.py:220-244`), explaining why the reporter's manual line disappears. Offline vision capability likewise returns unknown for staged models absent from the curated catalog (`hermes_cli/local_runtime/capabilities.py:55-78`). Qwen3.6 has explicit projector metadata in `catalog.json:86-114`, while repository search finds no Gemma4-E4B catalog entry, matching the reported difference. The issue has no comments.
- Inference: A browsed, non-catalog multimodal GGUF cannot currently acquire a durable model-to-projector mapping, even when the user manually places the correct projector under `models/assets`. This is a deterministic product limitation; standalone llama.cpp works because the user supplies `--mmproj` directly, whereas Hermes regenerates policy-owned presets and has no association to regenerate for Gemma.
- Recommended action: Fix this in the current Hermes Agent local-runtime owner. Extend repository browsing to expose compatible projector companions without listing them as standalone models; on download, require an unambiguous or user-selected projector, store it under `models/assets`, and persist a per-model sidecar association that `presets.py` and `capabilities.py` consult after the curated catalog. Do not infer across repositories or silently choose among multiple projectors. Affected upstream files: `hermes_cli/local_runtime/hf_browse.py`, `hermes_cli/web_routers/local_models.py`, `hermes_cli/local_runtime/presets.py`, `hermes_cli/local_runtime/capabilities.py`, and focused tests. Regression check: browse a fixture repo containing one model GGUF plus one mmproj, download once, assert the projector is not a second model, the regenerated preset retains its `mmproj` path, and vision capability is true; a fixture with two projectors must require an explicit choice.

## 2026-09-29 — #985 Delegated subagents appear as separate sidebar chats instead of inside the chat that spawned them

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/985
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments, and both halves remain in current HEAD `ba86e0f210525302359efef84987ecb7bcb29b1b` and `origin/main`. Local session listing selects every row accepted by `sessionVisibilityPredicate` (`src/main/sessions.ts:263-306`), while that predicate checks only the optional `archived` column and never `parent_session_id` (`src/main/db.ts:9-16`). The cache rebuild uses the same predicate (`src/main/session-cache.ts:125-148`), and local IPC routes the sidebar's direct and cached session calls into those functions (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2277-2303,2730-2779`). The repository already knows `parent_session_id` exists for subagent/branch rows when deleting a parent (`src/main/sessions.ts:742-778`), so schema uncertainty is not the missing design intent. On the parent-chat side, `applyDashboardStreamEvent` handles message/reasoning/tool/clarify/completion events and returns state unchanged for every unknown type (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:641-731`); the WebSocket hook forwards accepted events through that adapter after only special-casing session/background events (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:995-1050`). `git grep` on `origin/main` finds no `subagent.*` handling or tests anywhere under `src`/`tests`.
- Inference: Child session rows deterministically satisfy the local sidebar query, while relayed `subagent.start/progress/thinking/tool/complete` frames are accepted for the parent runtime session and then discarded by the adapter's default branch. The reporter's live event/RPC trace is therefore consistent with both current code paths; no platform-specific condition is required.
- Recommended action: Extend the schema-aware visibility predicate with `s.parent_session_id IS NULL` when that column exists, so direct listing and full cache reconciliation both evict children while legacy databases remain readable. Separately, intercept `subagent.*` before the generic message adapter, keep a roster keyed by parent conversation plus delegation/subagent id, hydrate it with `subagent.list`, and expose expandable tail plus steer/interrupt actions through the existing dashboard JSON-RPC client. Affected files: `src/main/db.ts`, session/cache tests, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts`, `Chat.tsx`, a new roster component/types, and focused renderer tests. Regression check: seed one parent and two child DB rows and assert only the parent appears before and after cache sync; replay start/tool/progress/complete events on the parent's session and assert one in-chat roster updates in place, survives hydration, and its steer/interrupt RPC targets the selected child exactly once.

## 2026-09-30 — #636 v0.6.0 AppImage is corrupted: both latest-linux.yml AND the AppImage binary are broken

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/636
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report provides a reproducible v0.6.0 mismatch: `latest-linux.yml` described a 201,990,360-byte AppImage while the release URL served a 202,174,197-byte file with a different SHA-512; comments say v0.6.1 and v0.6.2 also failed. At tag v0.6.0, both x64 and arm64 jobs produced the same `${name}-${version}.AppImage` filename, while the release job downloaded all artifacts into one merged directory and published that shared name. Current commit `5e7ce0c6f117385c5c12ba9eb55d21aef4966a20` gives every AppImage an architecture-qualified filename (`electron-builder.yml:78-79`), uploads x64 and arm64 outputs separately (`.github/workflows/release.yml:144-208`), then merges and publishes them (`.github/workflows/release.yml:280-301,367-383`). The current v0.7.7 release exposes distinct `hermes-desktop-0.7.7-x86_64.AppImage` and `hermes-desktop-0.7.7-arm64.AppImage` assets; the x64 asset metadata size (195,379,843 bytes) exactly matches the live `latest-linux.yml` entry. `tests/release-artifacts.test.ts:7-23` locks architecture-qualified names and both update feeds, and today's focused run passed all 52 tests.
- Inference: The historical manifest/binary mismatch is real and the cross-architecture filename collision is a concrete release-pipeline root cause: merging two artifacts with the same path can replace the binary independently of the x64 manifest. Architecture-qualified artifacts remove that collision, and the current published manifest/asset metadata agree, so the issue appears fixed in later releases. The repository still has no CI step that extracts each AppImage and independently recomputes the published SHA-512, so the original squashfs-corruption claim was not fully re-exercised on this macOS host and recurrence from another cause would not be caught.
- Recommended action: Close after one Linux x64 smoke check of the current release (`sha512sum` against `latest-linux.yml`, then `--appimage-extract` and launch). Suggested close note: "The old Linux release collision has been fixed: commit `5e7ce0c6` made AppImage names architecture-specific and added regression coverage, so x64 and arm64 artifacts can no longer overwrite one another during merged publishing. The current v0.7.7 x64 asset size matches its live update manifest. Closing as fixed in later releases; please reopen if a current AppImage fails checksum or extraction." As follow-up hardening, add a publish gate that recomputes manifest hashes and extracts every AppImage before release.

## 2026-09-30 — #632 Installation path causes startup failure on Ubuntu

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/632
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The Ubuntu 24.04 report says both package forms crashed at startup and shows that moving `/opt/Hermes One` to `/opt/HermesOne` fixed the installed DEB; one commenter confirmed the workaround. Commit `8f1cd21fac50a3aa293b2acc890e5cc42e471d90` later changed the global packaging `productName` to the space-free `HermesOne`, specifically because Chromium's zygote failed to `execvp` the whitespace-containing path. Current packaging therefore installs Linux under `/opt/HermesOne` (`electron-builder.yml:2-11`) while preserving the visible "Hermes One" desktop label (`electron-builder.yml:72-77`); the Linux post-install hook targets the same space-free sandbox path (`build/linux-after-install.sh:18-27`). `tests/packaging-identity.test.ts:39-46` evaluates Electron Builder's real metadata and asserts `/opt/HermesOne`; today's focused run passed all 52 tests.
- Inference: The report's successful manual rename and the later source fix establish the same root cause. Current DEB/RPM packages no longer create the failing whitespace path, so the reported installation failure appears fixed. The issue did not provide a separate AppImage trace, and no packaged Ubuntu launch was available today, so the conclusion for AppImage relies on the common packaging identity rather than an independent reproduction.
- Recommended action: Retest the latest DEB and x64 AppImage on Ubuntu 24.04 without the symlink workaround. Suggested close note: "Fixed by `8f1cd21f`: Linux packages now use the space-free `/opt/HermesOne` runtime path while keeping the user-facing name 'Hermes One'. The packaging regression asserts the resolved install path and the focused suite passes. Closing as apparently fixed; please reopen with a current AppImage/DEB launch log if either still fails."

## 2026-10-01 — #592 App crashes on startup with virtual display adapters — "GPU process isn't usable"

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/592
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The v0.5.8 Windows report shows repeated abnormal GPU-process exits followed by Chromium's fatal "GPU process isn't usable" shutdown; its 2026-09-29 comment reports the same Electron signature with GameViewer in other applications. Hermes-specific commit `8bf2fe0f6` (2026-06-09), one day after the issue, added a guard explicitly for virtual-display-adapter GPU crashes. Current startup installs that guard before app readiness (`src/main/index.ts:1-12`). On the first non-clean GPU child exit it persists software-rendering state, relaunches once with `--hermes-gpu-disabled`, and exits the failing process (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:304-349`); the next launch calls `app.disableHardwareAcceleration()` and adds the GPU/compositing switches before readiness (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:173-209`). Regressions cover persisted disablement, the one-shot relaunch sentinel when persistence fails, clean-exit filtering, loop prevention, and an already-disabled launch (`tests/gpu-fallback.test.ts:193-238`). Today's focused run passed all 84 tests.
- Inference: The original fatal retry loop is intercepted by a post-report, purpose-built recovery path, while setting the switches inside Electron before readiness addresses why externally supplied `--disable-gpu` did not necessarily rescue the reporter. A live machine with the reporter's virtual adapter was unavailable, so an exit that occurs before Electron emits `child-process-gone` remains unproven.
- Recommended action: Retest a current packaged Windows build with the virtual adapter enabled and verify the first crash triggers one automatic relaunch and the relaunched app stays open in software mode. Suggested close note: "Hermes now catches abnormal GPU child exits, persists software rendering, and relaunches once with hardware acceleration disabled before app readiness (`8bf2fe0f6`, with later loop-safety hardening). Focused GPU recovery tests pass. Please retest on the current build with the virtual adapter enabled; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with the new startup log if it still exits before recovery."

## 2026-10-01 — #594 Gateway spawn fails: PID file mismatch (gateway.pid vs gateway.lock) + missing "run" argument

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/594
- Classification: **NOT REPRODUCIBLE**
- Observed evidence: Desktop does launch the local Agent as bare `hermes gateway` (`src/main/hermes.ts:3310-3316,3369-3382`) and uses `gateway.pid` for liveness and shutdown (`src/main/hermes.ts:3451-3484,3492-3530`). Those choices agree with the current Agent contract rather than conflicting with it: Agent documents and returns `{HERMES_HOME}/gateway.pid` as the PID record, while `gateway.lock` is a distinct mutual-exclusion lock (`../hermes-agent/gateway/status.py:1-12,38-48,217-228`). Agent's parser labels `run` the default and its dispatcher has treated an absent gateway subcommand as `run` since commit `619c72e566f` (2026-02-02), before this report (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/subcommands/gateway.py:32-49`; `../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/gateway.py:7747-7761`). Its process matcher also explicitly recognizes bare `hermes gateway` as a run command (`../hermes-agent/gateway/status.py:493-504`). Today's gateway/transport/GPU focused run passed 84/84 tests.
- Inference: Neither proposed mismatch exists in the current paired source, and the missing-`run` diagnosis conflicts with Agent behavior that predates the issue. The reporter may still have encountered a real Windows startup failure from a different cause or from mismatched installed sources, but the issue does not provide the spawned command's stderr/version pair needed to establish that cause.
- Recommended action: Do not change Desktop to read `gateway.lock` or require `gateway run`; both would duplicate or confuse the established contract. Ask for a current reproduction with Desktop version, `hermes --version`, `hermes gateway` output, and the per-profile gateway log emitted by `startGatewayDetailed`. If bare and explicit `gateway run` differ on the installed Agent, treat that as a packaging/version-skew defect and record both resolved executables before fixing it. Otherwise close as not reproducible on current paired sources.

## 2026-10-01 — #596 TUI Gateway path blocks message sending for 45s before API fallback (Windows)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/596
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The reported hard wait remains literal in the legacy local transport: `TuiGatewayClient.start()` races process exit against `waitForDashboardReady(..., 45_000)` (`src/main/hermes.ts:680-735`). `sendMessageViaBestApi` tries that client before the non-gateway API for ordinary local text messages, and the only global eligibility exclusions are test environments (`src/main/hermes.ts:916-933,2843-2895`). The newer default dashboard transport introduced after the report does show loading before send (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatActions.ts:375-383`), but it does not remove the block: local dashboard startup itself waits up to 45 seconds (`src/main/dashboard.ts:358-378,713-719`); in Auto mode an unavailable dashboard returns `false` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1579-1597`), and `sendToAgent` then invokes the legacy IPC path (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatActions.ts:161-181`), which can begin the separate 45-second TUI wait before finally using the API. Existing tests validate surrounding transport behavior but do not assert a bounded fallback latency; today's focused run passed 84/84.
- Inference: A failed local dashboard/TUI startup can still delay the first ordinary send for at least the reported 45 seconds and, through Auto fallback, can incur two sequential startup probes. The loading state and fallback toast improve feedback but do not fix latency. The exact Windows trigger was not reproduced on this macOS host, yet the timeout path is deterministic once readiness never succeeds.
- Recommended action: Carry an explicit "dashboard already unavailable" disposition from `useDashboardChatTransport.sendMessage` through `useChatActions`/preload IPC, and have `sendMessageViaBestApi` skip `TuiGatewayClient` for that fallback so it goes directly to the non-gateway API. Also bound or share the local readiness attempt so one user send cannot pay two 45-second probes. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatActions.ts`, preload/API types, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, `src/main/hermes.ts`, and focused transport tests. Regression check: make local dashboard readiness time out, assert one readiness attempt, immediate direct-API fallback afterward, one submitted prompt, visible loading throughout, and total fallback latency below a small explicit budget.

## 2026-10-02 — #911 HTTP 401: User not found.

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/911
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports that resuming an expired session sometimes yields `HTTP 401: User not found`, but omits the Desktop/Agent versions, provider, authentication type, connection mode, and failing endpoint. That exact phrase is not generated anywhere in the current standalone Desktop or sibling Agent source. The current Remote Gateway OAuth request path converts any HTTP 401 into the structured `oauth_login_required` error "Remote OAuth session expired. Sign in again," with a focused regression (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:251-275`; `tests/remote-oauth.test.ts:239-248`), so the raw phrase is unlikely to come through that path. Current Hermes Agent commit `97ca90f184c5c7c1a9ab8d9052f99be440e7fbd7` (2026-09-09) explicitly identifies `HTTP 401 'User not found'` from Nous Portal, Codex, and xAI as a rejected OAuth grant and adds provider/auth recovery metadata after this report. Agent now includes that `error_surface` in failed `message.complete` payloads (`../hermes-agent/tui_gateway/server.py:11289-11321`), but this Desktop reads only status/error/text, stores only a plain `error` string, and renders a generic red bubble (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:735-750,1038-1065`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/types.ts:8-29`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageRow.tsx:230-257`). All 88 focused auth/dashboard tests passed after rerunning the socket-backed suite with localhost permission.
- Inference: The exact wording and later upstream diagnosis make an expired provider OAuth grant the most likely cause; retrying or recreating the runtime cannot revive that credential. The standalone renderer still drops the structured metadata needed to offer reauthentication, so the user-visible failure likely remains even with a current Agent. If the reporter instead meant Remote Gateway OAuth, current source already gives an actionable sign-in error; the missing connection/provider details prevent a fully confirmed classification.
- Recommended action: Preserve and validate Agent `error_surface` on failed dashboard completions, add it to `ChatBubbleMessage`, and render a provider-auth card that opens the existing profile-scoped OAuth login flow when `layer=auth` and `auth_kind=oauth`; do not mark an authentication failure as requiring session recreation. Keep the raw text fallback for old Agents and route API-key failures to Providers rather than OAuth. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/types.ts`, `hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts`, `MessageRow.tsx`, `Chat.tsx`/`OAuthLoginModal`, and focused renderer tests. Regression check: replay a failed `message.complete` containing `HTTP 401: User not found` plus `{layer:"auth", auth_kind:"oauth", provider:"nous"}`, assert one "Sign in to Nous Portal again" action, one profile-scoped OAuth invocation when clicked, and no `session.close`/forced recreation; separately keep Remote Gateway 401 mapped to its gateway sign-in flow.

## 2026-10-02 — #674 无法导入备份

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/674
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The Windows 10 report shows Desktop failing the exact child command `pythonw.exe -m hermes_cli.main import <backup.zip>` and has no comments. Current Windows constants still select `venv\\Scripts\\pythonw.exe`, while `hermesCliArgs` produces `-m hermes_cli.main`; `runHermesImport` appends only `import` and the archive, gives the child a 120-second timeout, and reports stderr or the generic process error (`src/main/installer.ts:181-219,1275-1318`). The renderer selects the file and invokes that function without any overwrite confirmation (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/useSettingsData.ts:801-819`; `src/renderer/src/components/settings/DataPane.tsx:55-78`). The current Agent import deliberately prompts on stdin whenever the target already has `config.yaml` or `.env`, unless `--force` is passed (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/backup.py:1076-1092`); the parser exposes `--force` specifically for non-interactive overwrite (`../hermes-agent/hermes_cli/subcommands/import_cmd.py:17-31`). Desktop neither supplies stdin nor passes that switch, and the existing Desktop regression covers archive-path validation only (`tests/installer-import-validation.test.ts:9-44`). Blame shows the subprocess contract has remained materially unchanged since its April introduction.
- Inference: Importing into the normal configured Desktop home deterministically reaches an interactive prompt that the GUI cannot answer, so it waits until the Desktop timeout and surfaces a generic `Command failed` message—the reported behavior. Invalid archives can fail earlier for other reasons, but the missing non-interactive confirmation contract alone is sufficient to confirm this bug.
- Recommended action: After archive validation, show an explicit destructive confirmation in the renderer explaining that existing Hermes files will be overwritten; only after acceptance invoke the CLI with `--force`. Capture stdout as well as stderr so validation/import errors printed by Agent are visible, and refresh affected settings/session state only after success. Smallest affected surface: `src/renderer/src/components/settings/useSettingsData.ts` and its settings UI/tests, `src/main/installer.ts`, plus a behavioral import regression. Regression check: with an existing `config.yaml`, cancel once and assert no spawn; confirm once and assert the Windows argv contains `-m hermes_cli.main import <absolute.zip> --force`, completes without stdin or timeout, and surfaces a stdout-only validation error verbatim.

## 2026-10-03 — #996 Remote gateway sign-in never completes against an HTTPS dashboard (`__Host-`-prefixed session cookie not recognized)

The attachment does not include symbols or a reproducer, so it cannot prove which task action triggered the child launch. - Recommended action: Ship the current space-free Linux packaging, then reproduce once from the new `/opt/HermesOne` path; if no zygote `SIGTRAP` occurs, close as fixed. Focused regression check: install the generated DEB, assert its executable path contains no whitespace, launch a zygote/render child while running a task, and verify no `LaunchProcess`/`execvp` failure or core dump. Suggested close note: "The attached dump matches the Linux whitespace-in-install-path zygote `SIGTRAP`. Current source installs under `/opt/HermesOne`, removing that failing launch path. This will be included in the next release; please retest that build and reopen with a new symbolized coredump if it recurs."

## 2026-10-04 — #562 HUGE PROBLEM: When ever the app is start it removes custom API keys

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/562
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report predates the current custom-provider manager, contains no comments, and does not identify its app version, provider names, or before/after key files. Current settings store provider identity in `providers.json` and secrets separately in `.env` (`src/main/providers-store.ts:24-29`). Import and migration are additive: an Agent custom key is copied to its derived Desktop key only when that destination is empty, while the source key is retained (`src/main/providers-store.ts:80-127`); provider deletion occurs only through the explicit remove function (`src/main/providers-store.ts:206-229`) and its IPC handler (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2556-2580`). The renderer rebuilds its provider list from stored records, legacy models, and any valued orphan `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_*_KEY`, so a key without a record remains recoverable (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:567-629`); clearing the key is confined to the explicit Remove flow (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:651-677`). Named providers receive dedicated derived keys on save (`src/renderer/src/components/settings/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:956-984`), and runtime lookup checks that key before shared fallbacks (`src/main/hermes.ts:2623-2653`). Commit `0f6175f7c6e7e0690a44b7dcd92c5bd425a85dc6` introduced this manager after the report and is contained in v0.7.7. Focused provider persistence tests passed, including preservation of the original imported key and idempotent per-provider writes without clobbering OpenAI/Anthropic keys (`src/main/providers-store.test.ts:115-136`; `tests/compat-host-derived-key-persistence.test.ts:67-232`).
- Inference: The exact historical deletion path cannot be reconstructed from the report, but the later architecture removes the startup-time destructive behavior it describes: records persist independently, writes are additive, valued orphan keys are recovered, and only an explicit user removal clears a named key. This therefore appears fixed rather than conclusively reproduced on the unspecified old build.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retry v0.7.7 or newer. Suggested close note: "Custom-provider persistence was reworked after this report. Current releases use dedicated per-provider keys, preserve imported/orphaned keys, and remove them only through the explicit Remove action; the focused persistence tests pass. Closing as apparently fixed. If it still occurs, please reopen with the current app version, provider name/base URL, active profile, and a redacted before/after `.env` diff."

## 2026-10-04 — #550 Duplicate assistant messages and wrong message ordering after SSE stream → DB reconciliation

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/550
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report is for v0.5.2 and correctly points to post-stream DB reconciliation. Current completion handling still refreshes from the DB and calls `reconcileAfterDbRefresh` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatIPC.ts:185-218`), but three later fixes contained in v0.7.7 changed the reported failure modes: `0aa210d6c153012a6358e268c3a811ce97c11e7b` prevents old messages jumping to the bottom, `944e8cd0bccfc537eb0af0edd20cddbecec846fc` pre-seeds result bubbles to prevent near-duplicates, and `ef0b7941e35c1089a55b27a8933dae79fcde0b8a` interleaves instead of appending unmatched rows. The current algorithm uses FIFO buckets, DB-canonical ordering, normalized deduplication, chronological insertion of unmatched streamed rows, user anchors, and special handling for lossy reasoning/clarification rows (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/sessionHistory.ts:230-246,755-888`). The focused suite passes cases for duplicated content across turns, streamed rows exceeding DB rows, concatenated-versus-split assistant bubbles, long-chat tool splits, unmatched historical rows, synthetic tool canonicalization, and clarification ordering (`tests/reconcile-streamed-with-db.test.ts:176-306,545-717,776-806,927-966`); all 64 focused reconciliation/provider/approval-adjacent tests passed today.
- Inference: Text-derived bubble keys remain, so the reporter's proposed position-only rewrite was not adopted. Nevertheless, the exact duplicate and suffix-order symptoms were addressed by multiple post-v0.5.2 fixes plus focused regression coverage, and the current implementation no longer has the simple unmatched-to-end behavior described in the issue. A distinct current-version reproduction would be a new residual case rather than evidence that this original path remains unchanged.
- Recommended action: Close as fixed in later releases. Suggested close note: "Current releases include the post-v0.5.2 reconciliation fixes `0aa210d6`, `944e8cd0`, and `ef0b7941`: unmatched streamed rows are interleaved chronologically, result bubbles seed deduplication, and FIFO/DB-order regressions cover repeated content and tool rows. The focused reconciliation suite passes. Closing as fixed; please reopen with current-version session data if the exact duplicate/order symptom remains."

## 2026-10-04 — #524 Permission/license dialog window invisible — no clickable UI elements to accept/grant permissions

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/524
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: A later issue comment clarifies that an Agent write operation waits for approval and then times out because no confirmation UI is visible. Current runs-mode handling detects `approval.request` but deliberately stops that run and falls back because renderer approval controls are only available after the response finishes (`src/main/hermes.ts:1906-1912`). The legacy TUI/gateway path is worse: it responds to every approval request automatically with `choice: "once"` and `all: false`, without user input (`src/main/hermes.ts:2187-2210`). The dashboard event adapter models message, reasoning, tool, clarification, and completion events but has no approval event case (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/dashboardEventAdapter.ts:641-731`). The only renderer control is a regex heuristic over completed assistant text, hidden while loading and limited to the final message (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageRow.tsx:13-14,230-235,331-343`); `/approve` and `/deny` are merely prompt-level native commands (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatActions.ts:14-18`). No pending structured approval implementation exists on current HEAD. Commit `b2ae5bd836161ea96aa1893b170712ead1470348` implements structured desktop approvals on several integration branches, but it is not an ancestor of current HEAD. Today's 64 focused tests passed, but none exercises an end-to-end approval request reaching a clickable renderer decision.
- Inference: The missing dialog is not a rendering glitch. Current source has no viable structured approval transport/UI: one path abandons the request, another silently approves it, and the renderer ignores the event. That fully explains both the invisible confirmation and timeout described by commenters, while also exposing a security problem in the auto-approved legacy path.
- Recommended action: Adapt the structured approval architecture from `b2ae5bd8` into current main: define and normalize a shared approval request, keep a main-process pending registry bound to owner/run/request, relay it through IPC/preload, render an inline approval card, and send exactly the selected once/session/always/deny response. Remove automatic approval and prompt replay after an approval begins; clear pending requests on abort/disconnect. Expected affected surface: a shared approval type, `src/main/hermes.ts`, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, preload declarations, dashboard transport/adapter/chat state, message-list UI and focused tests. Regression check: inject `approval.request` through dashboard, TUI, and runs paths; assert no response before a click, exactly one matching response after each choice, denial never executes the tool, another run/window cannot answer it, abort clears it, and no fallback/replay occurs after interaction.

## 2026-10-05 — #998 Desktop model picker shows only current model for non-default profiles

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/998
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The new report has no comments and describes direct Remote/Docker mode with several profiles. The chat hook receives the selected profile and passes it to `getModelConfig`, but calls `listModels()` with no profile (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useModelConfig.ts:98-110,123-134`). The preload contract exposes no profile argument (`src/preload/index.ts:1263-1277`; `src/preload/index.d.ts:928-942`), and the `list-models` IPC handler accepts none: direct Remote calls `remoteListModels(conn)`, SSH dashboard uses the process-global active profile, and local reads the same global value (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2877-2901`). Direct Remote model config is also unscoped despite receiving the renderer profile: `get-model-config` calls `remoteGetModelConfig(conn)` and `set-model-config` calls `remoteSetModelConfig(conn, ...)` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1146-1178`). Finally, the remote client requests bare `/api/model/library`, `/api/model/options`, and `/api/model/set` paths with no `profile` query/body (`src/main/remote-models.ts:97-103,210-270`), even though the injected dashboard model-library endpoint explicitly accepts `profile` (`src/main/hermes-agent-compat.ts:238-270`). Existing tests pin the unscoped URLs and exercise only configs without a profile (`tests/remote-models.test.ts:40-87`); the hook test mounts `useModelConfig()` without a profile (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useModelConfig.test.tsx:20-38`). The seven remote-model tests pass when loopback binding is allowed, confirming this coverage gap.
- Inference: Desktop combines a named profile's current model with an unscoped/default-profile model library. On an older dashboard fallback, `modelsFromRemoteOptions` deliberately keeps the current model plus only configured/user-defined provider rows, which can reduce the picker to exactly the current model. A failed Spark request is therefore a trigger for stale/mismatched state, not the root cause; the missing profile at every renderer→IPC→REST boundary is deterministic.
- Recommended action: Thread the explicit profile through `listModels(profile)`, preload typing/IPC, `get-model-config`, and `set-model-config`; create the same profile-scoped remote config already used by session APIs, and add `?profile=<encoded>` to library/options/set endpoints (or the backend's equivalent body field) while preserving default-profile behavior. Use the passed profile for local and SSH fallbacks rather than rereading process-global state. Expected affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useModelConfig.ts`, both preload declarations, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, `src/main/remote-models.ts`, and their tests. Focused regression check: render the hook with `profile="james"`, assert all model reads/writes are scoped to `james`, return distinct default/james libraries from a test server, and verify switching profiles never collapses either picker to the other's current row.

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/564
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The June report and its one comment describe the former per-model editor accepting `https://model-go.com/v1` plus a key but failing to place that credential on the runtime path. Current v0.7.7 no longer has that split contract: a named custom provider's modal derives a dedicated `CUSTOM_PROVIDER_<NAME>_KEY` and persists its identity on close (`src/renderer/src/components/ProviderKeysSection.tsx:956-984`); the main store mirrors the provider into `config.yaml` with that exact variable as `key_env` (`src/main/providers-store.ts:175-203`). Legacy `custom_providers:` imports persist their supplied API key additively under the same dedicated name and, for recognized vendor hosts, a host-derived key (`src/main/models.ts:305-345`). At runtime, custom chat first checks a host-derived key, then matches the selected base URL in the model library and resolves the provider-label-derived key, before shared fallbacks (`src/main/hermes.ts:2610-2653`). Commit `e2124246c2e5691b625d716c4613191ae92851c1` added host-derived runtime compatibility on 2026-06-04; `0f6175f7c6e7e0690a44b7dcd92c5bd425a85dc6` added the named custom-provider manager on 2026-07-14. Both are contained in v0.7.7. Focused tests passed for unknown-host dedicated keys, known-host dual writes, non-clobbering of OpenAI/Anthropic credentials, idempotence, and terminal `key_env` import (`tests/compat-host-derived-key-persistence.test.ts:67-232`; `src/main/providers-store.test.ts:107-136`).
- Inference: The issue's expectation that `model.api_key` become uncommented is obsolete; the current intentional contract keeps secrets in `.env` and stores only `key_env` in `config.yaml`. For an unknown host such as model-go.com, the dedicated provider-label key is now persisted and resolved by the exact endpoint, addressing the reported 401 path. No live request to that third-party endpoint was made, so this is source-and-regression evidence rather than an external credential test.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to recreate the endpoint as a named Custom provider on v0.7.7+, add the model beneath that provider, and retry. Suggested close note: "The custom-provider credential path was reworked after this report. Current releases store each named provider's secret in its dedicated `.env` key, mirror that key name into `config.yaml`, and resolve it by provider label/base URL at runtime; focused persistence and routing tests pass. Closing as apparently fixed. If model-go.com still returns 401 on v0.7.7+, please reopen with a redacted provider record, model-library row, and env-key names (not values)."

## 2026-10-05 — #537 Context compression appends summaries at end of conversation, mixing history into active chat

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/537
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments. Current Hermes Agent marks a standalone compression summary persisted to `state.db` with `display_kind="hidden"` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/run_agent.py:2080-2094`), and its database schema carries `display_kind`/`display_metadata` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_state.py:1107-1131`). Hermes Desktop nevertheless defines `RawMessageRow` without that field and expands every non-empty assistant row into an ordinary bubble (`src/main/sessions.ts:546-557,564-605`); the local query does not select or filter `display_kind` (`src/main/sessions.ts:679-702`). The SSH history script likewise omits the column and renders every assistant row (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:1532-1557`). Remote history normalization drops presentation metadata (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:481-510`), while the current Agent session-message API allowlist does not expose `display_kind` at all (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/api_server.py:2272-2279`). Existing history tests cover plain user/assistant/tool/reasoning expansion but no hidden/compression row (`tests/sessions-history-items.test.ts:153-229`). All 45 non-network focused tests passed; the seven remote-model tests also passed after rerunning with loopback permission.
- Inference: A compression summary is intentionally durable because the model needs it after session rotation, but it is presentation-hidden metadata, not a user-visible assistant reply. Desktop discards that distinction on local, SSH, and Remote paths, so the exact reported wall-of-history bubble remains reproducible from current data. Filtering generic assistant text would be unsafe; the Agent's explicit metadata plus narrowly recognized legacy summary prefixes provide the correct boundary.
- Recommended action: Add optional `display_kind` to the shared raw row and drop rows marked `hidden` before expansion; make the local query schema-aware so older Agent databases lacking the column still load. Mirror the filter in the SSH script. For Remote, first update the Agent session-message response allowlist to include `display_kind`, preserve it in `normalizeMessageRow`, and use exact current/legacy compression prefixes only as a compatibility fallback for servers that cannot expose metadata. Expected affected files: `src/main/sessions.ts`, `src/main/ssh-remote.ts`, `src/main/remote-sessions.ts`, the upstream Agent API response, and history tests. Focused regression check: feed local, SSH, and Remote histories containing a hidden assistant summary between real turns; assert the summary never renders while adjacent real messages and a non-hidden assistant message containing the word “summary” remain unchanged, including against a legacy database with no `display_kind` column.

## 2026-10-06 — #541 [Bug]: Voice/TTS broken when Desktop connects to remote backend (Fedora Linux)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/541
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments and distinguishes working remote STT from two broken output paths: `/voice tts` plays on the Ubuntu server, while a `text_to_speech` tool result leaves Desktop with an unplayable server-side MP3 path. Current Desktop implements voice input by POSTing the recorded clip to the selected Agent's `/api/audio/transcribe` route (`src/main/hermes.ts:428-480`) and routes Agent slash commands unchanged through `slash.exec`/`command.dispatch` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/slash/handleSlashCommand.ts:64-75`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/slashExec.ts:60-129`). It has no renderer speech-synthesis or audio playback implementation: recognized MP3/WAV/OGG paths become a download-only chip (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/mediaUtils.ts:20-25,154-166`; `src/renderer/src/components/MediaImage.tsx:140-155,197-202`). Remote path resolution calls `/api/media`, but only accepts `data:image/*` from that response (`src/main/remote-sessions.ts:185-187,558-570`), while the current Agent tree has no `/api/media` route and its API response rewriter intentionally inlines images only, leaving non-image media untouched (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/gateway/platforms/api_server.py:676-728`). The current Agent already exposes the appropriate authenticated TTS boundary—`POST /api/audio/speak` returns a base64 `data:audio/*` payload and deletes its temporary server file (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/web_server.py:4448-4510`)—but Desktop does not call it. Today's 93 focused Desktop media/slash/reconciliation tests passed; they confirm the current contract but include no remote-audio playback case.
- Inference: Input works because audio bytes are explicitly uploaded to the remote Agent, whereas output fails because `/voice` remains an Agent/platform command and generated audio remains either server-local playback or a server-local path. Desktop has neither a client playback consumer for the Agent's TTS endpoint nor a safe remote-file transport for arbitrary audio, which explains both reported symptoms without depending on Fedora audio configuration.
- Recommended action: For Desktop voice mode, call the selected connection's authenticated `/api/audio/speak` (and later `/api/audio/speak-stream`) and play the returned `data:audio/*` in the renderer; do not invoke host speakers. Separately, make generated audio delivery explicit: either extend the authenticated Agent media endpoint to return allowlisted audio MIME data from validated media roots or have the Agent inline bounded audio data, then accept `data:audio/*` in Desktop and render an `<audio controls>` element with download fallback. Expected affected files: a main-process remote-audio client, IPC/preload declarations, chat voice state/UI, `MediaImage.tsx`, `mediaUtils.ts`, and Agent media/API tests. Focused regression check: connect Desktop to a remote Agent, enable `/voice tts`, complete one response, and assert the remote endpoint returns audio that is played exactly once on the Desktop; then return `MEDIA:/remote/cache/test.mp3` and assert a playable Desktop control appears without exposing or reading an arbitrary server path.

Suggested close note: "The failing `.github/actions/hermes-smoke-test` archive member no longer exists, and the Windows updater now detects concurrent Hermes processes, pauses gateways, quarantines/retries locked shims, and stages ZIP directory replacements so WinError 5 cannot leave a half-deleted install. ## 2026-10-07 — #639 Chat transcript can lose bottom pinning during streamed tool and model output

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/639
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments and its cited implementation is still current. `useChatScroll` records every scroll position as user intent, calls `scrollIntoView({behavior: "smooth"})`, and only re-runs its follow logic when the `messages` array changes (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts:15-51`). There is no resize/intersection observer or streaming-height signal. The hook is wired only to `messages` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:347`), while the transcript also changes from independent `isLoading` and `toolProgress` props (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:980-995`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageList.tsx:68-89,171-184`). Model chunks and tool rows do replace the messages array (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatIPC.ts:135-165,310-316`), but a programmatic smooth scroll emits intermediate scroll positions; the current handler can mark those as `userScrolledUp` before the next rapid delta, causing the next follow attempt to return early. No `useChatScroll` regression test exists. Commit `9bde574d50c3a5e2df26c2f5231a21c153a74e56` adds a manual jump-to-bottom button on a side branch, but it is not an ancestor of current HEAD and does not add height-driven pinning. The 22 adjacent chat transport/input tests pass and therefore do not cover this behavior.
- Inference: The missing rendered-height observer plus the smooth-scroll/user-intent conflation makes the reported loss of bottom pinning a current deterministic design gap, even though exact timing varies by stream rate and platform. Message-array updates reduce frequency but cannot cover async layout growth or prevent a programmatic scroll event from disabling later follows.
- Recommended action: Track “pinned to bottom” from explicit user scroll intent, not intermediate programmatic animation frames, and observe transcript content/container resize. While pinned, coalesce growth into one animation-frame `scrollTop = scrollHeight` (instant during streaming); when the user deliberately scrolls above the threshold, stop following and show a keyboard-accessible Jump to latest control. Expected affected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts`, `Chat.tsx`, chat CSS/i18n, and a new hook/component test. Focused regression check: start at bottom, grow one assistant/tool row several times without changing row count and assert the sentinel remains visible; then simulate a user scroll upward, grow again, assert position is preserved and Jump to latest appears, and verify activating it resumes pinning.

## 2026-10-09 — #613 CLI/CMD windows keep randomly popping up on Windows

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/613
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report and its sole comment cover three points. First, Windows and WSL Hermes homes are intentionally separate: current discovery documents that Desktop reads the Windows-side home, audits drift, and offers a WSL-to-Windows copy fix (`src/main/wsl-detection.ts:1-13`; `src/main/config-health.ts:712-800`), matching the screenshot. Second, all shared child-process options force `windowsHide: true`, and Windows uses the GUI-subsystem `pythonw.exe` (`src/main/process-options.ts:1-7`; `src/main/installer.ts:181-194`). The specific TUI dashboard launch now uses that interpreter with hidden spawn options (`src/main/hermes.ts:669-703`); post-report commit `54fbef99cbe4469afe87ca5c40041f8c01d0fde4` removed its deliberate conversion back to `python.exe`, directly fixing the dashboard-console flash. Third, a new chat is a fresh keyed `Chat` mount with `usage` initialized to null (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:196-198,481-484`), while the gauge now uses the gateway's live `context_used/context_max`, not a cross-turn cumulative total or guessed denominator (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:673-688`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:946-959`). Post-report commits `4d799b23478fffd351ede6d8fbf251d1ced7422b` and `bb3f1bc87709861b2f75a2bf6e50ba8b45cd75c6` hardened missing usage and the denominator. Focused regressions cover hidden subprocess options, platform interpreter selection, WSL drift, and exact context occupancy/window mapping (`tests/process-options.test.ts:5-27`; `tests/installer-platform.test.ts:18-33`; `tests/sibling-hermes-home-drift.test.ts:76-102`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/usageFromPayload.test.ts:20-46`); today's aggregate passed 105/105.
- Inference: Native Windows execution is expected unless the user explicitly selects a remote execution backend; it is not intended to transparently run inside WSL. The two actual symptoms shown—an empty/flashing console and a fresh-chat gauge displaying accumulated values—have targeted fixes that landed after the report. A native Windows end-to-end retest was unavailable.
- Recommended action: Retest a current Windows build with the same Windows/WSL split, start several new chats, and leave the dashboard idle long enough to catch any console flash. Suggested close note: "Hermes Desktop intentionally runs against its Windows-side Hermes home unless a remote backend is selected; current releases now explain and audit Windows/WSL drift. The dashboard console flash was fixed by switching the remaining launch path to hidden `pythonw.exe` (`54fbef99`), and the context gauge now resets per chat and uses live `context_used/context_max`. Closing as fixed in later releases; please reopen with a current-version screen recording if either UI symptom remains."

## 2026-10-10 — #526 Wrong timestamp in Artifacts on fresh install

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/526
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has one maintainer comment identifying that the Artifacts page belongs to the upstream `NousResearch/hermes-agent` desktop rather than this standalone `fathah/hermes-desktop` repository; no Artifacts screen or route exists in this repository's current `src/` tree. The checked current Agent snapshot (`55ef425d0c3967022cb54093112e638c5c3f9e01`) still assigns an artifact the raw message/session timestamp (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/app/artifacts/artifact-utils.ts:254-280`) and renders it with `new Date(timestamp)` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/app/artifacts/index.tsx:44-46,120-137,492-494`). Agent timestamps are Unix seconds: another current conversion multiplies the same `SessionMessage.timestamp` by 1000 before constructing a `Date` (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/lib/chat-runtime.ts:338-344`), and the artifact tests themselves use second-scale values without testing display formatting (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/src/app/artifacts/index.test.ts:8-67`). A direct check shows `new Date(1770000000)` resolves to 1970 while `new Date(1770000000 * 1000)` resolves to 2026.
- Inference: Freshness is incidental; any artifact backed by the normal epoch-seconds API contract displays a date about one thousand times too early. The defect is real in the current upstream Agent UI, but cannot be fixed in this repository because the owning files are absent here.
- Recommended action: Route the issue to `NousResearch/hermes-agent`. The smallest safe upstream fix is to normalize artifact timestamps to milliseconds once at the collection/display boundary (preferably a shared seconds-to-`Date` helper) and keep numeric sorting in the original consistent unit. Affected upstream files are `apps/desktop/src/app/artifacts/artifact-utils.ts`, `apps/desktop/src/app/artifacts/index.tsx`, and `apps/desktop/src/app/artifacts/index.test.ts`. Focused regression check: feed both message and session fallback timestamps at a known Unix-second instant and assert the rendered local date matches that instant rather than 1970; also verify newest-first sorting. Do not close this issue as fixed; transfer or cross-link it upstream.

## 2026-10-11 — #1002 Remote mode with OAuth: Schedules, MCP and other screens come up empty or "not available"

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1002
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report and its follow-up comments reproduce the failure on Desktop 0.7.7 with cookie-based Remote OAuth: Schedules logs `unauthenticated` and returns an empty list, MCP reports Unauthorized, and seven screens remain behind a Remote-mode notice. Current HEAD has a partition-aware authenticated dashboard boundary in `src/main/remote-api.ts:46-89`, but Remote cron bypasses it with plain Node `fetch`, bearer headers, and the legacy `/api/jobs` path (`src/main/cronjobs.ts:193-203,255-297,377-395,411-447`); Remote MCP does the same for `/api/mcp/servers` (`src/main/mcp-servers.ts:607-644,667-677`). The renderer still disables Discover, Profiles, Providers, Skills, Memory, Kanban, and Gateway in Remote mode (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:931-1034`). Gateway detection accepts any HTTP 200 from `/health` without validating content type or body, so a dashboard SPA HTML fallback can be misclassified as the raw gateway (`src/main/hermes.ts:1069-1098`). Commit `bc58e5de2a09af3ef3286a40a9825904d0e935db` on `origin/pr-1003` implements the cron routing and `/health` validation, but it is not an ancestor of current HEAD `ba86e0f210525302359efef84987ecb7bcb29b1b`. The focused Remote/API run passed 52 tests, but none exercises cookie-only OAuth through these direct feature adapters.
- Inference: The failures are one boundary mismatch, not absent server data: OAuth credentials live in Electron's persistent session partition, while the affected feature modules use unauthenticated Node fetches. Returning `[]` on cron errors converts authentication failures into a misleading empty state. The blanket renderer gates also hide features even where the dashboard now exposes a compatible REST contract.
- Recommended action: Keep open until the linked fixes are merged and verified. The smallest safe fix is to route Remote management calls through `remoteDashboardRequestJson`, use `/api/cron/jobs` with a 404/405-only fallback to legacy `/api/jobs`, validate `/health` as a gateway response rather than accepting SPA HTML, and surface authentication errors instead of empty arrays. Enable Remote screens only where a dashboard REST contract exists. Core affected files are `src/main/cronjobs.ts`, `src/main/mcp-servers.ts`, `src/main/hermes.ts`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx`, their feature adapters, and focused tests. Regression check: with a cookie-only OAuth session and no bearer token, list cron jobs and MCP servers through the persistent partition; expire the cookie and assert a sign-in error rather than empty data; return HTML 200 from `/health` and assert gateway detection rejects it.

- Observed evidence: The report contains no version, connection mode, logs, or comments. Its screenshot shows an interrupted (`中断`) Aliyun/Qwen turn whose composer still displays the blue Stop control, and says newly entered content cannot be sent. That visual state maps directly to `isLoading`: the composer renders Stop instead of Send while it is true (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx:833-862`). The legacy main-process stream funnels response end/error, request error, and timeout through one guarded terminal callback (`src/main/hermes.ts:1561-1580,1796-1825`), which emits `chat-done` or `chat-error` to the renderer (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2006-2020,2040-2046`). Post-report commits `5df767de9` and `5225dd861` further hardened the renderer completion lifecycle. Current dashboard regressions verify that socket close and normal completion clear loading and permit another send (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.test.tsx:307-335,448-455`).
- Inference: The screenshot is consistent with a backend/socket path that marked the turn interrupted but never delivered a renderer terminal event. Normal current terminal paths recover correctly, but the report does not identify which Desktop/Agent version or Local/Remote/SSH transport took the failing path, and there is no independent renderer watchdog for a connection that remains open without emitting completion. The available evidence therefore cannot establish whether this persists or which boundary failed.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter to retest a current build and provide Desktop and Agent versions, Local/Remote/SSH mode, selected transport, the main-process log around the interruption, and whether pressing Stop restores Send. If it still reproduces, the smallest safe fix is at the identified transport boundary: every interrupted/closed/stalled stream must settle the active turn exactly once and clear loading. Expected affected files are the relevant completion adapter plus its chat hook test. Focused regression check: simulate the reported interruption/connection termination without `message.complete`, assert Stop changes back to Send, then submit a second prompt successfully. Do not close until a current retest or diagnostic identifies the terminal path.

## 2026-10-12 — #749 Hermes Desktop Bug Fix Summary（Fixed, please refer to it。）

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/749
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue reports three concrete defects, and a contributor confirmed all three against main v0.7.0. In the checked-out HEAD `42aba74068fb925e077b844926ae0874e75498ea`, completion-tail recovery still requires a byte-exact `finalText.startsWith(streamedText)` and drops the suffix after any whitespace drift (`src/main/tui-gateway-stream.ts:66-77`); database reconciliation adopts timestamp/attachments but never a longer canonical assistant body (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/sessionHistory.ts:276-310`); and chat follow uses smooth `scrollIntoView`, treats all resulting scroll events as user intent, and only reruns when the message array changes, with no existing-row resize observer (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts:21-67`). PR #1024's branch commit `b8d206bd` implements whitespace-tolerant confirmed-tail recovery and longer canonical DB content adoption, but PR #1024 remains open and that commit is not in local HEAD. PR #1028 merged today as `acaf9ea1a5ccae5933923e843cf875b469905c87`, adding persistent bottom pinning through transcript growth, but local HEAD is behind `origin/main` and does not contain that commit. The owner explicitly linked #1024 and #1028 in the issue and is waiting for the fixes before closure. Current baseline tail/reconciliation/dashboard tests pass; they do not invalidate the missing cases described above.
- Inference: The root causes are (1) an over-strict prefix comparison that assumes identical whitespace between stream and completion payloads, (2) reconciliation treating streamed content as immutable even when the canonical DB row is a strictly longer confirmed version, and (3) scroll intent and rendered-height tracking that cannot distinguish programmatic motion or react to asynchronous row growth. The issue remains confirmed for the checked source: two reply-integrity fixes are unmerged, while the scroll fix is merged upstream but not yet present locally.
- Recommended action: Merge PR #1024 for the two reply-integrity defects and update the checkout/release to include merged PR #1028; avoid a duplicate local implementation. Affected files/tests are `src/main/tui-gateway-stream.ts` and its focused test, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/sessionHistory.ts` plus `tests/reconcile-streamed-with-db.test.ts`, and `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatScroll.ts` plus its scroll regression. Focused checks: whitespace-only prefix drift yields exactly the missing suffix without duplication; a matching longer canonical assistant row replaces only the confirmed streamed prefix while divergent/user rows remain unchanged; and existing-row height growth stays pinned at the bottom until an explicit upward wheel/keyboard action, after which reading position is preserved. Keep open until #1024 merges and a packaged build containing both PRs passes those checks.

## 2026-10-13 — #1034 Fallback leaves stale applied-model cache: next SSH chat send fails with "dashboard did not switch" (v0.7.7)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1034
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments and gives the exact current error after the Agent automatically falls back from `openai-codex/gpt-6-luna` to `deepseek/deepseek-flash`. `ensureSelectedModel` reads fresh `model.options`, but only invalidates its remembered applied-model key for two custom-provider repair branches (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1880-1932`). It otherwise runs `/model` solely when `appliedModelRef.current !== key` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1936-1946`); after an Agent fallback, that key still names the user's selected model, so the switch is skipped and the fresh fallback value is validated as though it were the post-switch value (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:1948-1966`). The first failed send clears the cache and requests session recreation, which explains why retrying later succeeds (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts:2293-2344`). The existing cache regression only covers two sends where no live drift occurs (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.test.tsx:591-614`). Current HEAD `23c78fc18afc009be31067117e479652d378a167` still has this path; today's focused dashboard/Remote/discovery run passed 144 tests but has no fallback-drift case.
- Inference: `appliedModelRef` is a command-deduplication cache, but the code treats it as proof of mutable Agent state across turns. Automatic fallback breaks that invariant, producing the reported one-send failure before the catch path clears the cache.
- Recommended action: After the fresh `model.options` read, execute `/model` when either the remembered key differs **or** the live model does not match the desired selection, then refresh and validate before `prompt.submit`. The smallest affected surface is `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useDashboardChatTransport.ts` and its focused test. Regression check: apply the selected SSH model, simulate an Agent fallback before the next send, return that fallback from `model.options`, and assert the next send performs exactly one new `slash.exec`, verifies the selected model, and only then submits the prompt; if the switch is refused, assert the prompt is not submitted and the draft remains retryable.

The legacy health and chat paths derive the same saved Remote URL/auth headers (`src/main/hermes.ts:130-225,1094-1127,1729-1737`), and current connection validation also checks authenticated `/v1/models` rather than trusting a public health response (`src/main/hermes.ts:3845-3914`; `tests/remote-mode-url-and-spawn.test.ts:759-780`). However, both observable signatures have targeted post-report fixes: a temporarily down tunnel no longer traps Remote startup, and a raw gateway tunnel without dashboard `/api/ws` now falls back immediately to the same `/v1/chat/completions` path the attachment proved healthy. This is strong source-and-regression evidence, not a current packaged Windows/SSH reproduction.
- Recommended action: Retest with a current Desktop/Agent while using the same tunnel. Suggested close note: "Current releases no longer block Remote users on a failed startup probe, use the saved Remote URL/key consistently for health and chat, and immediately fall back to `/v1/chat/completions` when a gateway-only tunnel has no dashboard WebSocket. Focused regressions pass. Closing as fixed in later releases; please reopen with current Desktop/Agent versions and the main-process log if it persists."

## 2026-10-14 — #163 5 vulnerabilities (2 moderate, 3 high)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/163
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report has no comments and records an `npm install` audit of the v0.4.0 development tree; it does not name the five advisories or claim an exploit in the packaged app. Against the current lockfile, `npm audit --json` reports 16 findings (12 moderate, 4 high), while `npm audit --omit=dev --json` reports **zero** production findings across 250 production dependencies. Every currently reported node is therefore in the development/build graph. Direct parents include Vitest and its coverage package (`package.json:76,93`; locked at 4.1.8 in `package-lock.json:3908-3970`) and electron-builder (`package.json:78`; locked at 26.15.3 in `package-lock.json:5844-5879`); affected transitives include `brace-expansion`, `http-cache-semantics`, `source-map-js`, and `undici`. The current production-audit command is explicitly defined at `package.json:11`. The current advisories are not necessarily the same five reported in May, but the broad claim that a clean source install has audit findings remains directly reproducible today.
- Inference: The packaged runtime is not shown to contain these findings, so the issue's current security impact is build/test-tool exposure rather than an established end-user vulnerability. The nonzero full audit is still actionable: compromised or attacker-controlled inputs reaching test mocking, packaging downloads, glob/brace parsing, or build-time HTTP paths can affect contributors and CI.
- Recommended action: Keep the issue open but retitle/scope it to development and packaging dependencies. Update the direct Vitest/coverage, electron-builder, lint/build parents and regenerated lockfile to versions whose transitives clear the cited advisories; avoid blind forced major upgrades or unverified overrides. Affected files are `package.json` and `package-lock.json`. Focused regression check: from a clean checkout run a frozen install, full `npm audit`, `npm audit --omit=dev`, all tests, and unpacked Windows/macOS/Linux packaging; require both audits to be clean or document a time-bounded exception with reachability analysis.

## 2026-10-14 — #306 [Bug] Windows: Sub-agents and conversation history lost on restart due to process.env.HOME fallback to /tmp

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/306
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: Two comments refine and corroborate the report: Desktop supplies `HOME`, so the practical Windows drift is `%USERPROFILE%\\.hermes` versus Desktop's resolved `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\hermes`, not normally the literal `/tmp` fallback. Current Desktop independently resolves its real data root into exported `HERMES_HOME` (`src/main/installer.ts:59-73,85-95,192-197`), but `startAdapter` passes `HOME: homedir()` and does not pass that resolved `HERMES_HOME` (`src/main/claw3d.ts:945-974`). The current Hermes Office adapter still reads `HOME || "/tmp"`, derives config from `${HOME}/.hermes`, and persists history under that directory (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-office/server/hermes-gateway-adapter.js:64,69,243-250`); the current upstream file read today has the same contract. Existing command-resolution tests verify Windows invocation shape but never assert the adapter's data-root environment (`tests/claw3d-command-resolution.test.ts:10-119`). All 128 adjacent Office/SSH tests passed, so none currently detects this cross-process path drift.
- Inference: On a default Windows install where Desktop selects `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\hermes`, Office still writes its registry/history under `%USERPROFILE%\\.hermes`; restarting cannot recover state from the Desktop-owned home. Changing only the `HOME` fallback to `USERPROFILE` would preserve the same wrong directory and would not fix the confirmed case.
- Recommended action: Make Hermes Office resolve one explicit `HERMES_HOME` (falling back to `${HOME}/.hermes`) for config, registry, history, and workspaces, and have Desktop pass its imported `HERMES_HOME` explicitly in the adapter spawn environment. Expected affected files are `src/main/claw3d.ts`, the Hermes Office `server/hermes-gateway-adapter.js`, and a spawn/persistence regression. Focused regression check: simulate Windows with `homedir() = C:\\Users\\alice` and `HERMES_HOME = C:\\Users\\alice\\AppData\\Local\\hermes`, create an Office agent and history, restart the adapter, and assert both reload from the latter directory with no writes under `C:\\Users\\alice\\.hermes`.

## 2026-10-14 — #358 SSH Tunnel mode only sends GET /health, never POST /v1/chat/completions

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/358
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report's three comments reproduce the old failure on macOS and Windows with nginx/Docker-backed gateways: the tunnel's `/health` was 200, but the Desktop's pidfile/systemd status probe declared the gateway stopped, ran a repair sequence, and could replace the cached bearer with an empty file read before chat. Current default-profile status now supplies the configured remote port and falls back to a remote loopback `/health` probe whenever the pidfile is absent or its PID is outside the host namespace (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2157-2216`), with an exact container-pid regression (`tests/ssh-remote.test.ts:228-245`); commit `9132fd38b15cf5065cb8dabc093fcad2adb1897a` added that behavior after the report and is in current HEAD. The current gateway branch no longer calls the lossy `sshReadRemoteApiKey`: it transactionally preserves or generates a non-placeholder `API_SERVER_KEY`, ensures `API_SERVER_ENABLED`, waits for `/health` only when a start/restart was actually needed, opens the tunnel, and caches the guaranteed nonempty returned key (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:631-663`; `src/main/ssh-env-update.ts:99-138`; `src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2423-2488`). The focused Office/SSH/Remote run passed 128 tests, including API-key provisioning, health fallback, Docker targeting, and Remote transport coverage.
- Inference: The current health fallback recognizes the reporter's nginx-on-8642 setup as already running even without `hermes.service` or `gateway.pid`, so it skips the destructive repair path; the transactional key path also removes the empty-key overwrite mechanism. A packaged Windows/macOS connection to that exact VPS was not available, so this is source-and-regression evidence rather than a live reproduction.
- Recommended action: Close after one current packaged-build smoke test against a gateway exposed through nginx or Docker without a host-visible pidfile. Suggested close note: "Current SSH transport treats a successful loopback `/health` probe as gateway liveness when systemd/pidfile detection cannot see the process, and it now provisions/preserves the API key transactionally instead of caching an empty read. Focused nginx/Docker/SSH regressions pass. Closing as fixed in later releases; please reopen with current Desktop/Agent versions and the main-process log if no POST reaches the gateway."

## 2026-10-15 — #364 [Bug] Desktop updater should gracefully stop server.py before syncing updates to avoid aiohttp WinError 5 file lock corruption on Windows

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/364
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The open report has no visible comments and describes a Desktop-triggered Windows update corrupting `aiohttp` when a live venv process keeps `_http_parser*.pyd` locked. Desktop still delegates the local action directly to `hermes update` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:906-989`; `src/main/installer.ts:810-867`), but the checked current Agent snapshot `55ef425d0c3967022cb54093112e638c5c3f9e01` now owns the required safety boundary. It identifies venv-backed Desktop/server processes that can hold `.pyd` modules (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:10218-10331`), stops discovered Windows gateways before mutation and records enough state to relaunch them (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:10334-10455,10564-10627`), and invokes that pause before dependency work (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:10785-10809`). If a non-gateway Desktop backend still holds the venv, it now aborts before sync with an actionable close-the-app message instead of risking a partial uninstall (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:10794-10809`). It also stages ZIP directory replacements before swapping (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py:6802-6836,6915-6929`). Dedicated tests cover gateway pause/resume, concurrent-process refusal, quarantine/reboot fallback, and preservation of the old tree after simulated WinError 5 (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/hermes_cli/test_update_concurrent_quarantine.py:345-447,451-598,719-758`; `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/hermes_cli/test_update_zip_atomic_replace.py:1-80`). The Agent tests could not be executed because its venv lacks pytest; source and regression coverage were inspected directly.
- Inference: The exact silent-corruption mechanism is no longer reachable through the normal current updater: gateways are paused/restarted, and any remaining venv lock holder makes the update fail safely before dependency replacement. A native Windows packaged update was not available, so this is “appears fixed” rather than a live reproduction.
- Recommended action: Smoke-test one Desktop-triggered update on Windows with a running gateway and close if the gateway returns healthy afterward and imports remain intact. Suggested close note: "Current Hermes Agent now pauses and relaunches Windows gateways before updating, refuses dependency sync while another Desktop/venv process still holds native modules, and stages ZIP replacements so a failed copy cannot leave a half-deleted install. Closing as apparently fixed; please reopen with the current full update log if a Desktop-triggered update still produces WinError 5 or a broken `aiohttp`."

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/348
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report targets v0.5.0 on macOS Intel. Its discussion identified the actual failure as the x64 package carrying an arm64 `better-sqlite3` binary rather than a normal WAL lock, and the reporter confirmed a local build with PR #202's changes. PR #202 merged as `329fc22ab74cbb96bdf030f30af6d1b2e93becf8` on 2026-05-24 and is an ancestor of current HEAD.
