The release workflow builds macOS x64 and arm64 separately, rebuilds native dependencies for each target, packages the requested architecture, and verifies the packaged `better-sqlite3` binary (`.github/workflows/release.yml:63-86,119-133`; `scripts/verify-native-module-architecture.sh:1-25`; `electron-builder.yml:21-23`). Today's focused run passed 79 Sessions/cache/API-key tests.
- Inference: Both causes of the observed infinite spinner are absent from current source: the packaged native module is architecture-checked, and an IPC/database failure cannot leave `loading` set forever. This is source/test evidence; the original v0.5.0 Intel package was not rerun.
- Recommended action: Close as fixed by PR #202. Suggested close note: "Fixed by PR #202 (`329fc22a`): macOS releases now rebuild and verify `better-sqlite3` for each target architecture, while Sessions always releases loading and falls back to its cache after a sync failure. Closing as fixed; please reopen with a current version and logs if the spinner returns."

## 2026-10-15 — #454 发第二次报错Error: Session continuation requires API key authentication. Configure API_SERVER_KEY to enable this feature.

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/454
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report and two corroborating comments describe the second message failing across providers with the gateway's unauthenticated-session-continuation error. Current key handling repairs many historical installs: it resolves six key locations including `api_server.token`, migrates non-canonical keys to `.env`, and injects the resolved key into the gateway process (`src/main/config.ts:1623-1764,1830-1875`; `src/main/hermes.ts:3514-3545`). Those fixes are in current HEAD (`094954ac`, `ab0a323e`, and merge `44230834`). However, a genuinely keyless local profile remains explicitly supported as a detectable warning (`src/main/config-health.ts:232-252`), and the chat path still adds the resumed `session_id` to the JSON body before auth is known (`src/main/hermes.ts:1495-1503`) and unconditionally prefers `_resumeSessionId` for `X-Hermes-Session-Id` even when `hasAuth` is false (`src/main/hermes.ts:1537-1549`). The exact defensive fix exists in side-branch commit `59ab27b86feb76e9acb8a772138b6b1d6c7db06a`, which gates resumed session IDs on auth and adds tests, but that commit is not an ancestor of current HEAD. Today's 79 focused tests passed, but the existing API test fixture supplies auth and does not exercise a resumed keyless request.
- Inference: Setup-generated keys and migration reduce how often users enter this state, but they do not remove the current deterministic failure: if all key sources are empty, the first anonymous send can work and the second resumed send carries a continuation identifier without bearer auth, so the gateway returns the reported error. Provider choice is irrelevant because the failure is gateway session authorization.
- Recommended action: Restore the auth gate from `59ab27b8` and apply it consistently to both the JSON `session_id` and `X-Hermes-Session-Id`; also ensure the normal local onboarding/startup path creates a persistent `API_SERVER_KEY` before enabling authenticated continuation. The smallest affected surface is `src/main/hermes.ts` plus `tests/hermes-api.test.ts`. Focused regression check: with every API-server-key source empty, make a second send with a resume ID and assert neither continuation field is sent and no 403 loop occurs; with a configured key, assert both fields and the bearer are preserved so the session genuinely continues.

## 2026-10-16 — #160 v0.3.7 macOS DMG installs unsigned x86_64 app on Apple Silicon; arm64 ZIP works

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/160
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The original report identified an unsigned x86_64 v0.3.7 DMG, and a 2026-08-16 comment reported that the v0.7.6 arm64 ZIP still failed strict signature and Gatekeeper checks despite matching GitHub's published bytes. The same defect reproduces on the latest v0.7.7 arm64 ZIP: the downloaded asset's local SHA-256 exactly matches GitHub's asset digest (`b9a867f66ac5861fe5daa607ac37e9f7d47e66c503922f5f9b318f4b6b66ecbc`), its executable is arm64, but both native `ditto` and `unzip` extraction yield an app for which `codesign --verify --deep --strict --verbose=4` reports `invalid signature (code or signature have been modified) In architecture: arm64`; `spctl --assess --type execute -vv` also fails. The bundle reports hardened runtime, Team ID `WM6645CA5X`, and a stapled ticket, so notarization metadata alone is not evidence of a valid final signature. The release workflow imports a Developer ID identity, packages separate x64/arm64 outputs, then deletes the temporary keychain and checks only the packaged `better-sqlite3` architecture before upload (`.github/workflows/release.yml:63-71,96-142`; `scripts/import-macos-certificate.sh:29-69`; `scripts/verify-native-module-architecture.sh:1-25`). Signing/notarization is configured in `electron-builder.yml:40-57`, but neither the workflow nor `tests/release-artifacts.test.ts` and `tests/macos-signing.test.ts` verifies the final ZIP/DMG with codesign, stapler, or Gatekeeper. Post-report signing hardening commits `56e29f9a`, `bb74e079`, `12b62091`, and `169b191b` are all in v0.7.7, so they did not close the observable failure.
- Inference: The proven root cause at the release-system boundary is that signature-invalid packages can be published because the final artifacts are never cryptographically or Gatekeeper-verified. The exact post-sign byte mutation or signing-stage failure cannot be determined from the repository alone. `build/afterPack.js:20-51` performs ad-hoc nested/outer-app signing if invoked, but no explicit active hook was found in `package.json` or `electron-builder.yml`, so it should be audited rather than assumed to be the culprit.
- Recommended action: Keep open and block macOS uploads on a post-package gate. Extract each final ZIP with `ditto`, mount each DMG, and require `codesign --verify --deep --strict --verbose=4`, `xcrun stapler validate`, `spctl --assess --type execute -vv`, and the expected executable architecture before publishing; retain signing logs and investigate any mutation after Developer ID signing. If the ad-hoc `afterPack` signer is active, remove its outer-app re-sign and let Electron Builder sign nested code and the app once before notarization. Expected affected files are `.github/workflows/release.yml`, the beta release workflow, a new focused verification script and tests, and possibly `build/afterPack.js` after its activation path is established. Regression check: package x64 and arm64 ZIP/DMG assets, require all signature/notarization/Gatekeeper checks to pass, then mutate `Info.plist` or `app.asar` and assert the upload gate fails.

## 2026-10-16 — #452 [Bug] RTL/LTR mixed text direction rendered incorrectly in prompt/text input

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/452
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and reports that Arabic/English mixed text is scrambled while composing. The live composer textarea has no `dir` attribute (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx:765-782`), and `.chat-input` defines neither `direction` nor `unicode-bidi` (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:6267-6284`). It therefore inherits the application root direction, which `I18nProvider` derives from the selected interface locale (`src/renderer/src/components/I18nProvider.tsx:18-24`); an English UI forces the composer into an LTR paragraph even for Arabic-first text. Sent prose has separately acquired `dir="auto"` and per-block direction handling (`src/renderer/src/components/AgentMarkdown.tsx:217-225,258-283`; commits `3a0331ba` and `6a829596`), but that does not affect the pre-send textarea. `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.test.tsx:51-72` renders this control, yet its current tests have no direction or bidirectional-text assertion. The focused ChatInput suite passes, confirming only the currently covered behavior; the exact visual ordering was not independently screenshot-tested today.
- Inference: The root cause is that the composer delegates paragraph direction to the UI locale rather than the entered text. Browser bidirectional layout then applies an unsuitable LTR base direction to an RTL-first mixed prompt; the already-fixed sent-message renderer cannot repair the text while it is being authored.
- Recommended action: Add `dir="auto"` to the textarea and use `unicode-bidi: plaintext` with logical `text-align: start` on `.chat-input`, avoiding a globally hard-coded RTL direction. Expected affected files are `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css`, `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/ChatInput.test.tsx`, and the chat-input design note if implemented. Regression check: in real Chromium, under an English UI, an Arabic-first mixed prompt computes RTL and an English-first prompt computes LTR; multiline paragraphs receive plaintext bidi handling, and submitting/copying preserves the exact input string. Add a unit assertion that the textarea owns `dir="auto"`.

## 2026-10-16 — #285 SSH-mode Gateway Start collides with systemd hermes.service → infinite crash-loop

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/285
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report and its comment identify a default-profile SSH collision where Desktop launched a nohup gateway while `hermes.service` already owned the same port. PR #304 merged as `5dc7de0411b54f4745b8fd09b37af998a0106e8a` (implementation commit `cd00a4ec57178ce44ab4af02c7f044a609969a36`) and is an ancestor of current HEAD. Current SSH management explicitly documents #285, detects a configured systemd unit, and routes default-profile start, stop, and status through systemd instead of the nohup/pidfile path (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2050-2057,2065-2067,2091-2154,2157-2195`). Its callers use that shared status/start/stop contract (`src/main/ssh-remote.ts:2198-2258`). The focused regression asserts that the systemd branch uses `systemctl` and does not invoke nohup, kill, or pidfile logic (`tests/ssh-remote.test.ts:184-226`), and it passes today. Named SSH profiles intentionally keep their independent gateway process because the host's `hermes.service` represents the default profile.
- Inference: The collision mechanism described by the issue is removed for the affected default-profile path: once the unit exists, Desktop cannot start the competing nohup process. This conclusion is based on merged source and focused regression coverage rather than a live reproduction on the reporter's host.
- Recommended action: Close as fixed by PR #304. Suggested close note: "Fixed by PR #304 (`5dc7de04`; implementation `cd00a4ec`): SSH default-profile gateway start, stop, and status now detect `hermes.service`, use systemd when present, and never start a nohup fallback in that branch. Focused regressions pass. Closing as fixed; please reopen with current SSH logs if the unit still crash-loops."

The exact installer commit pinned by Desktop is `503d863fcd2cbfc0be5a6d6c536fae2e98aa4204`, but a directly installed or subsequently managed Agent can expose the newer staged layout reported here.
- Inference: This is a compatibility-boundary defect, not a broken Agent install. Desktop equates “usable Hermes” with one historical filesystem layout instead of resolving the Agent's authoritative launcher/active runtime; the healthy CLI therefore cannot satisfy onboarding, status, doctor, version, or downstream subprocess paths.
- Recommended action: Introduce one security-conscious invocation resolver shared by adoption, status, verification, version/doctor, and feature subprocesses. It should retain the legacy venv path, recognize official managed-install metadata or a verified Hermes-owned launcher, resolve the active Python runtime without guessing hash directories, and reject arbitrary lookalike executables. Expected affected files start with `src/main/installer.ts`, its callers that directly consume `HERMES_PYTHON`/`hermesCliArgs`, and installer/adoption tests. Focused regression check: build a Debian fixture with the reported checked-in entry point, non-symlink public launcher, and only a staged managed venv; assert adoption and restart succeed, `--version`/`doctor` use that runtime, and a similarly shaped untrusted folder is rejected.

## 2026-10-19 — #458 Windows: Cannot detect or run Hermes installed in WSL2

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/458
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and its two-part diagnosis still matches current source. On Windows, Desktop derives only the native `hermes-agent/venv/Scripts/pythonw.exe` and `Scripts/hermes.exe` paths, while existing-home validation checks the same native layout and install status gates on those files (`src/main/installer.ts:192-245,522-552`). The WSL module explicitly exists only to discover sibling homes for configuration-drift diagnostics; it returns UNC paths for Node file access and does not build executable invocations (`src/main/wsl-detection.ts:1-13,21-40,71-130`). Runtime consumers still execute `HERMES_PYTHON` directly—for example profile creation (`src/main/profiles.ts:296-314`) and dashboard startup (`src/main/dashboard.ts:748-770`)—and the same pattern remains across `cronjobs.ts`, `hermes-auth.ts`, `hermes.ts`, `kanban.ts`, `mcp-servers.ts`, `model-discovery.ts`, and `skills.ts`. Today's focused platform/adoption/WSL suites pass, but they cover native layouts and Windows↔WSL config copying, not running a WSL Agent from the Windows app.
- Inference: A normal WSL-only install is rejected before adoption; merely accepting its Unix `venv/bin/python` over `\\wsl$` would move the failure to process launch because Windows cannot execute the Linux ELF directly. Current WSL diagnostics are coexistence support, not a WSL execution backend, so the requested use case remains unsupported.
- Recommended action: Introduce one typed Hermes-runtime resolver used by validation and every local CLI/process caller. A WSL runtime should persist the distro plus Linux home, validate through `wsl.exe -d <distro> -- test -x ...`, translate working directory/environment paths, and return a `wsl.exe -d … -- <linux command>` invocation; native resolution must remain unchanged. Expected affected files start with `src/main/installer.ts` and `src/main/wsl-detection.ts`, followed by the direct consumers listed above. Focused regression check: under a simulated Windows host, adopt a discovered Ubuntu home, assert version/doctor/profile/gateway/dashboard commands all route through the same distro-qualified `wsl.exe` invocation with Linux paths, and assert native Windows installs retain their current command/arguments while an inaccessible distro fails before adoption.

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/459
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report and its only comment describe one visible conversation silently becoming many server sessions and the Agent answering from unrelated context. Current API chat explicitly reuses the supplied session id in both the request body and `X-Hermes-Session-Id`; a fresh chat instead gets a unique `desk-<timestamp>-<uuid>` id so equal first prompts cannot collide with the Agent's fingerprint-derived session id (`src/main/hermes.ts:1472-1501,1520-1558`). Main-process IPC announces that id as soon as a turn starts (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:2033-2066`), while the renderer scopes the announcement to the originating run, persists it, and only refreshes DB history for the accepted id (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/hooks/useChatIPC.ts:64-79,84-135`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/Chat.tsx:190-206,626-643`). The focused regressions assert that a resumed CLI session survives a cold API start and that fresh chats receive different ids instead of colliding (`tests/hermes-cli-session-id.test.ts:361-403`; `tests/hermes-api.test.ts:200-250`). The relevant fixes include `317ec5f59e98fab31385fc7b978e317e59645b67` (cold-start session continuity), `3bafc8738f69572228ca6a39e15719301d737c40` (per-chat ids), and the later first-turn announcement fix `ed82553889ffb0571dc5a7f34c76c0088057c0c0`; today's focused chat/provider/dashboard run passes all 185 tests after rerunning loopback fixtures outside the sandbox.
- Inference: The original install and state database are unavailable, so the precise trigger—cold CLI/API handoff, first-prompt id collision, or renderer session adoption—cannot be isolated. All three boundaries now preserve or uniquely allocate one session id and have focused coverage, so the reported multi-session/context-switch symptom appears fixed in current source rather than currently reproducible.
- Recommended action: Retest one long current-version chat with Memory recall both off and on, and verify the same session id remains selected across several tool calls and a gateway restart. Suggested close note: "Current releases preserve one session id across cold CLI/API handoff and resumed turns, generate collision-resistant ids for fresh chats, and bind session updates to the originating chat run; focused continuity regressions pass. Closing as fixed in later releases. Please reopen with current Desktop/Agent versions and the affected session ids if context still switches mid-chat."

## 2026-10-23 — #592 App crashes on startup with virtual display adapters — "GPU process isn't usable"

- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The v0.5.8 report and a later independent Windows 11 comment reproduce the same GameViewer/indirect-display crash signature: repeated GPU-process exit `-2147483645` followed by Chromium's fatal `GPU process isn't usable`. Commit `8bf2fe0f6c1c974b08e33178982956bc3f84ea28` landed the next day and is contained in v0.5.9 onward. Current startup applies GPU preferences and installs the crash guard before `startMainProcess` (`src/main/index.ts:1-22`). The guard listens for an abnormal Electron `child-process-gone` event of type `GPU`, persists a fallback flag, and relaunches once with `--hermes-gpu-disabled` (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:305-349`); the relaunched process disables hardware acceleration and GPU compositing before app readiness while retaining SwiftShader (`src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:173-209`). - Inference: The exact Todesk/GameViewer Windows setup was not available for a packaged smoke test, so this is source-and-regression evidence rather than a native reproduction. The current fallback directly targets the reported fatal GPU-process sequence and no longer relies on the ineffective external flag combinations in the issue; at worst the first launch crashes and automatically restarts in software-rendered mode.
- Recommended action: Ask the reporter or the later commenter to launch the current package with GameViewer's virtual adapter enabled and confirm one automatic recovery followed by a usable window; then close if successful. Suggested close note: "Current releases detect an abnormal Electron GPU-process exit before Chromium reaches its fatal retry limit, persist a temporary software-rendering fallback, and relaunch once with hardware acceleration disabled. This fix landed in `8bf2fe0f6`, with regressions for the fallback and crash-loop guard. Please retest with the virtual adapter enabled; closing as apparently fixed, and we can reopen with current logs if the recovery relaunch also fails."

Suggested close note: "DashScope routing was corrected after this report in `54d36cbf`: both regional Aliyun endpoints now use the Agent's native `alibaba` provider and `DASHSCOPE_API_KEY`, with legacy aliases mapped to the same credential. - URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1044
- Classification: **LIKELY**

This repository's current window enables vibrancy and transparent backing only on macOS (`src/main/app/start.ts:169-206`), and the renderer adds `shell-vibrant` only for macOS/main (`src/renderer/src/App.tsx:262-275`; `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:579-605`), so its Linux path does not explain the report. The exact upstream v0.22.5 source does: `apps/shared/src/translucency.ts:133-149,196-204,225-247,274-293` marks Linux unsupported but still gives its first-launch fallback the macOS intensity 29 in clear mode; `apps/shared/src/translucency.ts:382-409` maps that to native opacity about 0.941; and `apps/desktop/electron/main.ts:1492-1513,1601-1640` unconditionally spreads those opacity options into every chat-window constructor despite the separate Linux support verdict. Upstream tests assert Linux is unsupported and that the Linux-shaped default falls back to clear, but never assert that such a window omits opacity (`apps/desktop/electron/translucency.test.ts:327-338,494-516,604-624`).
- Inference: The issue was filed in the wrong repository, but the matching v0.22.5 code has a platform-gating gap that plausibly produces exactly the reported Ubuntu translucency whenever that Electron/Wayland/X11 combination honors constructor opacity despite the source comments assuming Linux is a no-op. The sparse report prevents upgrading this from likely to confirmed on a specific compositor.
- Recommended action: Route the defect to `NousResearch/hermes-agent` and ask for Ubuntu version, Wayland/X11/compositor, screenshot, `translucency.json`, and launch logs. The smallest upstream fix is to gate both constructor `windowOpacityOptions` and runtime `applyWindowOpacity` on `TRANSLUCENCY_SUPPORTED` (or force unsupported platforms to an opaque zero-intensity state) in `apps/desktop/electron/main.ts`, with the shared helper only if the platform belongs in its API. Regression check: for Linux with no persisted setting and with a stale non-zero clear setting, assert every chat-window option omits `opacity`, runtime never calls `setOpacity`, and the rendered backing remains opaque.

## 2026-10-24 — #536 Installation fails on Windows 11 with Smart App Control enabled

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/536
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report supplies Windows 11 25H2, SAC enabled, error 4551 at the dependencies stage, and the blocked venv interpreter; five later comments independently confirm the failure through 2026-10-05, including after auto-update and after antivirus exclusions. Current Desktop still routes Windows bootstrap to PowerShell (`src/main/installer.ts:930-964,1090-1162`), downloads and verifies the pinned upstream `install.ps1`, and passes a per-user `~\.hermes\hermes-agent` install directory (`src/main/installer-windows-download.ts:12-38`; `src/main/installer-download.ts:1-6`). That exact pinned script at commit `503d863fcd2cbfc0be5a6d6c536fae2e98aa4204` creates the venv with `uv venv` and then executes `venv\Scripts\python.exe` during later stages (`scripts/install.ps1:2408-2428,2545-2565` upstream). The repo's Windows package is per-user (`electron-builder.yml:24-39`), and its Windows release job has no Authenticode certificate/signing setup (`.github/workflows/release.yml:210-246`); neither current installer error mapping nor focused tests recognize SAC/4551 (`src/main/installer-errors.ts:1-10`; `tests/installer-recovery.test.ts:52-79`). Today's four focused installer suites passed 8/8 tests, confirming the generic current flow but not compatibility with SAC.
- Inference: SAC is blocking executable code provisioned into a user-writable runtime tree, and the current source retains that trust boundary without a signed runtime/package path. `ExecutionPolicy Bypass` affects PowerShell policy only and cannot bypass WDAC/SAC; signing only the outer Electron installer would likewise not establish trust for the downloaded Python runtime and native extensions. The repeated native reports make this confirmed even though this macOS host cannot enable SAC.
- Recommended action: Do not advise disabling SAC. Add a supported signed Windows distribution path: package a trusted/signature-verified runtime and native binaries through a signed MSIX/MSI or equivalent protected installation, and configure release CI to fail unless every executable payload verifies under the intended Authenticode chain. As an immediate diagnostic improvement, detect error 4551 and explain that SAC blocked an unsigned payload, without claiming antivirus exclusions or elevation will fix it. Expected affected surfaces are `src/main/installer.ts`, `src/main/installer-errors.ts`, `electron-builder.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, the upstream Windows bootstrap/runtime packaging, and focused installer/release tests. Regression check: on a clean SAC-enabled Windows 11 VM, install/update successfully, verify payload signatures, run the venv probe and one native extension, and separately simulate 4551 to assert a specific failure is surfaced rather than success or disable-SAC guidance.

Current `runInstall` returns through `runInstallWindows` before reaching the bash path (`src/main/installer.ts:930-964`), stages the verified pinned PowerShell script and invokes `powershell.exe` (`src/main/installer.ts:1090-1162`; `src/main/installer-windows-download.ts:12-38`), and rejects nonzero exits when no installed runtime exists (`src/main/installer.ts:1172-1214`). Windows paths are platform-specific (`src/main/installer.ts:198-215`), and regressions cover the path contract, verified PowerShell bootstrap, nonzero platform failure, and refusal to mask verification failure behind older binaries (`tests/installer-platform.test.ts:10-33`; `tests/installer-windows-download.test.ts:9-33`; `tests/installer-recovery.test.ts:52-90`; `tests/installer-windows-verification-result.test.ts:29-47`). Today's focused run passed all 8 tests.
- Inference: The v0.3.5 failure mechanism no longer exists: fresh Windows installation does not invoke bash, and a failed PowerShell install with no runtime cannot emit the reported success. This is source/history/test evidence; a packaged Windows smoke test was not run on this host, and separate SAC failures tracked in #536 are not a recurrence of the hardcoded-bash bug.
- Recommended action: Close as fixed in v0.3.6+. Suggested close note: "Fixed by `e2f544a6` and retained in current releases: Windows now takes a dedicated verified PowerShell bootstrap path, uses Windows venv locations, and rejects installer failures instead of reporting success on a fresh install. Focused installer regressions pass. Closing as fixed; please open a new issue with current logs for any different Windows bootstrap failure."

## 2026-10-25 — #149 Version 0.3.6 Windows PC restart repeats installation

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/149
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The v0.3.6 log is a PowerShell parser cascade and visibly contains a corrupted non-ASCII dash (`�?"`) inside the downloaded script. That tag downloaded UTF-8 `install.ps1` with `Invoke-RestMethod -OutFile` and executed it directly under PowerShell 5.1, with no BOM normalization (`v0.3.6:src/main/installer.ts:614-646`). Current bootstrap first verifies the pinned upstream bytes, then explicitly reads them as UTF-8, removes any existing BOM, rewrites UTF-8 with BOM, and only then invokes the file (`src/main/installer-windows-download.ts:12-38`); the comment identifies PowerShell 5.1 as the compatibility reason. Commit `f272ee489` added this verified conversion and is an ancestor of HEAD, though it is not yet contained in a release tag. The focused regression locks the hash-before-convert-before-execute order and BOM write (`tests/installer-windows-download.test.ts:9-33`), while `tests/installer-windows-verification-result.test.ts:29-47` prevents a failed verified bootstrap from being reported as success. Today's four focused suites passed 14/14 tests.
- Inference: The reported repeat-install loop followed from the first install never completing because Windows PowerShell 5.1 decoded a BOM-less UTF-8 script with the legacy code page, corrupting punctuation and breaking parsing. The current staging boundary removes that deterministic encoding failure while preserving integrity verification. A native Windows PowerShell 5.1 package run remains prudent because the fix is not in a tagged release yet.
- Recommended action: Ship the current bootstrap change, then test installation and restart on Windows 10/11 using Windows PowerShell 5.1 with a non-UTF-8 system code page. Verify the script parses, the venv is detected after reboot, and onboarding does not restart. Suggested close note: "Current source fixes the v0.3.6 PowerShell 5.1 encoding failure: the pinned installer is checksum-verified, decoded as UTF-8, rewritten with a BOM, and only then executed. Regression tests also prevent a failed verification from appearing successful. Closing after this fix ships and passes a PowerShell 5.1 non-UTF-8-locale smoke test."

## 2026-10-26 — #368 OAuth sign-in fails in SSH Tunnel mode by spawning local Hermes Python

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/368
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report's `ENOENT` path matches the current implementation exactly. The renderer calls `oauthLogin(provider, profile)` without a connection target (`src/renderer/src/components/OAuthLoginModal.tsx:36-58`; `src/preload/index.ts:246-257`), and the IPC handler unconditionally calls `runHermesAuthLogin` without inspecting connection mode (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1008-1038`). That function always spawns the desktop-local `HERMES_PYTHON`, uses local `HERMES_REPO`, and exports the desktop user's `HOME`/`HERMES_HOME` (`src/main/hermes-auth.ts:86-128`); those constants resolve to the local installation tree (`src/main/installer.ts:192-218,233-237`). In contrast, the adjacent status handler is explicitly SSH-aware and reads the remote dashboard or remote `auth.json` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1040-1069`), so SSH can display remote auth state while the Sign in action still writes locally or fails locally. The current tests pin local spawning and named-profile arguments but contain no SSH login case (`tests/hermes-auth.test.ts:157-243`). Today's focused four-suite run passed 31/31 tests, confirming the implemented behavior rather than covering this missing route.
- Inference: A remote-only SSH deployment with no local Agent deterministically raises the reported local-path `ENOENT`; if a local Agent happens to exist, sign-in can misleadingly succeed while storing the token on the wrong machine. The root cause is that provider OAuth mutation is not target-aware even though provider status reads are.
- Recommended action: The smallest safe fix is to fail closed in SSH mode: pass an explicit connection ID through `Providers`/`OAuthLoginModal`/preload, resolve that immutable target in IPC, disable or reject Sign in for SSH with a copyable `hermes -p <profile> auth add <provider> --type oauth` remote command, and never fall through to the local process. A fuller fix can add a cancellable, streamed SSH auth runner using the existing remote Hermes launcher resolution, but it must preserve the captured connection/profile across awaits and handle browser/device-code flows explicitly. Expected files are `src/renderer/src/screens/Providers/Providers.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/components/OAuthLoginModal.tsx`, `src/preload/index.ts`, `src/preload/index.d.ts`, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`, and an SSH auth helper in `src/main/ssh-remote.ts` or `src/main/hermes-auth.ts`. Focused regression: with an SSH connection selected and local `HERMES_PYTHON` absent, clicking Codex Sign in must never call local `spawn`; it must either invoke the captured remote target/profile or return the actionable SSH-mode refusal, and switching connections mid-flight must not retarget it.

## 2026-10-26 — #300 Session ID Lost When Switching Windows/Tabs in Hermes Desktop

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/300
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The collaborator comment identifies two concrete causes and PRs: desktop session-state synchronization (#291) and the gateway requiring `X-Hermes-Session-Id` rather than only a body field (#293). Both merged after the report as commits `612f874ead0c2919fbb25d630acb898774424108` and `8795d9aea31ecd56e240944a44c636fff8c77f69`, and both are contained in v0.5.0 and every later stable tag. Current legacy API sends always retain or mint a session ID in `X-Hermes-Session-Id` and announce it back to the renderer (`src/main/hermes.ts:1500-1559`); the focused request regression asserts the resume header (`tests/hermes-api.test.ts:194-218`). Current UI architecture gives each tab/run an immutable connection/profile and its own session ID (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/chatRuns.ts:11-27`), patches only the reporting run (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:304-313`), keeps every Chat mounted while hiding inactive tabs, and passes that run's session back into its Chat (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/Layout.tsx:1021-1054`). Exact tuple lookup prevents cross-window/profile session collisions (`src/renderer/src/screens/Layout/chatRuns.ts:161-172`; `tests/chat-runs.test.ts:38-93`). Today's focused four-suite run passed 31/31 tests.
- Inference: The report's proposed `sessionStorage` explanation was incorrect, as its collaborator noted; the actual transport/state defects were fixed two days later and the current multi-run design is stronger than the original patch. No native macOS focus-switch smoke test was run today, so an unrelated modern tab-lifecycle issue cannot be ruled out, but the reported mechanism no longer exists.
- Recommended action: Smoke-test v0.7.7 by sending multiple turns in two open tabs, switching to another macOS window and back, and confirming each tab retains its session ID/history. Suggested close note: "The two causes identified here were fixed by #291 (`612f874e`) and #293 (`8795d9ae`) and shipped in v0.5.0: the renderer keeps each run's session ID aligned, and API sends resume through `X-Hermes-Session-Id`. Current tabs keep independent run/session identities and focused regressions pass. Closing as apparently fixed; please reopen with a current version and exact tab/window sequence if continuity still breaks."

## 2026-10-26 — #373 Failed to parse JSON from 'hermes kanban': Unexpected end of JSON input

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/373
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The reporter's follow-up attributes the parse error to a malformed SQLite Kanban DB and documents 453 backup files created by retries. Desktop Local/SSH Kanban still calls the Agent CLI (`lat.md/kanban.md:1-6`), but it checks the subprocess error first and only parses stdout as JSON after a zero exit (`src/main/kanban.ts:109-161`); therefore an Agent that returns nonzero on corruption surfaces stderr instead of the generic JSON parser error. The current locally installed Agent now integrity-checks before task command dispatch and catches initialization failure as actionable stderr plus exit code 1 (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/kanban.py:1002-1020`), raises a specific `KanbanDbCorruptError` preserving the original and backup path (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/kanban_db.py:1649-1665`), content-addresses identical corrupt backups and caps mutating-corruption backups at 10 (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/kanban_db.py:1333-1339,1668-1770`). Upstream regressions cover refusal, deterministic backup reuse, and retention (`/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/hermes_cli/test_kanban_db.py:4306-4369`; `/Users/fathah/.hermes/hermes-agent/tests/hermes_cli/test_kanban_db_repair.py:204-277`). A direct runtime probe today opened the same corrupt bytes three times and produced three actionable corruption errors but only one backup. The Agent checkout's pytest dependency is unavailable, so those upstream pytest files were inspected but not executed.
- Inference: The concrete failure chain in the issue—corruption yielding successful/non-JSON output and unbounded duplicate backups—has been replaced by a nonzero actionable error and bounded, deduplicated quarantine. Desktop's generic parse-error branch still exists for any different CLI bug that exits zero with invalid JSON, so this is not proof that every malformed-output case is impossible.
- Recommended action: On a disposable current install, corrupt a copied Kanban DB, open the board repeatedly, and confirm the UI shows the Agent's preservation/repair message and backup count stays bounded; close if it does. Suggested close note: "Current Hermes Agent fails closed on a corrupt Kanban DB with a nonzero actionable error, preserves the original, reuses a content-addressed backup for identical bytes, and caps changing-corruption backups. Desktop handles that nonzero error before JSON parsing. Closing as apparently fixed; please reopen with current Agent/Desktop versions and the preserved DB path if the generic JSON parse error still appears."

## 2026-10-27 — #551 Tool actions panel is not resizable - fixed height limits debugging

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/551
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: Current chat rendering folds contiguous calls/results into `ToolActivityGroup`; the group and each item only keep an `open` boolean and expose click-to-collapse controls, with no resize/maximize state or control (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/HistoryRow.tsx:181-240,244-324`; `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/MessageList.tsx:343-377`). A result body receives `chat-history-pre--scroll` (`src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/HistoryRow.tsx:230-236`), whose unchanged rule sets `max-height: 600px; overflow-y: auto` (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:7271-7279`; introduced by `1cadb02d`). The later grouped-tool redesign only animates collapse and hides overflow; it adds neither `resize` nor an expansion mode (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:7447-7467,7565-7568`; `dfb5c11a`). Existing tests cover grouping/windowing/title counts, not output resizing or expansion. Today's focused run passed 126/126 tests, confirming the current component path but not supplying the requested affordance.
- Inference: Any individual tool result taller than 600px deterministically remains internally scrollable and cannot be enlarged, matching the report. Multiple grouped results can make the overall group taller, but that does not let the user expand one clipped result or maximize it. The root cause is a hard CSS cap combined with a component state model that only distinguishes collapsed/open.
- Recommended action: Add the smallest explicit expansion affordance rather than persisting arbitrary pixel dimensions: give each long result an “Expand output”/“Collapse output” button in `ToolActivityItem`, toggle a modifier that removes the 600px cap (or uses a viewport-bounded full-height overlay), and retain the existing capped default for transcript performance. Expected files: `src/renderer/src/screens/Chat/HistoryRow.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css`, and a focused component test. Regression check: render a result over 600px, assert the default body scrolls at the cap, activate Expand, assert the full/viewport-expanded content is reachable without the inner 600px cap, then collapse and verify the original bounded layout and keyboard/ARIA state return.

## 2026-10-28 — #580 Theme: hardcoded colors don't adapt on system light/dark mode switch

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/580
- Classification: **LIKELY**
- Observed evidence: The broad claim is only partly true in current source. `ThemeProvider` follows OS changes in System mode and updates the root `data-theme` attribute, so previously mounted ordinary bubbles inherit new variables (`src/renderer/src/components/ThemeProvider.tsx:36-43,71-103`); user/agent bubble backgrounds and text use theme tokens (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:9-75,5170-5229`). However, fenced source code still lazy-loads only Prism One Dark, stores it globally, never consumes theme context, and passes its fixed inline style to every highlighted block (`src/renderer/src/components/AgentMarkdown.tsx:16-19,58-68,105-167`). Diff additions/removals also retain fixed dark-palette foregrounds (`src/renderer/src/assets/main.css:5447-5469`). Existing Markdown tests cover syntax, diagrams, and diff structure but no live dark/light switch (`src/renderer/src/components/AgentMarkdown.test.tsx:238-317`). Today's focused run passed 84/84 tests, which does not exercise palette adaptation.
- Inference: Plain historical chat text should adapt correctly now, so the report's “all messages” wording is not confirmed from source. Highlighted code and diffs are still plausibly unreadable after switching to a light theme because their inline/fixed dark-theme palette bypasses the newly resolved theme, and context-free memoized Markdown has no reason to select a different Prism palette. A rendered contrast check is needed to quantify severity.
- Recommended action: The smallest safe fix is to make `CodeBlock` consume the resolved theme appearance, lazy-load both One Dark and One Light, and select the palette on every theme change; replace diff foregrounds with light/dark theme tokens. Expected files are `src/renderer/src/components/AgentMarkdown.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css`, and `src/renderer/src/components/AgentMarkdown.test.tsx` (plus lat.md documentation for the new behavior). Focused regression: render an already-mounted fenced block and diff under System/light, fire a mocked OS change to dark and back, and assert the selected Prism palette and computed diff/text contrast update without remounting or losing collapsed state.

## 2026-10-31 — #164 Crash with Wayland on Alpine Linux

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/164
- Classification: **NEEDS INFO**
- Observed evidence: The v0.4.0 report includes Chromium fatal assertions in `sandbox/linux/services/credentials.cc` and `zygote_host_impl_linux.cc`; one commenter confirms that launching with both `--ozone-platform=x11` and `--no-sandbox` avoided the crash. The report does not isolate which switch mattered. Current source has upgraded Electron from v0.4.0's `39.2.6` to `44.1.1` (`package.json:62-79`), applies no automatic Ozone or no-sandbox switch (`src/main/index.ts:1-22`), and intentionally retains renderer sandboxing (`src/main/app/start.ts:169-206`). Current Linux targets are AppImage, Snap, DEB, and RPM, not Alpine APK (`electron-builder.yml:58-81`). Later packaging work added Chromium sandbox/AppArmor setup for DEB/RPM (`48f810f5c6c6d95ef092a58bf7b6c61efe7d35b2`, replaced by the complete default-hook restoration in `171c0d9042a0c537a76a8ee583b71ff28157d36c`), but that does not configure an Alpine source launch. Today's non-Linux focused regressions passed 79/79; the generated Linux hook suite passed 6/8 on isolated rerun, with two fixtures timing out at their five-second shell deadline rather than producing an Alpine launch result.
- Inference: The captured failure is a Chromium sandbox-initialization failure, not evidence that native Wayland rendering itself crashed; applying X11 and disabling the sandbox together obscures the cause. The Electron upgrade and packaged-Linux sandbox changes may affect a current reproduction, but neither proves Alpine is fixed, and disabling the sandbox permanently would be an unsafe workaround.
- Recommended action: Ask for a current v0.7.7 reproduction with Alpine version/kernel, glibc-compat layer, `XDG_SESSION_TYPE`, user-namespace availability, `chrome-sandbox` owner/mode, and separate launch results for default flags versus `--ozone-platform=x11` while keeping the sandbox enabled. If current Electron still fails, first make the launcher report the missing sandbox prerequisite clearly; only add Alpine packaging/support if CI can install a valid SUID sandbox or verify usable unprivileged user namespaces. Focused regression: launch a packaged build in an Alpine VM with Wayland and XWayland, assert the default sandbox remains enabled, the supported path opens a window, and a deliberately invalid sandbox setup fails with an actionable diagnostic rather than suggesting `--no-sandbox`.

A native SQLite call can still occupy the main process until SQLite returns, but ordinary lock/open/query failures now terminate with cached data rather than an endless loading state. Focused Sessions/cache regressions pass. Closing as fixed; please reopen with a current-version log if a locked database can still leave the spinner indefinitely."

## 2026-11-07 — #1064 Browser signin for remote gateway does not work

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1064
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The Hermes 0.23 / Desktop 0.7.7 report says browser authentication succeeds and the authenticated desktop appears inside the modal, but the modal never completes and quitting cancels the flow. `openRemoteOAuthLogin` deliberately creates a parent-blocking modal and can complete only after `remoteOAuthSessionState` recognizes a cookie; otherwise closing it rejects as cancellation (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:378-452`). The current recognizer strips `__Host-`/`__Secure-`, then accepts only the exact base names or names that *end* in `_hermes_session_at` / `_hermes_session_rt` (`src/main/remote-oauth.ts:13-22,93-117`). Current Hermes Agent webapp auth names gateway-specific cookies in the opposite direction—`hermes_session_at_<gateway>` and `hermes_session_rt_<gateway>`—as documented and implemented at upstream commit `981791d9d41fd2efde27f563873ce8c48ef1dac6` in `hermes_cli/dashboard_auth/cookies.py`. Those cookies are valid and let the embedded dashboard render, but Desktop's predicate returns false, so the 750 ms poll never destroys the modal and the IPC transaction never reaches the config commit (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1747-1771`). Existing OAuth tests passed within today's 56/56 focused tests, but cover bare, security-prefixed, and pre-base tenant-prefixed names only (`tests/remote-oauth.test.ts:260-288`), not the current post-base gateway suffix.
- Inference: The cookie-direction mismatch directly explains every reported symptom and remains in current source. The reporter did not provide the concrete cookie name or deployment gateway digest, but the authenticated dashboard rendering proves that the browser session completed while Desktop's bounded recognition did not.
- Recommended action: In `src/main/remote-oauth.ts`, accept a delimiter-bounded suffix after either recognized base cookie name (including `__Host-`/`__Secure-` variants) while retaining the existing exact and legacy pre-base forms; update `tests/remote-oauth.test.ts` and `lat.md/remote-dashboard-oauth.md`. Focused regression: make the fake OAuth partition return `__Host-hermes_session_at_<gateway>`, trigger navigation/polling, and assert that the modal is destroyed, the promise resolves, and `remoteAuthMode: "oauth"` is committed. Do not treat arbitrary names merely containing the substring as authenticated.

## 2026-11-07 — #1063 crashes it wont show the ui

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1063
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The Fedora launch log is deterministic: after GPU and sandbox fallback, Chromium cannot create or access `/dev/shm/.org.chromium.*`, reports `/dev/shm` unusable, raises a fatal assertion, and the renderer exits before UI appears. Current pre-ready startup applies GPU preferences and the GPU crash guard, but never checks shared-memory availability or appends Chromium's `disable-dev-shm-usage` fallback (`src/main/index.ts:1-22`; `src/main/gpu-fallback.ts:175-208`). The main window only logs `render-process-gone` after creation (`src/main/app/start.ts:169-224`), which cannot recover this repeated renderer-start failure. Repository search and history contain no `/dev/shm` handling or shared-memory switch. Today's GPU and surrounding startup tests passed within 56/56 focused tests, but they cover GPU-process recovery rather than renderer shared-memory initialization (`tests/gpu-fallback.test.ts:80-175`).
- Inference: The immediate host defect is a missing, unmounted, or incorrectly permissioned `/dev/shm`; the launcher's separate GPU/sandbox fallbacks do not repair it. Because current Desktop has no preflight or Chromium fallback, the same environment still produces the reported no-UI crash. The log is sufficient to confirm the path without Fedora hardware access.
- Recommended action: First advise the reporter to restore a mounted `/dev/shm` with mode `1777`. For a resilient product fix, add a Linux-only pre-ready helper that checks whether `/dev/shm` exists and is writable/executable; only when unusable, append `disable-dev-shm-usage` so Chromium uses disk-backed temporary storage, with a clear warning. Keep this separate from GPU and sandbox fallback. Affected files: `src/main/index.ts`, a small main-process runtime/preflight module, its unit test, and `lat.md/main-process.md`. Focused regression: mock Linux with missing/inaccessible `/dev/shm` and assert the switch is appended exactly once; assert a healthy `1777` mount leaves Chromium defaults unchanged, then smoke-test a packaged build in a namespace without `/dev/shm`.

## 2026-11-08 — #1066 Portable setup issue - windows 11

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1066
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The Windows 11 report shows PowerShell failing while parsing the staged wrapper after the non-ASCII username in `C:\Users\Michał\...` was corrupted, before the downloaded installer could run. Current code safely quotes apostrophes in embedded paths (`src/main/installer-windows-download.ts:8-19`) and, after hashing, rewrites the downloaded upstream installer with a UTF-8 BOM for Windows PowerShell 5.1 (`src/main/installer-windows-download.ts:23-31`). However, the outer wrapper—which contains `$hermesHome` and `$installDir`—is itself staged as BOM-less UTF-8 (`src/main/installer.ts:1090-1129`) and executed through `powershell.exe -File` (`src/main/installer.ts:1135-1148`). Thus the compatibility conversion happens too late to protect the script PowerShell must parse first. The existing regression verifies quote escaping and hash-before-conversion ordering but not wrapper-file bytes or a non-ASCII Windows profile path (`tests/installer-windows-download.test.ts:11-33`). Today's focused installer/session/locale run passed 69/69 tests, confirming the current test gap rather than disproving the reported failure.
- Inference: Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less `-File` script through the legacy system code page, so UTF-8 bytes from a username containing diacritics can be misdecoded into parser-significant characters. That directly accounts for the reported corrupted path and `Unexpected token` error. PowerShell 7 may not reproduce it, but current code explicitly falls back to Windows PowerShell, so the defect remains reachable.
- Recommended action: Stage the outer wrapper with a UTF-8 BOM (for example, prepend `\uFEFF` before `writeFileSync`) while preserving the existing downloaded-installer hash boundary. Affected files: `src/main/installer.ts`, a focused installer test such as `tests/installer-windows-download.test.ts`, and `lat.md/desktop-security.md` when implemented. Regression: simulate Windows with `LOCALAPPDATA=C:\Users\Michał\AppData\Local`, capture the wrapper bytes, assert they begin `EF BB BF` and round-trip the Unicode paths, and retain the assertion that the downloaded installer is hashed before its separate BOM conversion.

- Inference: Interrupting an in-place Agent update/build can plausibly leave a partial tree or a still-running/locked build that explains both the failed relaunch and subsequent rebuild stall. The current Desktop still lacks an atomic update/rollback or explicit repair workflow, so the underlying lifecycle gap remains real. ## 2026-11-10 — #488 Subject: Technical Issue Report: Gateway Connectivity and AI Response Failure

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/488
- Classification: **APPEARS FIXED**
- Observed evidence: The report says Start left Messaging Gateway at “Stopped” and chat then waited indefinitely; three comments confirm the symptom. The maintainer linked the two concrete repairs to PRs #484 and #499. PR #484 was closed only because its Windows/Electron startup repair was already present on main: current `startGatewayDetailed` opens `gateway-stderr.log` synchronously and gives `spawn` a numeric descriptor, avoiding the `createWriteStream` `fd: null` race (`src/main/hermes.ts:3558-3639`). PR #499 merged as `4cdc79a4ea90ed9c7171b55482bc70528fb337e2` and shipped in v0.5.8. Current chat dispatch recovers and retries once when a local API transport fails before output, but never replays after partial output (`src/main/hermes.ts:3145-3289`); native recovery stops the stale gateway, starts it, waits for health, and serializes per-profile restarts (`src/main/hermes.ts:3989-4125`). Focused startup/recovery tests include stopped-gateway, stale-ready-cache, reset-socket, partial-output, and native-restart paths (`tests/hermes-cli-session-id.test.ts:432-672`; `tests/gateway-restart.test.ts:287-484`). Today's focused run passed 178/178 tests across eight files.
- Inference: The two source defects explicitly associated with this issue—Electron rejecting an unresolved stderr stream and chat trusting a stale/unhealthy local gateway—are both absent from current source and covered by regressions. No packaged Windows gateway/chat smoke test was performed today, so an unrelated current startup failure would need fresh logs.
- Recommended action: Close as fixed after one current Windows smoke test that starts a stopped gateway and sends a chat message. Suggested close note: "The Windows gateway-start race is fixed by opening the stderr log as a real file descriptor before spawn, and PR #499 (`4cdc79a4`, shipped in v0.5.8) added health-checked stale-gateway recovery so chat no longer waits indefinitely. Focused startup/recovery regressions pass. Closing as fixed; please reopen with current Desktop/Agent versions and `gateway-stderr.log` if Start still leaves the gateway stopped."

## 2026-11-10 — #481 Unable to connect to remote Hermes on Mac

Its screenshot shows the old Remote Hermes connection screen rejecting `http://192.168.1.76:8642` with an API key, while the body says only that the server answered `curl`; it does not include Desktop/Agent versions, the curl command, response status/body, or remote logs. The URL itself has no trailing `/v1`, so the known doubled-path defect does not explain this case. Current Welcome delegates connection to the main process (`src/renderer/src/screens/Welcome/Welcome.tsx:52-76`), which probes the remote auth mode before saving the connection (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:1689-1719`). Current validation rejects SPA HTML, tries both gateway `/health` and dashboard `/api/status`, and validates a supplied gateway token on protected `/v1/models` rather than accepting only a public health response (`src/main/hermes.ts:3845-3915`). Regressions cover new dashboard URLs, valid/invalid gateway tokens, trailing `/v1`, and HTML responses (`tests/remote-mode-url-and-spawn.test.ts:716-856`). Commit `c547301c37b84062308078e2e9de6e6ebff8df5a` later hardened keyless gateways, candidate probing, and sign-in HTML. Today's focused run passed 178/178 tests across eight files.
- Inference: “Curl works” may mean only the public `/health` endpoint succeeded while the submitted bearer key failed on a protected route, or it may describe an old probing defect now changed. The sparse report cannot distinguish invalid/mismatched credentials, a version-skewed Agent, network reachability from the app, or a historical Desktop bug, so neither confirmation nor closure is defensible.
- Recommended action: Ask for exact current Desktop and Agent versions; the full redacted commands/status/content types for `curl http://HOST:8642/health` and `curl -H 'Authorization: Bearer …' http://HOST:8642/v1/models`; whether the Agent requires `API_SERVER_KEY`; and the remote gateway log for the connection attempt. Retest with the current connection flow before closing.

The report gives no Desktop/Agent version, provider endpoint, credential source, gateway log, or HTTP response body. Current provider routing maps `GLM_API_KEY` to native provider `zai` (`src/renderer/src/constants.ts:598-608`) and that provider to `https://api.z.ai/api/paas/v4` (`src/main/provider-registry.ts:38-47`). Gateway startup forwards every nonempty profile environment entry and then fills missing provider-enumerated secrets (`src/main/hermes.ts:3478-3512`). Tests pin both GLM install-key and native-provider routing (`tests/install-gate-providers.test.ts:34-46`; `tests/constants.test.ts:258-274`). Later commit `d465836b4740203bc10420d415e6ad1b7a4f7e21` broadened security-provider credential forwarding, while `59ab27b86feb76e9acb8a772138b6b1d6c7db06a` fixed a separate unauthenticated session-continuation rejection; neither commit proves the screenshot's provider-originated “Invalid API key” cause. Today's focused run passed 178/178 tests across eight files.
- Inference: Because the same provider key completed the first turn, the later failure could involve credential expiry/rotation, a tool or auxiliary model using a different provider, profile/env divergence after gateway restart, or an upstream Z.ai response. Current static routing looks coherent, but no evidence identifies which credential or endpoint produced the error, so the issue cannot responsibly be marked fixed or reproduced.
- Recommended action: Ask for exact Desktop/Agent versions, the redacted active model/provider/base URL, whether the key is stored as `GLM_API_KEY`, `ZAI_API_KEY`, or another alias, gateway logs covering both turns, and whether a second plain text-only prompt fails before any tool call. Focused reproduction: send two turns through the same GLM session—one plain follow-up and one tool-using request—while recording the outbound provider endpoint and credential variable name (never the key value).

## 2026-11-11 — #1069 [Bug] Computer Use approvals fail on Windows 11: Desktop client does not support server-initiated requests (Agent 0.23.0)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1069
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The report gives a deterministic Windows 11 reproduction on current Desktop commit `171024862c849475634d217a5635fedff796360c` and Agent 0.23.0; read-only Computer Use succeeds, CLI approval succeeds, but Desktop logs that the attached client predates server→client requests. Current `TuiGatewayClient` opens the WebSocket without sending `client.capabilities {server_requests: true}` (`src/main/hermes.ts:650-781`). Its frame decoder treats every frame with an `id` as a response to a Desktop-originated request and returns immediately when that id is unknown, so a server request carrying both `id` and `method` is discarded (`src/main/hermes.ts:784-815`). The approval path still expects the pre-0.23 `event: approval.request` notification and answers with the paired `approval.respond` RPC (`src/main/hermes.ts:2361-2425`). PR #904 / `b2ae5bd836161ea96aa1893b170712ead1470348` implemented that old contract and explicitly validated against Agent `503d863f`; upstream Agent commits `ebe8cda8eac5` and `9f7f2f28c017` replaced the `*.request`/`*.respond` pair with true JSON-RPC server requests on 2026-09-14, and `f9d178f78ed8` made `client.capabilities` mandatory. Agent's current protocol documentation requires the advertisement after `gateway.ready` and a response frame with the same request id. Existing transport tests inject the old event shape and assert an `approval.respond` call (`tests/chat-approval-transports.test.ts:206-285`), so today's 72-passed/6-skipped focused run does not cover Agent 0.23's wire contract.
- Inference: This is a protocol-version incompatibility, not a Windows-specific Computer Use or renderer-card defect. Agent 0.23 refuses to emit the approval because Desktop never advertises support; even if it did emit one, Desktop's current decoder would drop the request frame. The exact log and both sides of the wire contract establish the failure without needing local Windows hardware.
- Recommended action: Add inbound JSON-RPC request handling to `TuiGatewayClient`, advertise `server_requests: true` once after `gateway.ready` (tolerating `-32601` from older Agents), translate the `approval` request into the existing run-scoped approval UI, and answer with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":"srq-…","result":{"choice":"once"}}`; unsupported methods must return `-32601`, and `request.cancel` must invalidate only its matching card. Affected files: `src/main/hermes.ts`, `tests/chat-approval-transports.test.ts`, shared request typing as needed, and `lat.md/chat-commands.md`. Focused regression: mock Agent 0.23 sending `gateway.ready` then an `id`+`method:"approval"` frame, assert the capability advertisement, one visible card, and an exact same-id response frame with no legacy `approval.respond` call; also assert an older backend's `-32601` capability response leaves chat usable.

## 2026-11-11 — #1070 Broken download link for Linux AppImage on the website [https://hermesone.org/] (404 Error)

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1070
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments. The live `https://hermesone.org/download` payload still configures Linux to download `hermes-desktop-0.7.7.AppImage`, while the v0.7.7 GitHub release contains `hermes-desktop-0.7.7-x86_64.AppImage` and `hermes-desktop-0.7.7-arm64.AppImage`, not the unqualified name. This repository intentionally includes `${arch}` in every AppImage filename (`electron-builder.yml:78-79`), and the release regression pins that contract (`tests/release-artifacts.test.ts:7-14`). The focused release test passes, confirming that packaging is correct and the stale external website URL is the defect. Repository search found no Hermes One website source here, only links to the deployed site.
- Inference: The website hard-coded a pre-architecture filename instead of consuming the release's actual asset name. Changing the Desktop packaging back to an unqualified filename would reintroduce x64/arm64 ambiguity and is not the safe repair; the website must track the published asset contract.
- Recommended action: In the separate Hermes One website source, change the x64 Linux option to `https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/releases/download/v0.7.7/hermes-desktop-0.7.7-x86_64.AppImage`. Prefer generating download options from GitHub release assets or a checked manifest rather than hard-coding version/name pairs. Focused regression: for every configured website download option, assert that the basename exists in the target release's asset list (or that the URL resolves successfully), covering both x86_64 and arm64 AppImages. No local Desktop source file should change.

## 2026-11-11 — #1071 i18n: Discover page has no Chinese (zh) translations — full zh string set included

- URL: https://github.com/fathah/hermes-desktop/issues/1071
- Classification: **CONFIRMED**
- Observed evidence: The issue has no comments and accurately describes current source. Discover renders all visible labels through `discover.*` keys (`src/renderer/src/screens/Discover/Discover.tsx:398-662`). Shared resources import and register Discover only for English, Hebrew, Turkish, and Arabic (`src/shared/i18n/index.ts:18-41,231-254,283-315,538-569`); the `zh-CN` block contains no `discover` namespace (`src/shared/i18n/index.ts:413-437`), and there is no `src/shared/i18n/locales/zh-CN/discover.ts`. Both i18next and the synchronous helper fall back to English when a locale key is absent (`src/shared/i18n/index.ts:603-617,630-643`). Git history shows Discover was added by `4db57c7df` on 2026-06-02 without a Chinese namespace. Today's focused i18n/Discover tests pass because no test asserts namespace/key parity for this screen.
- Inference: Selecting Simplified Chinese deterministically falls back to the English Discover dictionary; this is not a cache, language-persistence, or macOS-only problem. Traditional Chinese and most other locales also lack the namespace, but #1071 specifically supplies and requests `zh-CN` strings.
- Recommended action: Add `src/shared/i18n/locales/zh-CN/discover.ts` with the supplied translations and unchanged `{{kind}}`, `{{author}}`, and `{{name}}` placeholders; import and register it in `src/shared/i18n/index.ts`. Add coverage in `src/shared/i18n/index.test.ts` or a Discover locale test. Focused regression: render Discover with `zh-CN`, assert Chinese title/subtitle/search/action labels and interpolation, and assert those keys do not equal the English fallback. Review `zh-TW` separately rather than silently reusing Simplified Chinese.
